- F134 — The gate scripts (DQ-10, DQ-11, DQ-14; CF-068, CF-069). `io_budget --assert` accepted `inf`, `~0` and `10**100` and skipped `__init__.py`: each module's evaluated budget must be an int (or map of ints) from 0 to 1024, packages included. `check_tested` cleared a definition by any same-spelt name, walked only `tree.body` and took any `.get(...)` for a route: references resolve per module through imports, `if`/`try` bodies and lambdas count, routes are handlers on the module's own router; the seven definitions it had cleared by collision now have tests. `_ready`'s readiness ids leaked `BOUNDARY_TEXT_TOO_LONG`; they are retyped.
- F135 — Corrections to F108 (DQ-5, DQ-11, DQ-14): FP-07's bundle half was neither claimed nor deferred and is F132's; FP-18 was cited but `check_tested.py` was unchanged in round 3, and FP-19 was only the `float("inf")` spelling (both F134); "every optional field in the set digest is tagged (N8 closed)" was false, only `expects_blocked` and `research_brief` are, and N8 stays open because tagging moves every committed set's digest; the loader's readiness code is correct only now; "verdicts bind their evidence" gains its backdating half in F131; FP-17's "reads a string narrative as carrying none" is reversed, the verifier refuses one.
- F136 — E9 and E6 (DF-2/MAX-08, MAX-17). E9 counted a stream live on one blocked read, so a proxy that held after the first frame, or cut at one second, or buffered two frames and closed, passed: frames must keep arriving for 3 s with no gap over 1.5 s and no close. A truncated body raised `IncompleteRead` past every catch: E6 and E9 record the class as their row.
- F137 — Preflight (DF-3, MAX-07). Payload logging configured through `telemetry_config`, and a `pending_config` turning capture on or adding an entity, passed E1: both are refused, read through the SDK's own dataclasses. The documented standalone command failed on import; preflight puts the repository root on its path.
- F138 — What ships (DF-4, DQ-9). `--shipped` checked a 17-path sample and CI deployed an empty page: the shipped set is every tracked file under the sync roots and every file of the built export, E3 holds the CLI's own record to it, E2 is `validate -o json` and refuses a resolved endpoint, price or ceiling other than the one given, CI builds the export and checks the resolved price, and boot refuses an `index.html` naming a file that did not ship. `sync.paths` is an allowlist, so any `exclude:` key, in any spelling, and a target-level `sync:` are refused.
- F139 — App names (DF-5). Every target but dev deployed `caos`, so a new target took over production and two developers collided: the app is `caos-${bundle.target}`, prod overrides to `caos`, dev is `caos-dev-${workspace.current_user.id}`, and E5 reads the name the CLI resolved at E2.
- F140 — Gate integrity (DF-11, MAX-13; CF-066, CF-067). "Suppressions may only fall" ran only on pull requests and the baseline had slack: `tests/gate_baseline.json` equals the measured counts (a count below it also fails), and CI runs `--against` on every push. `check_gate_config` now refuses the effective weakenings it used to pass (tool-config keys outside an allowlist, `addopts` outside its tokens, coverage tables, tracked override files, whole-file suppressions, a CLAUDE.md gate missing verbatim from CI, `|| true`, `continue-on-error`), and budgets the complexity snapshot's total; `qualify._capture` left the snapshot, so 25 legacy functions remain. The rest is N46.
- F141 — The stand-in and the runbook (DF-12, DF-13, MAX-22, MAX-N02). The stub now refuses what the platform refuses (an `overwrite=false` import over an existing file, an invalid app name), records a deployment's command and environment and boots from them, and can report a crashed app; the runbook names the stale-lock recovery. Each stand-in run clears the bundle state an earlier one left (the CLI panicked on it) and refuses a real workspace's; the runbook records `bundle deployment unbind`. The runbook names `GRANT USAGE, CREATE ON SCHEMA public` and a test boots on exactly those grants; the manual commands carry their variables.
- F142 — Announcements and focus (DF-6, DF-7). Six commands succeeded silently and an equal sentence was not re-announced: every outcome carries a sentence, and each is its own keyed node. A confirmed act that removed its own control, and a link from the unavailable page, dropped focus to `<body>`: focus moves to the nearest heading still on the page.
- F143 — The event tail (DF-8, MAX-14). `Retry-After: 0` reopened a refused tail with no wait (a unit test pinned it): the wait is at least the doubling back-off. A connection the browser was re-establishing left the view marked live: every error marks it not live until the next open.
- F144 — Target size (DF-9). Sign, freeze, file and the confirm step's buttons were 19 px targets, and no fixture offered them: every control in a confirm step is 24 px, and the accessibility matrix gains fixtures offering every act and two routes with an armed step (312 entries, clean).
- F145 — Commands that must not rotate or stall (MAX-01, MAX-18, MAX-19). A transient refusal after a possibly committed write drew a fresh idempotency key: `STORE_UNAVAILABLE` and the other transient codes keep it. Start and Retry sent a fingerprint held only from this session's pin or approval, so a reloaded, approved run could not start: a successful preview also supplies it, and the server still compares at commit. A view reached by case alone tailed only case events: the tail follows the run the view displays.
- F146 — What round 4 left open, each with its reason: ST-10 (an exhausted rate limit keeps its reservation; releasing it contradicts "nothing is released", N33), MAX-09 (Autoscaling; the bundle cannot choose the resource type from a variable and the stand-in cannot show what the platform injects, N45), DF-1's fleet half (a stopped standalone worker's beat stays fresh by design, N37), and the partial fixes named above (N34–N49). The reviewers ran at effort max; the six patchers ran at xhigh, the session's effort when they launched.
- F147 — The suite and CI after round 4. Two tests read server-wide Postgres state: the set-up lock test counted every database's advisory locks, and the dropped-session test found its worker by a fixed `application_name` on a database with no schema, where the worker faults, closes its session and backs off, so a session to end existed only by chance. The first counts its own database's locks; the second migrates its database, names each run's worker uniquely and scopes the lookup to its database. CI's test job hung at its 60-minute limit on 653dc9c and c37733e printing dots only: the step runs with `--max-worker-restart=0` (a dead worker fails the session instead of waiting on a replacement) and `-o faulthandler_timeout=600 -ra --durations=25` (a stuck test prints its stack), pinned in `CI_GATES`. `tests/test_health.py::test_each_failed_probe_names_its_code_and_answers_503` fails when the suite runs as root, where a 0o500 directory is writable; CI runs as a user.

### Owner decisions, 2026-09-23 (the final-sweep contract's OD-1 and OD-2, approved by the owner)

- D29 (2026-09-23) — N31 closed: `MAX_REQUEST_BYTES` is 4 MiB (`caos/provider.py`), not the legacy 1 MiB, and the bundle's default `run_ceiling` is 100.00. Alternatives: keep 1 MiB and record (FULL routes refuse `REQUEST_OVER_CEILING` at CP-5 by construction with real handoffs); raise the per-section bound instead (the request still overflows). Reason: the catalog's widest node carries about 1 MiB of authority and upstream sections at their bound, and Claude on the gateway takes a 1M-token context, while the one-token-per-byte reservation still over-counts. The pricing goldens stay legacy-derived (F57): `tests/parity/generate_goldens.py --package server --root <legacy b5a3932> --group pricing --request-ceiling 4194304` under the legacy 3.14 environment, manifest `package: server`; the legacy ceiling is kept as two ordinary sizes (`request_1048576`, `request_1048577`), and `worst_case` at the default Claude price is 22.609920 (was 6.881280). The default ceiling is derived, not chosen: the widest execution profile at its section bounds (FULL_CREDIT_32, 22 nodes, 75.398490 at the default price, evidence excluded) plus one worst-case call (the gate's evidence, or one D30 second attempt), 98.008410, rounded up; 25.00 could not finish even a LITE_CREDIT_22 route (31.59) because every billed attempt keeps its reservation (CF-008, legacy `remaining` semantics, unchanged). `tests/test_run_ceiling.py::test_the_default_run_ceiling_finishes_the_widest_profile` recomputes it from the bundle and holds `databricks.yml`, `scripts/enterprise_deploy.sh` and `scripts/enterprise_deploy.py` to one value. Side effects, all derived from the constant: the gate may show a source whole up to 1.5 MiB of text (`GATE_SOURCE_BYTES`), so both committed 10-Ks (Boeing, Ford) are now shown whole and fit a consumer whole; the page path (§98) is still proven with them at the legacy ceiling (`tests/test_large_documents.py::legacy_ceiling`); two register documents over 1 MiB now fit (`qualification/DOCUMENTS.md`, regenerated). Tests that priced a run at TERRA-like rates under the legacy $5 default now start under a ceiling that covers one worst-case call, since a run below that is refused at admission (`runtime._affordable`). Evidence: `uv run pytest tests/parity/test_pricing.py tests/parity/test_generate_goldens.py tests/test_run_ceiling.py` green; the test above failed at 25.00 (`25.00 >= 75.398490 + 22.609920`) before the default moved.
- D30 (2026-09-23) — N32 closed on the host side: a node whose answer is refused `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` gets exactly one second attempt, in the same pass, reserved and priced like any other (invariant 8), carrying what the checks reported on the refused answer. Alternatives: record only (no real CP-0 passes); a host final-check block naming the vendor's severity rule (restates a vendor rule and breaks prompt parity); a vendor `SKILL.md` step (edits vendor bytes, invariant 4). What the second attempt carries, in the new host block `icm/shared/prompt/validator_feedback.md` (pinned in `icm/HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json`, F67; declared conditional in every stage contract): the vendor's own `validate_handoff` messages on the stored answer, as written, and the host's verbatim-quote check as a count -- no host text restates a vendor rule. Each line is cut to 512 characters and crosses `BoundaryText`, at most 16; a vendor message may quote the model's own line back to it, and these lines go into that one request only, never a log, refusal or row. The block renders last and is folded into the section tag, so a first attempt's bytes are unchanged (prompt parity) and the refused answer could not know the markers around it. The ledger decides (`canonical.second_attempt_due`, `store.outcomes.node_attempts`): the second attempt is due when the node's latest attempt is its only `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` refusal, so a crash between the refusal and the second attempt changes nothing, a second refusal stops the run as before, and an operator's later retry is an ordinary attempt. It adds one reader of a refused attempt's stored body beside the replay path, and hands back only the checks' lines. A qualification case that declares `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` is now met only when both attempts are refused. Measured live the same day (`qualification/PROVIDER_RUNBOOK.md`, $1.13 of an $8.00 authorisation, Gemini 2.5 Flash and Claude Haiku 4.5): every refused CP-0 got its one second attempt and no more, and the fed-back check was acted on (Haiku met the severity rule it had broken, and in another run the heading order), but each second answer broke or kept another rule, so no CP-0 was accepted; N50 and N51 are the host-side follow-ups. Under D29 each run's ceiling must cover one worst-case call at the transport ceiling (22.61 at the Opus price), so a two-case Opus set needs `scripts/qualify.py --ceiling` of at least 45.22 as its authorisation although the calls themselves cost a few dollars; a model priced like Gemini 2.5 Flash needs a fraction of that. Evidence: `tests/test_second_attempt.py` (7 tests) green; disabling the retry fails three of them and stripping the carried lines fails two.

### CP-0 investigation, 2026-09-23 (every stored CP-0 answer replayed through the host's own verdict)

- F148 — The host refused quotes its body carried. `handoff._quoted` forgave quotation marks around a quote but not what prose puts beside them: a sentence's full stop or comma after the closing mark, or a citation's brackets, so `"Total debt at 31 December 2026".` read as a different last word. Replaying the host's full post-call verdict (`canonical._replayed_answer`, read-only) on the 21 stored CP-0 answers of 23 September, and classifying the 75 citations refused as not verbatim, found 12 carried word for word with only that around them: all nine of one Claude Opus 5.5 answer's (F111 read them as the model's miss; they were the host's) and the single miss that sank an otherwise vendor-clean Gemini 2.5 Flash answer, which now meets the next check instead. Action: an edge word may wear opening marks before the quote's first word and closing marks (quotation marks, `.,;:!?`, closing brackets) after its last, and nothing else; inner words match exactly and a letter, digit or dash touching the edge still makes a different word (`_wears`, `_carried`). Anchoring in the document (`verify_citations`) is unchanged. The other 63 misses were read as the models': paraphrased, truncated or absent quotes (F149 found eight of them the host's). Evidence: `tests/test_handoff_record.py::test_a_quote_the_body_ends_a_sentence_with_is_still_quoted` (8 cases, all failing before) and `test_a_quote_whose_edge_word_is_a_different_word_is_not_quoted`.
- F149 — The host refused quotes its body carried in Markdown's own spelling. A backslash before ASCII punctuation is CommonMark's escape for that mark, so `\"Total debt\"` reads `"Total debt"`; `handoff._body_words` compared the Markdown source, where the backslash made the edge word a different word. One Claude Sonnet 5 CP-0 answer of 23 September wrote all eight quotes it was refused for that way (F148 counted them among the models' misses). Action: body words are read with CommonMark's backslash escapes resolved (`_MARKDOWN_ESCAPE`); a backslash before a letter or digit is no escape and still makes a different word; a code span, where a backslash is literal, is read the same way (a known over-forgiveness, marked in the code). With it the Sonnet answer carries all ten quotes, clears the validator and the completeness check, and is refused at anchoring for one citation naming page 17 for a line its prompt shows under page 18: the model's. Evidence: `tests/test_handoff_record.py::test_a_quote_the_body_writes_with_markdown_escapes_is_still_quoted` (3 cases, all failing before) and the backslash-before-a-letter case of `test_a_quote_whose_edge_word_is_a_different_word_is_not_quoted`.
- F150 — Why CI hung after round 3 (runs on 653dc9c, c37733e and ab88a4b: the suite reached 95-97% in 13 minutes, one xdist worker went silent, and the job idled to its 60-minute limit). The fault dump round 4 added to the CI test step (`-o faulthandler_timeout=600`) placed the silent worker inside `Thread.start()` in the workspace stub's `serving`, waiting for a server thread that never ran. F102's child limiter, `_limit_address_space`, caps the extraction child's address space at its decode budget plus 512 MiB; `tests/test_pdf_extraction.py::_drive_child` runs `child_main()` in-process so a raised exception reaches the test, and so applied that cap to the test worker itself, 768 MiB against a worker already mapping more than a gigabyte. On Linux every later mapping in that worker fails: a new thread whose stack comes from glibc's cache starts, cannot allocate, and dies before it signals `_started`, so `Thread.start()` waits forever (reproduced in a Linux container: the same frame, `threading.py:982`, after one exited thread and a 513 MiB cap); a worker that had not run `_drive_child` finished its share, which is why the stall came late and named no test. macOS ignores `RLIMIT_AS`, which is why every local run passed. The driver now patches the limiter out, as `tests/test_admission_limits.py` already did for its own in-process call, and asserts the worker's `RLIMIT_AS` is unchanged afterwards, so the next in-process caller fails on any platform rather than hanging on one. `--max-worker-restart=0` and the fault dump stay: a dead worker now fails the job in minutes with its stack, not after an hour without one.
- D30, addendum (2026-09-23) — What the second attempt carries grows by two host-rule lines, closing N50 and N51 (first-attempt bytes unchanged): an answer that is not the transport gets `host transport check:` with the JSON parser's own fixed message (and, for an unescaped control character, that a newline or tab inside a JSON string is written `\n` or `\t`), where it carried nothing; and the quote line names which citations failed by their place in the list (at most 20, then a count), never their text, where it gave only a count. Evidence: `tests/test_second_attempt.py::test_retry_feedback_says_why_a_body_is_not_the_transport` and `test_retry_feedback_names_at_most_the_first_twenty_failed_citations`.
- D30, second addendum (2026-09-23) — The second attempt also carries the vendor completeness checker's messages (against the module's own `SKILL.md`) and, for CP-0, the T8 parser's, labelled `completeness_check:` and `navigation:` and bounded like the validator's: a second attempt told only the first check it failed trips over the next one. Found live: the one answer to clear transport, every quote and the validator on 23 September (GPT-5.6 luna, $0.03) was refused `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE` for a copied fixture marker and a second T8 table, neither of which the validator reports. Evidence: `tests/test_second_attempt.py::test_retry_feedback_carries_the_completeness_and_t8_checks_too`.
- D30, third addendum (2026-09-23; N52, on branch `d30/widen`, for the owner to merge or drop: it widens OD-1) — `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE` and anchoring's refusals earn the same one second attempt as `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` (`canonical.SECOND_ATTEMPT_CODES`), still one per node whichever code refused first, carrying the completeness checker's and T8 parser's messages the second addendum already computes. Why: the completeness contracts name columns the checker matches literally (CP-1's and CP-1B's `Period 1…N`, CP-1's `issuer-specific segment_id/name/type`) and disqualify the `[Insufficient Information]` the shared canon tells a model to write, so an answer refused incomplete and retried without being told which register failed repeats the same table. Measured live on 23 September: GPT-5.6 luna CP-0 refused incomplete (L1: fixture marker, second T8 table); GPT-6 Luna Pro CP-1 refused incomplete for the four `Period 1…N` tables and two issuer-specific columns (G1); luna CP-3D refused incomplete for placeholder critical cells (L2). Anchoring's refusals (`CITATION_NOT_LOCATED`, `CITATION_AMBIGUOUS`, `CITATION_NOT_DELIVERED`) earn it too, and every second attempt now carries one host-rule line naming which citations did not anchor and why, by number, never text (`handoff.anchoring_line`): each citation is judged alone by `verify_citations`, the rule the answer was refused by, over the blocks that node was given (`canonical._anchoring`); measured, L3's first CP-0 (page 11 cited with page 18's spacing), Sonnet 5's one wrong page and G1's first CP-0 all stopped there. A second attempt the ceiling cannot pay for now leaves the first refusal standing as the run's stop code (it was `BUDGET_CEILING_REACHED`, which hid what the answer was; `runtime._with_second_attempt`). Fifteen tests that pinned one attempt after an incomplete or unanchored answer now count the second attempt; the two crash-and-retry tests in `tests/test_runtime.py` that only used an unanchored quote as a billed refusal now use an answer about another issuer (`HANDOFF_IDENTITY_MISMATCH`, no second attempt), so they still watch replay and retry alone. Evidence: `tests/test_second_attempt.py::test_an_incomplete_answer_gets_the_second_attempt_too`, `test_an_unanchored_citation_gets_the_second_attempt_naming_it`, `test_the_anchoring_line_names_each_failed_citation_by_number_and_reason` and `test_one_second_attempt_per_node_whichever_code_refused_first`.

### Deployment fork of the vendored bundle, 2026-09-23 (owner-authorised: "authorised to refactor the vendor files if that is better" and "authorised to re-pin")

- D31 (2026-09-23) — `vendor/deploy-v` is a deployment fork, build `99ed0dc3713e9b7ba6055469f77eb1784e4f6b7c3b1a801ac55b9d22581eef7c` (was `78c24be4`), manifest `0075418b` (was `bc13ee89`), because the bundle contradicted itself: its canon and method files tell a model to write what its own checkers refuse, and three prompt reviews (gate/LITE/terminal, CP-1/CP-2, CP-2E..CP-4C; N55) and the live runs of 23 September traced most refusals to that, not to the models or the host. Alternatives: host workarounds (limited to second-attempt lines; the first attempt's prompt cannot change without the same prompt-parity cost) and upstream-only reporting (no module gets passable). What changed, each inside the vendor's own stated intent: (V1) the canon's Missing Information Treatment says a register cell never carries a bare limitation (`[Insufficient Information] — ` plus what is missing, or `—`), and every skill's disqualifier list keeps the bare placeholders but drops `insufficient information`, `unknown` and `not assessable`, which the vendor's own closed value lists use as values, and the substring `quantitative threshold not available in provided materials`, which CP-2's and CP-3's methods prescribe verbatim; (V2) the shared completeness checker matches a contract column up to case, emphasis, dash variants and spacing around `/`, takes a template column (`Period 1…N`, `issuer-specific …`) as the header cells no other column claims, ends a register ID at a sentence full stop, and finds a snake_case register under its title on a heading line; (V3) the shared validator reads QA findings only from tables in QA Validation, skipping rows marked resolved, since the canon's caps are "CP-5A severity gates" charged per CP-5A finding (an analytical Severity scale, such as CP-4's capacity grade or a source gap CP-0 routes to its consumer, no longer forces Blocked and ends the run); (V4) CP-0 marks a consumer CONDITIONAL only when its own workflow cannot proceed without the missing source (otherwise READY_WITH_LIMITATIONS with the gap carried), names the command sheet in Analysis and tables it once as T8 in the appendix (the method had required it in both places), and the T8 parser accepts a backticked or bold module ID or readiness; (V5) every canon core states the score band the validator enforces (below 40 is Insufficient Information). Procedure, repeatable: edit the owning skill's file (shared helpers live in `cp-0-source-readiness/scripts/`), `python3 -B vendor/deploy-v/verify_package.py --refresh` (syncs shared copies, runs the vendor's 53 regression tests and 10 self-checks, regenerates manifest, baseline, integrity and build id), `uv run python scripts/host_manifest.py` (host pin), and the `prompt` goldens re-baselined from the legacy snapshot's own code over the forked bundle (a scratch copy of the snapshot with `vendor/deploy-v` replaced; `tests/parity/generate_goldens.py --package server --root <copy> --group prompt`), so prompt parity is still the legacy host's. One vendor regression test was updated (a bare `unknown` is now a value; it uses `n/a`). Any further vendor edit needs the owner's explicit authorisation again. Evidence: `tests/test_vendor_fork.py` (7 tests, each a reviewed conflict run on the vendor code the host loads); the finding tests in `tests/test_canonical_handoff.py` and `tests/test_second_attempt.py` now put their QA finding in QA Validation; the build and manifest pins in `tests/test_bundle_pin.py`, `test_methodology_bundle.py`, `test_loop_charges.py`, `test_canonical_proof.py`, `test_qualification_matrix.py` and the delivered sizes in `test_delivered_authority.py` are re-measured; `tests/parity/test_prompt.py` 27/27 against the re-baselined goldens.
- D30, fourth addendum (2026-09-23) — The second attempt is also told which of its module's register IDs the answer never writes (`handoff._absent_ids_line`), a fact about the answer rather than a rule: CP-1A live wrote every register under a human title and, told eleven registers were missing, could not see why. Evidence: `tests/test_second_attempt.py::test_retry_feedback_says_which_register_ids_the_answer_never_writes`.
- D30, fifth addendum (2026-09-23) — Three gaps the prompt reviews found in what the second attempt is told (first-attempt bytes unchanged; vendor messages still carried as written): (G2-16, G3-10) the vendor lines are ordered cause-first (validator, CP-0's T8 parser, CP-DR's dossier validator, then completeness with a table that does not parse ahead of the interface tables it voids), and when the lines pass `MAX_FEEDBACK_MESSAGES` the last says how many were not shown (`handoff.capped`), where the tail was dropped silently; (G1-6) CP-0 is told when its T8 names a different module set from the route's (`handoff.readiness_set_line`, module IDs only), a `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE` that carried nothing; (G1-4) CP-DR is told the vendor's dossier validator's own message, which reached neither the model nor the operator. Evidence: `tests/test_second_attempt.py::test_capped_says_how_many_check_messages_were_dropped`, `test_retry_feedback_is_feedback_lines_capped`, `test_a_table_that_does_not_parse_is_named_before_what_it_voids`, `test_the_readiness_set_line_names_what_t8_lacks_and_adds`, `test_cp_dr_is_told_the_dossier_validators_own_message`.

N11 (keep D7's `databricks-langchain`, so N25/CF-034 only if `ChatDatabricks` exposes the id, and N34); N44's revocation half; N14 (measure streaming on the workspace first). **Keep:** N33 (a 429'd attempt keeps its reservation: budgets fail closed); N63 (D34). **Close:** N42 (no second declarable refusal exists) and FP-31 (LOW wording the render goldens bind). Each item is recorded under its own `Fn` or `Dn` when it lands.

- D40 (2026-09-23, owner-approved in D39: "Authorise and re-pin. For N55, the checker follows the method's columns unless a downstream module reads the checker's") — Vendor fork r3 (build `e6fc7978`, manifest `39870ed0`; D31's procedure, run as `verify_package.py --refresh --rebuild-authorities` because `research.py` and the research brief are pinned authority components, authority digest `2480e369`). Closes G1-4, G1-6, G1-14, G1-18, G2-6, G2-10, G2-14, G2-15, G2-18, G3-8, G3-12, G3-13, G3-14, G3-15, G3-17, N55 and N57. **Canon:** a module-status to `qa_status` map ("D1 FROM MODULE STATUS": complete Passed, with gaps or limitations Restricted, Blocked Blocked, a LITE UPGRADE limitation Restricted), pointed at from all 21 canon cores (G3-12); `Not Reviewed` is never a handoff's `qa_status` (G2-18); SEC5 defines UPGRADE and every LITE block that uses it says so (G3-8); an absorbed command writes its owner's one artifact under one opening H3 (CP-1A, CP-2A, CP-1D, CP-2E, CP-L10; G2-18, G3-17, G1-18). **Contracts:** CP-1, CP-1B and CP-1C registers take the method's columns (REF_13, REF_14 and the step specs), keeping only what the host qualification matrix reads -- CP-1B T4.12 `values`/`changes` and CP-1C T4.5 `Total/Net/Sr Sec Leverage`, which the method now defines (G2-6, N55); CP-2D T2E.6 is a six-column register (G2-14); a CP-2 T2.10 direction with no supported driver is one `None supported` row (G2-15); CP-2G's case rule matches a leading word, so `Base case` passes (G3-13); CP-2B's rules are enforced from CP-2A's profile and CP-1A's snapshot table is unconditional only (G2-18); CP-1D carries an empty CP-1 bridge as one `NONE` row per register (G2-10); CP-L10's TL*.4 may be empty, as its payload may (G1-18). **Text:** CP-0 tables CP-DR in T8 on the deep-research routes (G1-6) and, with no objective delivered, assesses readiness against the pathway's use case (G1-18); CP-5 and CP-6 agree that CP-5 is CP-6's QA gate (G1-14); CP-2H cites triggers by the whole evidence line and may paraphrase them in prose (G3-14). **Research (G1-4):** the validator accepts a defined gap row (claim_type `gap`, the canon's `—` in its three gap fields only); an UNRESOLVED finding cites its own gap row; missing evidence is Complete with Gaps, never Blocked. **Scripts:** `covenant_headroom` and `recovery_waterfall` write `Not Calculable` with the missing inputs named instead of a bare placeholder (G3-15); `parse_figure` and the host's `figure_value` strip every digit-group space (thin and narrow no-break included) and `parse_figure` refuses an underflowing exponent; the percent readings (points in `parse_figure`, a fraction in CP-MODEL's `_number`) stay as their callers take them, documented at both definitions (N57). **Pins:** `bundle_pin.py`, the build pins in the tests, `MEASURED` (CP-0 151,778; CP-L10 205,707; CP-5 169,178). **Parity:** all 27 `prompt` goldens and three `forecast` driver-mapping goldens regenerated from the legacy snapshot over fork r3 with the RELATIVE_VALUE CP-1 fixture mirrored. **Tests:** 16 in `tests/test_vendor_fork.py` (e.g. `test_a_research_gap_row_takes_the_canon_null_and_is_cited`, `test_the_checker_follows_the_method_columns_and_keeps_what_is_read`, `test_module_status_maps_to_qa_status_in_the_canon`), 13 vendor regressions.

- F167 — Gate integrity (AR-07, N46, MAX-13): `_hook_problems` matched only `- id:`, the complexity ratchet held only a sum, nothing refused a collection hook or `collect_ignore` in a `conftest.py`, and a bare `scan_floors.py --cover` skipped its claim check. Each pre-commit hook's `entry`/`stages`/`files`/`exclude`/`args` is held to a declared expectation, complexity is budgeted per (path, function), a conftest collection hook is refused unless it is the one pinned in `tests/conftest.py`, and `--cover` needs a directory. Tests `test_a_hook_s_own_body_weakened_in_effect_is_named`, `test_two_baselined_functions_cannot_swap_complexity_unnoticed`, `test_a_conftest_collection_hook_is_refused_unless_pinned`, `test_main_refuses_a_bare_cover_flag_with_no_directory`.

- F168 — Gate configuration (FP-10, in part): complexipy and bandit could be reconfigured through `complexipy.toml`, `.complexipy.toml`, `.bandit` or `[tool.complexipy]`, and `check_pr_size.py` counted a binary file's change as nothing. All four configurations are refused and a binary numstat row is named. Tests `test_a_complexipy_or_bandit_config_file_is_refused`, `test_a_complexipy_table_in_pyproject_is_refused`, `test_a_binary_file_change_is_refused_rather_than_skipped`. A CODEOWNERS file and narrowing `check_pr_size.py`'s `vendor/**` exemption are the enterprise repository's policy (N69).

- F169 — Suppression budget (FP-11): `--against` compared two commits' own committed `gate_baseline.json`, so a change that weakened a `SUPPRESSIONS` pattern could read its weaker count as a fall; `# complexipy: ignore` and a `noqa` code swap were unbudgeted; a retargeted pull request never re-ran the gates. `--against` takes a revision and measures both trees fresh under the current patterns (`suppression_counts_at`, `tracked_python_at`, `blob_at`); `complexipy_ignore` and one budget per `noqa` code are counted at their current values; `edited` is in `pull_request.types`. `CLAUDE.md`'s gate line says so. Tests `test_baseline_problems_measures_the_base_tree_fresh_not_its_own_json`, `test_a_noqa_code_swap_is_named_even_though_the_aggregate_holds`, `test_a_retargeted_pull_request_reruns_the_gates`.

- F170 — Two security tests never ran in CI (FP-21, CF-063): `test_the_dev_proxy_strips_client_identity_and_injects_the_local_actor` and `test_production_build_contains_no_fixture_or_demo_route` skip without Node, and no job had both Node and pytest. The `frontend` job runs them by node id after its build, and `CAOS_REQUIRE_NODE=1` turns their skip into a failure. Tests `test_node_and_vite_fails_rather_than_skips_when_required`, `test_the_frontend_job_runs_the_two_security_tests_that_need_a_real_build`.

- F171 — `${{ github.base_ref }}` was spliced into the size job's `run:` script (FP-40): it passes through `env:`, and any `${{` left in a `run:` script is refused. Test `test_a_template_expression_spliced_into_a_run_script_is_refused`.

- F172 — No gate scanned the uncommitted tree for secrets (CF-064): a second gitleaks hook, pinned like the first, runs `gitleaks dir` over the working tree. Test `test_the_gitleaks_dir_scan_of_the_uncommitted_tree_is_required`.

- F173 — `check_icm.py` checked prompt blocks by name and path only (CF-079): `verify()` reads every declared block through `prompt_block()`, and `manifest_problems()` asserts a freshly generated host manifest equals the committed one. Tests `test_verify_catches_a_declared_block_whose_bytes_do_not_match_the_manifest`, `test_manifest_problems_catches_a_source_file_edited_without_regenerating_it`.

Tests `test_normalise_splits_an_acronym_from_the_word_after_it`, `test_identifiers_includes_pep695_type_parameters`, `test_violations_catches_a_banned_word_spelled_after_an_acronym`.

- F176 — `io_budget.py --assert` checked that a number was declared, never that it held (N35, in part; F123's declared 37 cost 149): `--record` writes the analysis, model and book measurements to `tests/io_measurements.json`, `--assert` refuses a declaration below its recorded measurement, and `BLOB_BUDGET` is a second named dimension. The other blob-reading modules and a 20-node FULL harness stay N35.

The SCIM, workspace-identity and `caos/pricing.py` branches remain (N68).

- F178 — A malformed `CAOS_DATABASE_URL` printed its password (CF-078): the lifespan's unguarded connect raised psycopg's own message to stderr. `_boot_or_refuse()` prints only the typed code, and `scripts/qualify.py` and `scripts/release_pack.py`, which connected the same way, are swept. Tests `test_a_malformed_database_url_refuses_to_start_without_the_password` and the malformed-URL cases in `test_qualify_script.py` and `test_release_pack.py`.

- F179 — A malformed `CAOS_RUN_CEILING` was invisible until the first run (CF-048): the app's boot and the worker's `_configured()` validate it first, as `CAOS_MODEL_PRICE` is. Tests `test_a_malformed_run_ceiling_refuses_to_start`, `test_a_malformed_run_ceiling_refuses_the_worker_at_boot`.

- F181 — The store connection had no socket or statement bound (CF-041): `connect()` asks for TCP keepalives and `tcp_user_timeout`, and takes an optional statement timeout that the worker's polling connection sets under `LEASE_SECONDS`; `apply_schema` passes none. Tests `test_connect_asks_for_keepalives_and_an_optional_statement_timeout`, `test_connect_s_statement_timeout_bounds_the_whole_session`.

- F182 — The checkpoint thread was keyed on the run id alone (CF-037): it is `<run_id>:<route digest>`, so a thread checkpointed under another route shape is absent, and the worker's `_forget` uses the same key. The ledger stays the truth (D6). Test `test_a_checkpoint_thread_is_bound_to_the_pinned_route_s_digest`.

- F186 — A parked run left no event and no log line (CF-044, backend half): `RunEvent.RUN_PARKED` (migration `0032`) is appended when the worker parks a run, whose code it prints first, and `RunSummary.stop_code` carries the code to the runs list and the Directory (wire regenerated). Tests `test_a_parked_run_s_stop_code_reaches_the_runs_list`, `test_the_directory_names_a_parked_run_s_stop_code`.

- F190 — A static directory redirect built its `Location` from the client's Host over http (CF-086): `site.py` rewrites a 307/308 `Location` to its path and query. Test `test_a_directory_redirect_never_echoes_the_client_host`.

- F191 — Every `/assets/` response, refusals included, was cached as immutable for a year (CF-087): only a 200 or 304 is. Test `test_the_immutable_asset_cache_is_never_set_on_a_refusal`.

- F192 — uvicorn's `limit_concurrency` counted idle keep-alive connections and answered a bare 503 with no security headers (CF-051): `EdgeGuard` bounds in-flight requests and refuses `CONCURRENCY_LIMIT_REACHED` (503, transient, with the headers and Retry-After), and `serve.py` sets explicit keep-alive and header-read bounds, the tuned values staying N26. Tests `test_the_in_flight_limit_is_a_typed_refusal_with_headers_and_retry_after`, `test_main_moves_the_concurrency_bound_off_uvicorns_own_count`.

- F193 — A 51-document upload was told `REQUEST_INVALID` (CF-075): admission parses one part past `max_documents` and refuses `SOURCE_TOO_LARGE` itself. A pack far past the ceiling still meets the parser's own bound first and is answered `REQUEST_INVALID`; that bound is what keeps an oversized form from being read, so it stays. Test `test_a_pack_over_the_document_ceiling_is_source_too_large`.

- F194 — The QUALIFIED read served only the reviewer name an admin typed (CF-027): `QualificationRead.reviewer_id` is the authenticated signer (`verdict_reviewer_id`), as the release pack already serves. Test `test_verdict_reviewer_id_is_the_authenticated_signer_or_none`.

- F195 — Start and Retry needed a preview after a reload, and the tail's reconnect read as a pause (N48, backend half): the run read serves `input_fingerprint`, and the cursor frame carries an SSE `retry:` line. The frontend half is the design session's. Test `test_the_cursor_frame_carries_a_short_reconnect_retry`.

- F196 — A request waiting on another request's SCIM lookup held an AnyIO worker thread (N36, ED-8): identity is an async dependency; the leading lookup runs on one thread and its waiters await the flight's `anyio.Event`. The identity tests run as concurrent tasks on one loop, as requests do; `test_platform_boot.py` passes.

- F197 — One reader could hold both evidence-page `FRAME_CHILDREN` slots (N38, EV-7): an actor holds at most one (`ACTOR_FRAME_CHILDREN`). Test `test_one_actor_cannot_hold_both_frame_children_slots`.

- F198 — Identity-cache expiry had no test (CF-070): `test_the_positive_and_negative_caches_expire_on_their_own_schedules` moves a replaced clock past `CACHE_SECONDS` and `NEGATIVE_SECONDS`.

- F199 — `caos/store/members.py` said both the case standing and the global role are rechecked at commit (CF-081): only the standing is; the global role comes from the identity cache (D10, F13).

- F200 — The operator clearances gave the wrong fix (G2-17): `HANDOFF_BLOCKED` reads "Supply what the module's stated blocker names, then start a new run.", and `HANDOFF_MALFORMED`, `HANDOFF_IDENTITY_MISMATCH`, `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE` and `HANDOFF_UNDECLARED_FIELD` read "Retry the attempt; an operator must verify a stored handoff refused on read." Test `test_the_handoff_clearances_name_the_right_fix`.

- F201 — Three `IO_BUDGET` numbers were declared, not measured (N9): `reads/model.py` and `reads/qualification.py` were already exact; `commands/members.py` now declares `GRANT_IO`, `REVOKE_IO` and `WITHDRAW_IO` (13, 13, 11), each held exactly by `test_each_new_command_meets_its_declared_store_budget`.

- F202 — Any document with `%PDF-` in its first kilobyte was read as a PDF, so a text memo naming `%PDF-1.7` near its top was refused (CF-074): a header at byte 0 declares a PDF, and a later one in the first kilobyte counts only with `%%EOF` in the last kilobyte, so a PDF behind leading junk still dispatches as one. Tests `test_a_text_that_mentions_a_pdf_header_near_its_top_is_read_as_text`, `test_a_pdf_with_leading_junk_before_its_header_is_still_read_as_pdf`.

- F203 — The PDF extractor made one token of a run of any length, and the cut measured raw length where `BoundaryText` measures NFC (CF-072, CF-073 for PDF): PDF runs are cut on their NFC form (`nfc_pieces`), each piece taking its share of the run's rectangle, under `PdfExtractor` identity v3; v2 rows verify as recorded. Tests `test_a_run_past_the_token_limit_is_cut_and_shares_its_rectangle`, `test_a_pdf_with_a_long_run_admits_and_its_neighbours_stay_citable`, `test_the_pdf_cut_is_chosen_on_the_nfc_form`.

- F204 — `line_groups` cut a wide line inside a word, so a block quoted whole was refused `CITATION_NOT_LOCATED` (CF-013): admission packs between tokens as packing 2 (`source_extractions.format_version`, migration `0035_packing_by_token`, bound by the output digest), anchoring reads packing 2 back by walking each block's pieces, and a source whose lines all fit stays packing 1 byte for byte. No prompt text moved. Tests `test_a_wide_line_is_cut_between_its_tokens_never_inside_one`, `test_each_block_of_a_split_line_can_be_quoted_whole`, `test_a_source_whose_lines_all_fit_keeps_packing_one`, `test_a_token_packed_source_whose_blocks_no_longer_tile_its_lines_refuses`.

- F205 — Admission measured NFC on Python's Unicode tables and `_group_counts` on Postgres's (N40, EV-1): a code point unassigned in Python's `unicodedata` (`Cn`) is refused `SOURCE_NOT_READABLE`, and the comment names the upgrade check (`unicode_version()` against `unicodedata.unidata_version`, 15.1 on both). Tests `test_a_document_carrying_a_code_point_unicode_does_not_assign_is_refused`, `test_a_code_point_this_python_assigns_is_admitted`.

- F206 — `TokenIndex` rebuilt a page's keys for every citation, 21.5 s for 512 citations of a 240,000-token page (N41, MAX-06): each page derives its exact keys, NFC keys and tracking join once (3.7 s). Test `test_one_index_derives_a_pages_search_keys_once`.

- F207 — Nothing bounded concurrent admissions, each of which holds its pack twice (N5): a process-wide slot (`ADMISSION_SLOTS` = 2) is taken after the writer's checks and before the pack is read, and given back when the admission answers. Tests `test_an_admission_waits_for_a_slot_before_it_reads_its_pack`, `test_a_refused_admission_gives_its_slot_back`, `test_a_stranger_is_answered_without_waiting_for_a_slot`.

- F208 — PDF text drawn in render mode 3 (every scan's OCR layer), near the colour behind it, or in glyphs under 2 pt was admitted with nothing to tell the approver or the model (N27, D39; N27's entry said such text was dropped, which it was not): `visibility.MarkingAggregator` marks each line with why, under `PdfExtractor` identity v4 and migration `0036_hidden_text`, the evidence page read serves `PageLine.hidden`, and the prompt prefixes a marked line `[host: not visible on the rendered page: <reasons>]`. The text stays citable. Tests in `tests/test_hidden_text.py`.

- F209 — `hides_text` refused every variation selector but U+FE0E/U+FE0F after a drawn character (N49, EV-3, D39): one selector is accepted after the base it can change (VS1–VS16 and U+034F after any drawn character, VS17–VS256 after a CJK ideograph, FVS1–FVS4 after a Mongolian letter); runs and base-less selectors stay refused, and `visible` removes the same set. Tests `test_one_selector_after_the_base_it_can_change_is_text_a_reader_sees`, `test_a_selector_with_no_base_it_can_change_is_hidden`, `test_the_selector_bases_are_the_tables_this_python_knows`.

- F210 — The second attempt was told neither which host text bound an answer broke nor which readiness row's blocker cell passed 512 characters (G1-16), so a retry repeated the fault: `feedback_lines` adds a "host text check:" line naming the bound and a "host readiness check:" line naming the row, never quoting the answer. First-attempt bytes unchanged. Tests `test_a_host_text_bound_is_named_for_the_second_attempt_never_quoted`, `test_a_blocker_cell_past_its_bound_is_named_by_its_row_never_quoted`.

- D41 (2026-09-23, owner-approved in D39; widens D30) — `HANDOFF_IDENTITY_MISMATCH` and `HANDOFF_UNDECLARED_FIELD` join `SECOND_ATTEMPT_CODES` (G1-16). The feedback names only the differing host-owned fields, upgrade keys or undeclared fields -- at most 20, the rest counted, never a value -- and identity is judged under the refused attempt's own ordinal. Tests `test_an_identity_mismatch_gets_the_second_attempt_naming_the_field`, `test_an_undeclared_field_gets_the_second_attempt_naming_it`, `test_the_front_matter_lines_name_fields_only_and_at_most_twenty`.

- F211 — A CP-2G driver row the vendor permits as NOT_APPLICABLE refused CP-CF `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE`, figures with comma groups or parentheses were not read, and `FORECAST_DRIVER_NOT_READY` was never raised (G3-9): `forecast.driver_value` reads them exactly as Decimals, `validate_driver_mapping` refuses `FORECAST_DRIVER_NOT_READY` when only not-ready rows fault and `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE` otherwise, CP-CF's second attempt names the CP-2G row by its ID, and `forecast_extension.md` (mirrored into the legacy snapshot; the three CP-2G-bearing prompt goldens regenerated) and CP-CF's brief say a READY zero needs its own source line. Tests `test_a_driver_value_reads_comma_groups_and_parentheses_exactly`, `test_a_not_applicable_driver_is_not_ready_but_a_bad_one_incomplete`, `test_a_not_applicable_cp2g_driver_stops_cp_cf_as_not_ready`, `test_cp_cf_second_attempt_names_the_driver_row_it_could_not_map`.

Tests `test_bills_at_compares_the_whole_price_a_provider_states`, `test_a_provider_charging_at_another_price_is_refused_before_any_attempt`, `test_a_reservation_at_another_price_is_refused_before_the_call`.

- F213 — An empty answer became a successful, billed `Completion` (CF-047): it is `PROVIDER_RESPONSE_INVALID`, still billed. Test `test_a_blank_answer_is_an_invalid_response_billed_not_a_success`.

- F214 — Pydantic printed a serializer warning quoting part of the answer when content came back as a list (CF-077): that one warning is contained by category and message around the call (serial calls; `catch_warnings` is not thread-safe). Tests `test_only_the_list_content_serializer_warning_is_contained`, `test_an_answer_in_content_parts_prints_no_warning_quoting_it`.

- F215 — A pinned route was trusted as stored (CF-025): `routes.require_catalog_route` re-resolves it from the pinned build's catalog when the run input is pinned and when the execution input is read, refusing `ROUTE_IDENTITY_INVALID`. Test `test_the_bundle_probe_proves_every_listed_file_at_most_every_ttl`.

- D42 (2026-09-23, owner-approved in D39) — N1 closed: runs stay sequential. `route.reachable` and `independent_batch`, which keyed a module-id edge set by route-node id and so returned empty sets, had no production caller and are deleted with their seven tests (`tests/test_concurrent_frontier.py`) (CF-035); the parity route compare drops `reachable`, and the route goldens were regenerated from the legacy package without the field.

- D43 (2026-09-23, owner-approved in D39) — Seven refusal codes no condition raised are retired: `ENVELOPE_INVALID`, `ENVELOPE_UNDECLARED_FIELD`, `ENVELOPE_UNCITED_CLAIM`, `READINESS_INVALID`, `READINESS_INCOMPLETE`, `FORECAST_CHAIN_BROKEN` with `cash_flow._check_chain`, which compared values that are always equal (N6), and `FORECAST_RESIDUAL_UNRECONCILED` (CF-099). `FORECAST_DRIVER_NOT_READY` (F211) and the two `DELIVERABLE_*` codes (N4's read) stay. The wire schema and `documents.ts` follow.

- D44 (2026-09-23, owner-approved in D39) — N20: the cash-flow reconciliation tolerance is the smaller of the stated tolerance and one part in a thousand of the opening debt and cash balances, still refused below zero or above `MAX_TOLERANCE` (FP-23); a case's periods run in fiscal-year order after the opening (FP-37); `MAX_WORK`, which could not fire, is deleted (FP-36); `cfo` is documented as before interest and taxes in the calculator and CP-CF's brief (FP-39). No golden moved; host pins regenerated. Tests `test_the_reconciliation_tolerance_is_relative_to_the_opening_balances`, `test_a_case_s_periods_run_in_fiscal_year_order_after_the_opening`, `test_cfo_is_named_as_before_interest_and_taxes_where_the_model_reads`.

- D45 (2026-09-23, owner-approved in D39) — N39: accepted handoffs are bounded by `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` (4 MiB), the largest answer transport accepts, not the vendor's 26 MB `MAX_FILE_BYTES`, whose validation took 6-22 s on every read; a larger one refuses `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` before any vendor validator. Test `test_a_handoff_past_the_response_bound_is_malformed_at_once`.

- F217 — Integration of the backlog branches: the frontend's hand-written validators (`frontend/src/wire/v1/documents.ts`) and its fixtures and inline test documents follow the wire changes (`stop_code`, `input_fingerprint`, `reviewer_id`, `PageLine.hidden`, `CONCURRENCY_LIMIT_REACHED`, the seven retired codes); `scripts/tracked._git` takes one argument sequence, keeping the positional-width budget at 24; the complexity snapshot holds 23 functions, and `CLAUDE.md` says so.

- F218 — A run cancelled while QUEUED or stopped, after a release or a park mid-route, kept its LangGraph thread for ever, because no worker held it to forget it (N21, DL-8): `request_cancel` deletes the run's thread (`<run_id>:<route digest>`) from the three checkpoint tables in the unit that ends the run, only for a run ever claimed and only for tables that exist. Tests `test_a_run_cancelled_while_queued_leaves_no_checkpoint_thread`, `test_a_cancel_with_no_checkpoint_schema_still_ends_the_run`.

- F219 — First boot needed a hand-run `GRANT USAGE, CREATE ON SCHEMA public` (DL-1, F141, MAX-22): the store lives in its own schema, `caos_store`, created by `apply_schema` under the schema lock and named on every connection as its `search_path`, so `schema.sql` and every digest are unchanged and the bundle's `CAN_CONNECT_AND_CREATE` is the whole grant. A role that may not create it is refused `STORE_SCHEMA_DRIFT`, printing only `schema: sqlstate 42501`. Nothing was deployed with data in `public`, so no data migration is provided; an existing local dev volume needs `GRANT CREATE ON DATABASE caos_dev TO caos_app` once. Tests `test_the_store_lives_in_its_own_schema_and_never_in_public`, `test_the_app_starts_on_the_bundle_s_database_grant_alone`, `test_a_role_that_may_not_create_the_schema_is_refused_by_sqlstate`.

- F220 — `run_attempts` and `artifacts` still accepted UPDATE and DELETE (CF-091's remainder, N16): the app only ever inserts into them, so migration `0037` refuses UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE on both (and supersedes `0033`'s header comment, which an applied migration keeps). Tests that corrupt a row on purpose do it through `conftest.tamper`, one statement as replica, so each still proves the application's own verification catches it. Test `test_run_attempts_and_artifacts_are_immutable`.

- D46 (2026-09-23, owner-approved in D39) — N15: an actor may have at most `MAX_QUEUED_RUNS_PER_ACTOR` = 4 runs queued or running (a run holds a worker node by node, and each queued run can commit up to one run ceiling). Start and Retry count it inside the queue write under a per-actor advisory lock taken last, refusing the new `QUEUED_RUNS_LIMIT_REACHED` (409); a cancel is never capped. The owner is `run_work.requested_by` (migration `0038`). Aggregate spend limits are the AI Gateway's, an enterprise setting. Tests `test_an_actor_s_queued_runs_are_capped_and_a_cancel_frees_a_place`, `test_one_actor_racing_past_the_queue_cap_queues_exactly_the_cap`.

- F221 — N4 (owner-approved in D39): the deliverable's page and audit package were produced only by the suite. `caos/api/reads/deliverable.py` serves `GET /api/v1/cases/{case_id}/revisions/{revision_id}/render` (a frozen or filed revision's page, byte for byte what `render` makes of its own stored payload; `DELIVERABLE_NOT_FROZEN` before an unfrozen one, a render refusal's own code otherwise) and `.../package` (a filed revision's audit package, built from `read_filed_receipt`'s proven bytes and passing `verify_package`, as an attachment); both at `VisibleCase`, the floor Report and Committee read at. The render answers a narrower Content-Security-Policy of its own (its inline stylesheet needs `'unsafe-inline'`, which the workspace policy refuses; no image, script, form, base or frame is allowed). Committee's body carries `render_url` and, once filed, `package_url` (wire, `documents.ts` and the committee fixture follow). `IO_BUDGET` 4 and 8, `BLOB_BUDGET` 1 and 2. Tests in `tests/test_deliverable_reads.py` (22).

- F222 — A superseded revision could still be signed, frozen and filed (CF-026, D39: one head per run): `deliverable._reviewed` checks the run's live head and refuses `COMMAND_EXPECTATION_STALE`, and the Report section's availability says the same for SIGN, FREEZE and FILE. `SIGN_IO`, `FREEZE_IO` and `FILE_IO` each rise by the one read (15, 56, 57). Tests in `tests/test_governed_write_routes.py` and `tests/test_command_availability.py`.

- F223 — The narrative's author could sign it (FP-33, D39): `sign_opinion_in` refuses `APPROVER_NOT_INDEPENDENT` when the signer saved the revision being signed; the tests that signed as the saver now mint a separate signer.

- F224 — The deliverable proof re-checked owner restrictions for CP-5 only (FP-32, D39): the node selection is one constant, `CREDIT_SCREEN_SELECTION`, beside `verify_owner_restrictions`, and CP-CF is checked as CP-5 is in the deliverable and qualification proofs. Test `test_proof_and_payload_reject_a_self_consistent_cp_cf_restriction_forgery`.

- F225 — A qualification set's digest tagged two of its nine optional fields, so two different sets could share one (N8, FP-25, D39): `matrix._digested` tags all nine; the 18 committed set digests and their `RESULT.md` files are recomputed, before any enterprise verdict binds one.

- F226 — The reviewer's document did not name the evidence it signs (N44's first half, D39): verdict `BINDINGS` gain `evidence_sha256`, checked by `read_verdict`, `record_verdict` and `current_verdict`, and `SignVerdict` carries it (wire regenerated; `commands.ts` follows).

- F227 — Recorded producers were compared with the prepared model only at signing (FP-24): `record_performed` compares them before a snapshot counts complete (`_models_confirmed`).

- F228 — A qualification case could spend a run on a quote its document cannot contain (FP-26): `_answerable` refuses `QUALIFICATION_KEY_UNANSWERABLE` before any spend, by a word-run check over the public extractor that is looser than the real search, so it never refuses a quote the search would anchor, and defers to `SOURCE_HAS_NO_TEXT` for a document with no text.

- F229 — `qualification/ba-fy2025-covenant-refinancing/` held documents and no set (FP-29): every route that set could take needs a financial-statement module its three debt-instrument documents cannot supply, so the directory is removed rather than given an invented key; `documents.json` and `DOCUMENTS.md` follow.

- F230 — Qualification tidiness (FP-30): docstring counts corrected, one `_LABEL_LIMIT`, one accepted-rows fallback, named fields for `Evidence`; behaviour unchanged.

- F231 — `read_filed_receipt` trusted its one caller to have checked the sign and freeze events (FP-34): it verifies that provenance itself; Committee's `IO_BUDGET` 20 to 22.

- F232 — A package's three-people check compared role names case-sensitively (CF-084): `_receipt_role_error` compares them case-folded, so one identity under two spellings is one.

- F233 — `release_pack.py` could print a Refusal's traceback (CF-094): already handled by F178's guard; a regression test pins the refusal-only exit.

- F234 — The plain-text extractor kept a leading byte order mark as the first word's first character, so the first line could not be quoted as shown (CF-017), and its token cut measured raw length where `BoundaryText` measures NFC (CF-073's plain-text half): `PlainTextExtractor` v4 decodes `utf-8-sig` and cuts with `nfc_pieces`; v1-v3 rows verify as recorded, and `page._text_frame` re-reads a document under the encoding its identity names. The 15 extraction goldens were regenerated from the legacy snapshot with the extractor mirrored; only each case's identity moved.

- D47 (2026-09-24, owner-authorised under D39/D40) — Vendor fork r4 (build `820dfc7c`, manifest `a80849a8`; D31's procedure) closes N70, fork r3's leftovers: CP-2's deprecated `Mixed` direction becomes a Positive and a Negative row in the method and in `cp2.materiality_direction_enum`; `cp2.cp_model_strengths_weaknesses` and `cp2g.cp_model_forecast_drivers` are unconditional only; the research-brief pointer in the other 19 modules and the canon applies where the brief is delivered and otherwise records a gap; the host's CP-DR fixture reports Restricted by D40's status map, which a host test pins. `MEASURED` CP-0 151,927, CP-L10 205,856, CP-5 169,493; the 27 prompt goldens regenerated over fork r4. Tests: 3 vendor regressions, 4 in `tests/test_vendor_fork.py`.

- F235 — N28, CF-016, AI-4 and AI-6 (owner: "Host enforces whole lines", D39): an accepted citation is one whole evidence line as the module was shown it -- a whole token line where it fits, each block of a wider one (packing 2 by the pieces the walk reads back, packing 1 by each width-cut block whose edges fall between tokens) -- the only such line on its page, with only the normalised pass's edge punctuation forgiven (`citations.WHOLE_LINE`); part of a line refuses `CITATION_NOT_LOCATED`. A record names the rule its citations were accepted under (`CanonicalRecord.citation_rule`, written only when it is not `ANY_RUN`, so every stored record keeps its bytes), and the proof and freeze re-anchor it by that rule. Short quotes are now cheap: 64 one- and two-word quotes of a 240,000-token page take 0.08 s against 20.7 s. `tagged.md` names every content section and says every value in any section, host-labelled ones included, is data; the UPSTREAM CITATION REGISTER and HOST SOURCE PREPARATION headers no longer call document quotes or uploader filenames host-owned. The 12 of 57 qualification answer keys that were fragments are restated as whole lines (7 set digests re-pinned); fixtures cite whole lines. Prompt and forecast goldens regenerated from the legacy snapshot with the texts and fixture quotes mirrored. **Status (2026-10-04): superseded for new answers by D105** (`EXCERPT`); a record accepted under this rule keeps `whole-line-as-shown` and is re-anchored by it.

- F236 — FP-39's prompt half: the owners that assign `cfo` on a CP-CF route were not told the calculator deducts cash interest and cash taxes itself (D44), so a CFO already after them would be deducted twice; `forecast_extension.md` defines `cfo` as before cash interest and cash taxes. The three prompt goldens that carry the extension regenerated from the legacy snapshot.

- F237 — The SDK's `FilesExt.download()` tried a presigned-URL mode first, which no local test exercised (CF-095): `caos/workspace.py` builds every client with `disable_experimental_files_api_client=True`, so the plain Files API path the blob tests cover is the one used; proved against the stub.

- D48 (2026-09-24) — `anyio` is declared as a direct dependency at the version already locked (4.15.1): production code imports it (identity's shared wait, F196; the admission slot, F207), and it was only ever locked through fastapi and starlette. `uv lock --offline` resolved from the cache and changed no other package.

- F238 — `caos/api/commands/cases.py` cited a `CLAUDE.md` section that no longer exists (CF-076): the comment says orphaned blobs, written before an admission that then failed, are kept on purpose, because deleting governed bytes is the workspace volume's retention policy (enterprise).

- F239 — The Playwright journey harness drove a stand-in edge whose Python half was dropped (F8, D14) and whose HMAC mode is gone (F188) (CF-102): `frontend/playwright.journey.config.ts`, `frontend/tests/journey/journey.spec.ts` and the `test:journey` script are removed; `tests/journey/pack.py` stays, still imported by the admission tests.

- F240 — Typed-refusal branches with no test in workspace identity and pricing (N68, CF-071's remainder): a client construction the SDK refuses reaching `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`, and `priced_request`'s non-Decimal, negative or over-ceiling byte counts, now have tests.

- F241 — The runbook's health smoke sent no bearer, and neither runbook had a rollback path (CF-096): `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`'s smoke carries the bearer `enterprise_deploy.py` reads, held in a variable and never printed, and both it and `ENTERPRISE_HANDOFF.md` give the rollback (redeploy the previous commit with the one command; the store's migrations are forward-only).

- F242 — `blockers.md` was stale (CF-062): B1 is closed into B2 (the CLI is installed, only the login remains), B4 and B8 are resolved since F110 (the owner's key has been in use through `tests/qualify_openrouter.py`), and B8's recipe names `--live-provider`, without which every live test is deselected.

- F243 — The build spec described a graph that no longer exists (CF-100): §4, §6 and §12 of `docs/rebuild/2026-09-22-caos-databricks-spec.md` are corrected in place, each with its `(corrected: Dn/Fn)` pointer (the `interrupt()` gates, the `RunState` TypedDict, `graph/node.py`, `store/connect.py`, the message-list invoke, `chat_model(effort=...)` and `caos-serve`).

- F244 — `ENTERPRISE_HANDOFF.md`'s "Afterwards" said no live model had met D30's second attempt: it states the live record (one set qualified end to end on GPT-6 Luna Pro; CP-0, CP-3D and CP-5 accepted live; stored CP-1 and CP-1A answers pass on replay; no other module yet reached by a real model), D38's portfolio inputs supplied as a CSV or PDF source, and the rollback.

- F245 — The endpoint, price, run ceiling and group defaults were copied in four places (N23): `scripts/bundle_defaults.py` reads `databricks.yml`'s own variable defaults (no YAML dependency), and `enterprise_deploy.sh`, `enterprise_deploy.py` and the stub take them from it.

- F246 — No deployment row drove a blob write or a model call through the deployed app's own HTTP surface (CF-054): row E10 admits a tiny text source to the case E9 leaves and starts the smallest enabled route as the deployer, proving one model call and one blob write through the app; proved against the stub in `tests/test_enterprise_deploy.py`.

- F247 — The deploy's own governed case was titled "deployment stream check" (N22): it is "CAOS deployment check (safe to archive)", and `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`'s operator notes say what it is and that it may be kept or archived.

- F249 — Integration of the backlog branches, second pass: the package read's cost is 10, not 8, since `read_filed_receipt` proves the sign and freeze events itself (F231); the CP-CF restriction-forgery and release-pack forgery tests corrupt `artifacts` through `conftest.tamper` (F220); the reviewer-id test records runs before the snapshot, as production does (F227); the PDF harness test names its extracted line as the quote, since the shared case helper now takes the whole raw line (F235).

- F250 — `route.dependency_order` hand-rolled a topological sort at complexity 20 (N29, SI-6): it runs on `graphlib.TopologicalSorter` with the same ready-batch order and the same `ROUTE_DUPLICATE_MODULE` and `ROUTE_HAS_A_CYCLE` refusals (complexity 9; the route goldens pin the order).

- F251 — Dead code with no defect behind it (N29, SI-6, SI-7, SI-9): `stream.case_tail`'s two unused overloads, `_status_refusal`'s branch that always agreed with its fallthrough, `provider.TRANSIENT` and `app.PERMANENT` (duplicated tables; `app.TRANSIENT` is derived from `_STATUS`), `verify_package.VERIFIER_VERSION` (referenced nowhere); `runs.fail_run`, which only tests called, lives in `tests/run_terminals.py`.

- F252 — Hand-built plumbing where the library already had it (SI-10): `lakebase.store_url` uses `psycopg.conninfo.make_conninfo`, and `blobs._not_found` matches `databricks.sdk.errors.NotFound` by `isinstance`, the error code kept as a fallback.

- F253 — "Store both bodies, or refuse `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`" was spelled out in both `runtime._settle` and `ModuleProvider.execute`: it is `BlobStore.put_both`.

- F254 — The 27 stage contracts repeated the same 36-line Process and Outputs sections (SI-12): they are stated once in `icm/CONTEXT.md` and each stage points at them, through `scripts/icm_stages.py`; the contracts are not delivered to a model, so no prompt moved.

- F255 — Comments and docstrings cited documents this repository does not hold (CF-101, FP-35): `docs/DECISIONS.md`, `SYSTEM_SPEC.md`, `docs/AI_CODE_QUALITY.md`, `docs/REBUILD_PLAN.md`, `docs/REPAIR_PLAN.md`, `docs/COMPLETION_PLAN.md` and "CLAUDE.md known gaps" are repointed to their decisions or the spec, or dropped, across 43 files in `caos/` and `scripts/` and three frontend files. `render.py`'s bytes moved with its comments, so `verify_package.RENDERER_SHA256` is regenerated.

- F256 — `handoff.MAX_TRANSPORT_CHARS` was twice the vendor's 26 MB `MAX_FILE_BYTES`: it is twice `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES`, D45's handoff bound, keeping the margin the retry feedback's own transport needs.

- F257 — Model and Book called `read_analysis` and dropped the tables it built (N58): they read `read_analysis_without_tables`, which skips `handoff_tables`; the document is otherwise the same. Tests `test_read_analysis_without_tables_matches_read_analysis_but_the_tables` and a call count in each section's tests.

- F258 — N59's backend half: `NarrativeFigure` carries `record_sha256` and `source_id`, resolved at read time by `source_sets.cited_source_ids`, so a figure can open its evidence; the saved revision is untouched. Test `test_cited_source_ids_resolves_the_run_pin_and_refuses_an_uncited_document`.

- F259 — N60's backend half: `BookColumn.unit` (percent, currency, multiple, count or none), declared per column.

- F260 — N61's backend half: `HandoffView`, `PendingNode` and `NodeView` carry `module_name`, read from each module's manifest folder (`bundle.module_display_names`), so the frontend need not mirror the stage slugs. Test `test_module_display_names_reads_the_bundle_catalog`.

- F261 — Start and Retry were offered while the command would refuse `QUEUED_RUNS_LIMIT_REACHED` (D46): the Run section counts the actor's queued and claimed runs (`QUEUE_IO` 1) and names the refusal. Test `test_a_full_queue_refuses_start_and_retry_as_the_commands_would`.

- F262 — A worker whose lease had expired could write one more checkpoint after a cancel had forgotten the thread (F218): `_forget` also forgets when the run is found no longer RUNNING, not only after the worker's own move. Test `test_a_late_checkpoint_write_after_an_abandoned_cancel_is_still_forgotten`.

- F263 — The admission slot (F207) waited without a deadline: it waits `ADMISSION_WAIT_SECONDS` (30) and then refuses `CONCURRENCY_LIMIT_REACHED` (503, Retry-After). Test `test_an_admission_refuses_concurrency_limit_reached_once_its_wait_expires`.

- F264 — Render mode 7 (text added to the clip path, drawn nowhere) was not marked (N27): it is marked as render mode 3 is; no fixture's output moved, so no identity bump. Test `test_render_mode_7_is_marked_the_same_as_render_mode_3`.

- F265 — `BLOB_BUDGET` was declared only for analysis, model and book (N35): it is declared and held by tests for the evidence, run, report and deliverable reads and the deliverable commands, and is zero where nothing is downloaded (cases, deps, health, app); the plain-number budgets join `io_measurements.json`.

- F266 — A connection lost during `apply_schema` read as `STORE_SCHEMA_DRIFT`, which ends the worker's boot (R24-05): an `OperationalError` without a SQLSTATE and the connection, rollback, resource and operator classes (08, 40, 53, 57) are `STORE_UNAVAILABLE` and retried; a statement the database refuses is still drift.

- F267 — An approver who lost standing while a call was out excluded that paid answer from replay for good (R24-06): the post-call run checks raise `RunRefusal`, which `_run_node` never records as the answer's refusal, so the answer is replayed once standing is restored, with no new call. Test `test_authority_lost_during_a_call_leaves_the_paid_answer_to_replay`.

- F268 — CP-DR's dossier refusal never reached the second attempt, because the vendor's exception was dropped before the text filter (R24-07): `_vendor_said` turns it into text first, and `retry_feedback` carries it.

- F269 — A resend could start after the provider call's total deadline (R24-08): `_sends_again` rechecks the clock after the 429 wait and the resend fence.

- F270 — Quote matching in the answer body did quadratic near-match work (R24-09): starts are compared one by one only while that costs no more than the body, otherwise one KMP pass over the inner words (`citations.occurrences`, now public) with edge checks per start.

- F271 — A parenthesised figure was rounded to 28 digits or raised `decimal.Overflow` (R24-10): `_exact` negates with `copy_negate`, so `(x)` reads as `-x` exactly and an out-of-range one has no value, as its positive form does.

- F272 — Qualification's pre-spend check refused whole-line keys the citation matcher accepts (R24-18): `_locatable` compares NFKD case-folded letters and digits, a necessary condition of anchoring under either rule, so it only refuses what could never anchor.

- F273 — `qualify.py` derived the run ceiling before validating its inputs (R24-N03): an empty set is `QUALIFICATION_SET_EMPTY` and a non-finite ceiling `MONEY_INVALID`, exit 2, before any database is created.

- F274 — An explicit `-p`/`--profile` sent a stand-in deployment to the real workspace that profile named (R24-02): the stand-in refuses a `databricks` command carrying either flag, including inside `sh -c`, and hands the CLI an empty private config. Test `test_an_explicit_profile_never_reaches_the_workspace_it_names`.

- F275 — The stand-in deleted bundle state it had no evidence it owned (R24-N02): a `resources.json` without a sync snapshot is kept, and an empty validate-only target is still cleared.

- F276 — Group names containing a comma broke the one-command deploy, because the CLI splits a `--var` on commas (R24-15): the group names travel as `BUNDLE_VAR_group_admin` and `BUNDLE_VAR_group_analyst`.

- F277 — A required CI step or job could be switched off with `if:` and still pass the gate-integrity check (R24-11): `check_gate_config` refuses any `if:` on a required gate's step or job.

- F278 — A package whose local ZIP headers disagreed with its central directory could extract different bytes from those verified (R24-17): `verify_package` compares each member's local CRC and sizes with the directory's, in the detached verifier too.

- D49 (2026-09-24, owner: "Autoscaling default"; R24-14) — Lakebase Autoscaling is the one-command deployment's default database; closes N13 and N45. The CLI cannot choose the app resource's form from a variable, so the target does: `dev` and `prod` bind a Lakebase Autoscaling project through the `postgres` resource and `CAOS_LAKEBASE_ENDPOINT`; `dev-provisioned` and `prod-provisioned` bind an existing Provisioned instance through the `database` resource and `CAOS_LAKEBASE_INSTANCE`; `scripts/enterprise_deploy.sh --provisioned` selects the second pair. E1 and E8 use the chosen kind's own API, and E2 holds the resolved binding to it. At runtime exactly one kind is configured, or the app refuses `STORE_NOT_CONFIGURED` before any mint. Changing an existing app's kind changes its Postgres role (Databricks Apps documentation), so a switch is an owner and DBA procedure (`docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` §7). The stand-in answers the Postgres API and checks app resources; platform injection of `PG*` for a `postgres` resource, role provisioning and live grants remain enterprise checks (E3, E6, E8).

- F279 — The Run API advertised and created `LITE_DEEP_RESEARCH` and `DEEP_RESEARCH` runs that Pin input could never give their required research brief, so both research workflows stopped before execution (R24-01, P1): `PinRunInput.research` carries a closed `ResearchBrief` (the caller-authored fields of `CP_DR_RESEARCH_BRIEF_V1.md`), `run_inputs.linked_research_brief` completes it with the host's fixed and subject-derived keys before `bound_research_brief` judges it as before, and the Run section's pin form asks for it on a route carrying CP-DR. Tests `test_a_research_route_requires_and_accepts_a_bound_brief` (pins and approves both gates) and the form's two frontend cases.

- F280 — A Report save that answered after the reader had left navigated back to the abandoned Report (R24-03): its success callback moves the address only while the form is mounted (F160's pattern). Test in `report.test.tsx`.

- F281 — Creating a run kept the previous run's `revision` in the address, which Report and Committee then refused (R24-04): the new run's address drops it. Test `test_creating_a_run_drops_a_revision_that_belonged_to_the_previous_run`.

- F282 — Model charts labelled dimensionless ratios in the forecast's currency, e.g. gross leverage as `10.0000 USD millions` (R24-12): `ModelValue.unit` (MONEY, MULTIPLE or RATIO) comes from the calculator's own shape, and each chart takes its line's unit. Tests in `tests/test_model_section.py` and `model.test.tsx`.

- F283 — Analysis captions summed known values without saying one was missing, and the maturity ladder dropped a facility with an unknown principal before choosing the nearest date (R24-13): a caption states a known subtotal when a value is missing, and every facility is kept, a gap where its principal is unknown, with the nearest date chosen from all dated facilities. Tests in `figures.test.tsx`.

- F284 — Under `WHOLE_LINE` a quote of exactly one shown block was refused `CITATION_NOT_DELIVERED` when the gate had withheld the rest of its source line (R24-16): delivery is judged against the one block the match is, and `ANY_RUN`'s per-line check is unchanged. Test `test_a_page_map_showing_only_the_first_block_of_a_split_line_still_anchors_it`.

- F285 — A credential mint abandoned at `MINT_SECONDS` left its helper thread running, since the SDK's OAuth exchange has no timeout, and a new helper could start every `FAILURE_SECONDS` (R24-N01; owner accepted the cap): while an abandoned helper is alive, a new mint refuses `STORE_UNAVAILABLE` at once instead of starting another, one helper per identity at most. Test `test_an_abandoned_mint_does_not_start_a_second_helper`.

- F286 — `wire.MARKDOWN_CHARS` and `PREVIEW_CHARS` were sized from the vendor's 26 MB `MAX_FILE_BYTES`, promising more than any accepted handoff can hold (D45): they read `handoff.MAX_HANDOFF_BYTES`; `schema.json`, `documents.ts` and `commands.ts` follow.

- F287 — 86 comments and docstrings under `tests/` cited documents this repository does not hold (CF-101's remainder): three are repointed to their live analogue (the spec's §7, D4, D6, correcting two claims D6 superseded) and the rest dropped with the sentence kept true; the prompt goldens' baked bytes are untouched.

- F288 — `io_budget.py`'s ratchet skipped a per-pathway (dict-shaped) budget, so `reads/reports.py` and `reads/deliverable.py` sat outside it (N35): `--record` snapshots a dict key by key and `--assert` refuses a key below its floor, or a declaration that stopped being a dict. Tests `test_record_measurements_snapshots_a_dict_shaped_module_per_key`, `test_measured_problems_refuses_a_dict_key_fallen_below_its_floor`.

- F289 — Two more ways a PDF hides text were admitted unmarked (N27's remainder): text inside optional content the default configuration switches off (`optional_content_off`: `/D /OFF`, or `/BaseState /OFF` without `/D /ON`, an OCMD by its policy; every state a conforming viewer could disagree on is left unmarked), and text a later opaque, normally blended, axis-aligned rectangle fill covers whole (`painted_over`, compared exactly against the glyph, a rectangular clip intersected, strokes, patterns, images, curves and forms never counting). Bounded: one indirect reference, 4,096 groups, 32 OCMD members, nesting 256, 4,000,000 cover work units. `PdfExtractor` v5 and v6; migrations `0039` and `0040`; `PageLine.hidden` holds up to five reasons. A clip that is one rectangle is intersected rather than refused, so page-sized clips don't silence the reading. Tests in `tests/test_hidden_text.py`.

- F290 — A marked evidence line carried N27's note inline, so on a scan no line could be quoted as shown, and a text line could imitate the note (review 2: C1, W3, W4): the note is a `hidden` line in the host header over each run of a page's same-marked lines, the final check says a quote never includes host text, `gate_view` counts every host byte (`evidence_sizes`), no token holds a line break (`PdfExtractor` v7, `LINE_BREAKS` written as spaces) and stored blocks are shown as one line. Tests `test_a_scans_lines_are_quoted_exactly_as_the_model_is_shown_them`, `test_a_document_cannot_write_a_header_or_its_note`, `test_the_gate_bound_counts_every_host_byte_of_the_evidence_section`, `test_no_token_carries_a_line_break`.

- F291 — On PDF sources the whole-line rule compared quotes with the tracking-joined line, so a shown line with separate one-character tokens, or decomposed accents, could not be quoted as shown, and two lines shown alike were not ambiguous (review 2: W6, N13): answers are held to `whole-line-as-shown` (NFC words as the evidence section shows them, ambiguity counted on that form, then edges, then the tracked-letter line); records accepted under `whole-line` are re-anchored by that first reading. Tests `test_a_line_is_quoted_as_the_evidence_section_shows_it`, `test_two_lines_shown_alike_are_ambiguous_however_each_is_stored`.

- F292 — The second attempt relayed vendor research messages that quote the model's own answer text (review 2: N7): quoted values are withheld (`<value withheld>`), the field and the fault kept.

- F293 — A short text naming both `%PDF-` and `%%EOF` was sent to the PDF reader and refused (review 2: N8): bytes with a header past byte 0 that no PDF parse can read are admitted as plain text, and the qualification pre-spend check reads them the same way.

- F294 — Hidden-text size was measured on the vertical axis only, and Indexed and Separation colours were never compared (review 2: N9): a glyph is measured on its narrower axis (`Tz` included), Indexed tables over gray, RGB or CMYK and exponential Separation tints are read, and `cs`/`CS` set their space's initial colour as ISO 32000-1 requires (`PdfExtractor` v7).

- F295 — pdfminer left its device at a form XObject's matrix after `Do`, so text after a form was laid out, and its citation rectangle stored, where no viewer draws it (invariant 11): the figure's end restores the page matrix (`PdfExtractor` v7, `form_matrix`); earlier rows verify as recorded. Test `test_text_after_a_form_is_placed_where_a_viewer_draws_it`.

- F296 — The worker's statement bound could drop a paid call's bill when the case row lock was held long enough, so the next claim paid for the node again (review 1: W1; invariant 6): the bill's own unit lifts `statement_timeout` and `lock_timeout` for itself. Test `test_a_paid_call_s_bill_outwaits_a_held_case_lock_under_the_worker_s_bounds`.

- F297 — `caos_store` and `caos_graph` were used whoever owned them, so a co-tenant with CREATE could create either first and run triggers as the app's role (review 1: W2): `store.owned_schema` refuses `STORE_SCHEMA_DRIFT` unless the schema and everything in it belong to the connecting role, both boots check, and a Cancel forgets threads only in tables it owns.

- F298 — The run-digest thread key (F182) was spelled in three places and two regression tests still keyed the bare run id, so they passed with their bugs restored (review 1: W3): `work.checkpoint_thread` is the one spelling, and the rekeyed tests fail under their mutations.
