- F473 (2026-09-29) — CI failed `tests/test_run_commands.py::test_a_successor_run_links_a_blocked_run_of_its_case` ("the audit payload binds the predecessor") on PR 20 and passed on 16 to 18: order-dependent. `scripts/enterprise_deploy.py`'s `main` writes `CAOS_MODEL_ENDPOINT`, `CAOS_MODEL_PRICE` and `CAOS_MODEL_CHOICES` into `os.environ` for the scripts it calls, and `tests/test_enterprise_deploy.py` calls it in process; since D75 `create_run` reads those names (it pins the configured model and audits it), so a `create_run` later in the same xdist worker pinned a model and its audit digest differed from the test's. Before D75 nothing on that path read them, so the leak was harmless. Action: an autouse fixture in `tests/conftest.py` restores the three names after every test (restored, not cleared first: the live-provider suites read them from the caller's environment). Reproduced without xdist: `pytest -p no:xdist tests/test_enterprise_deploy.py::test_a_recorded_cli_step_is_one_row_with_the_log_tail tests/test_run_commands.py::test_a_successor_run_links_a_blocked_run_of_its_case` failed before the fix and passes after.

## GitHub Copilot as the model, 2026-10-01 (owner: "Update them so I may initialise the build")

- D77 (2026-10-01) — The enterprise workspace has AI Gateway disabled (B12), so the spec's §3.3 path, `ChatDatabricks` on a serving endpoint, cannot answer a call there. Lakebase, Unity Catalog volumes and compute remain available. GitHub Copilot, which the firm already approves, becomes the model, and CAOS stays the orchestrator. A model named `copilot:<model>[@<effort>]` is answered through the GitHub Copilot SDK, and `copilot-cli:<model>[@<effort>]` through the Copilot CLI as a fallback chosen by name, never automatically. Both sit behind the one factory (`caos.models.chat_model` returns `ChatCopilot`), so the run's model pin, the attempt ledger, budgets, the canonical envelope and anchored citations hold unchanged. The worker runs on the analyst's PC, where the Copilot seat is, against Lakebase and the volume. The build is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-01-copilot-sdk-adapter.md` (PR 23): the SDK transport lands as "D77, addendum" (its Task 2), the CLI fallback as D78, the worker on Windows as D79. Initialising it amended spec §2 and §3.3, CLAUDE.md's Layer 0, `docs/rebuild/ENTERPRISE_HANDOFF.md` (status note; "No other provider") and `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` (status note), and reissued the goal (D76, addendum). Alternatives: Copilot CLI as the orchestrator, with one skill per module and Delta over ODBC (run order, the exactly-once ledger, the envelope and citations would rest on instructions, not code; rejected); RAI, the firm's own gateway (needs use-case onboarding; a later provider behind the same factory); waiting for the gateway (no date given). Open: whether Databricks Apps may host the API and UI (the administrator's answer; the plan's N124), and Compliance's confirmation that issuer documents may be sent to Copilot models (owner).
- D76, addendum (2026-10-01) — `docs/rebuild/2026-09-22-goal.txt` now states the Copilot build (D77) instead of the enterprise deployment through the gateway, which cannot pass while AI Gateway is disabled (B12). It executes the plan inline on three stacked branches, holds every CLAUDE.md gate, and makes no paid or Copilot call unless its VALUES line authorises one. The default authorises none, so the plan's Task 1 spike and its Task 7 live run finish as the owner's steps on the analyst PC. It does not push or open a PR. The file keeps its name so the launcher still finds it; it uses 2,868 of the 4,000 characters (`wc -m`). The deployment goal is at `dfd64ad`.
- D77, addendum: dependency (2026-10-01) — `github-copilot-sdk==1.0.16` (MIT) is locked with the documentation, so the build starts from a lock `pip-audit` has cleared. Its dependencies, `python-dateutil`, `pydantic>=2.11` and `httpx>=0.24`, were already locked. No standard-library module or pinned dependency speaks the Copilot runtime's protocol. Evidence: `uv lock --check` clean; `uv run pip-audit --strict` → \"No known vulnerabilities found\" (after F474).
- D77, addendum 2 (2026-10-06, owner decision of 6 October 2026: Copilot replaces the AI Gateway as the production model transport; re-specify the adapter now and build it against fakes; revised the same day after an adversarial review) — The plan's Task 1 spike ran on the owner's personal seat (`docs/rebuild/runs/copilot-spike-2026-10-06/SUMMARY.md`, git-ignored) and stopped at its gating risks: the SDK in empty mode authenticated with the GitHub CLI's token (`authType` `gh-cli`, no Copilot entitlement, 0 models listed) and the CLI did the same under a private `COPILOT_HOME`; the seat offers one model, `mai-code-1.1-flash`, routed `auto_v2` when no `--model` is sent; the CLI's JSON lines carry no `assistant.usage`, and billing arrives as AI units on `session.usage_checkpoint` (`totalNanoAiu` 251,164,000 and `totalPremiumRequests` 1 for one tiny prompt); and every `assistant.message` carries `serverTools: {"provider": "openai-responses"}`. The adapter is re-specified against the SDK's own types (`github-copilot-sdk` 1.0.16, which runs runtime 1.0.90 beside the IT-installed CLI 1.0.92) in the plan's section "Re-spec of 2026-10-06" (R1–R7), which supersedes Design 3–7 and 10 and restates Tasks 2–7; it is built against fakes every one of which the SDK's own `from_dict` accepts, and no live call is made until the firm-seat spike (N177). **Mapping (R1):** an allow-list of event types, any other refusing the finish reason and keeping the bill; the proof that the pinned model answered is `assistant.message.data.model`, exactly one `model.call_final_result` (`model`, `result == "success"`, `isByok` stated `false`) and, when a usage event is present, `assistant.usage.model` and the CAPI witness `copilotUsage.model`, all equal to the pin, with no `fusion` attribution anywhere; `session.auto_mode_resolved` present at all refuses; any `serverTools` refuses until the firm-seat spike shows a bare provider tag harmless per model family; a `turn_retry` or a `model.call_failure` followed by the one successful final result is the runtime's own recovery inside one logical operation and is admitted, billed once; with no usage event `stop` is derived from `assistant.turn_end` and an unaborted `session.idle`. **Charge (R2):** the reservation stays the per-token `priced_request` at the operator's pinned `ModelPrice` (D29 admission and ceiling unchanged), now with the output cap a transport can return (65,536 on the SDK; a pinned `CLI_OUTPUT_TOKENS` entry for a `copilot-cli:` model, which the CLI caps at the model's own limit); readiness refuses a pin below the listed `tokenPrices` (every cache-write tier, both context tiers) times the multiplier; the settled charge is `nano_aiu x credit_price / 10**9` in `Decimal` under `exact_context()`, where `nano_aiu` is the session's last `session.usage_checkpoint.totalNanoAiu` as a whole number above zero (the 10**9 ratio, read from the SDK's `creditsUsedNanoAiu` doc, is an assumption N177 measures) and `credit_price` is the dated pin `CAOS_COPILOT_CREDIT_PRICE=<usd>,<date>`, stored on the reservation by migration `0048_reservation_credit_price` (0047 is D118's, F584) so a redeploy cannot change a live run's charge (F468 for credits); no checkpoint, or zero on an answered call, is an unknown charge (billed unknown, refused, reservation kept); a settled charge above the reservation is recorded in full in the ledger, refused `BUDGET_CHARGE_OVER_RESERVATION` (new code) with one stderr line, and parks the run terminally for its pin (a requeue would overshoot the same way: the operator re-prices and starts a successor run); `session_limits.max_ai_credits` (30-credit floor, soft) is a guard and not a bound; a failed call that carries spend (`nano_aiu > 0`) or any `assistant.message` is billed and refused, never raised, so it is never a `drop_kind` and never re-attempted (D110; 0046's `call_outcomes_drop_unanswered` holds), and a runtime-recovered answer is paid once. **Auth and isolation (R3):** from the SDK source, `github_token` takes priority (`--auth-token-env COPILOT_SDK_AUTH_TOKEN`), `use_logged_in_user=False` adds `--no-auto-login`, `base_directory` is `COPILOT_HOME`, mode `empty` sets `COPILOT_DISABLE_KEYTAR=1`; inside the runtime `COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN` precede stored credentials, then auto-login runs `gh` (`gh-cli`). Both transports run under one environment allow-list (`child_environment`: `COPILOT_HOME`, `COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN` copied unread, `COPILOT_DISABLE_KEYTAR=1`, `COPILOT_AUTO_UPDATE=false`, `NO_COLOR`, a `PATH` of the executable's own directory so `gh` is unfindable, the platform's process variables and the firm's proxy) that excludes by name `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, every askpass variable, `COPILOT_PROVIDER_*` (BYOK), `COPILOT_ALLOW_ALL`, `OTEL_*`, `GH_HOST`, `COPILOT_GH_HOST`, `COPILOT_CLI_PATH` (an IT runtime is named by `CAOS_COPILOT_RUNTIME`) and the prompt-mode opt-ins; the SDK session disables the built-in MCP servers, skills, hooks, plugins, custom instructions and telemetry and denies every permission request; the CLI gets `--no-auto-login`, `--disable-builtin-mcps`, `--no-ask-user`, `--disallow-temp-dir` and deny rules for `shell`, `write` and `url`. Readiness requires `authType == "env"` and the pinned host, refuses `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` when the seat offers none of the approved Copilot models (a run pinned to an unoffered one parks, F468), and proves the CLI's cage with a spend-free negative probe (the CLI without the token must answer nothing). **Context (R4):** each offered model's window is declared once at readiness from the listing into `caos.copilot.CONTEXT_LISTED`, consulted by `request_ceiling` ahead of a pinned `CONTEXT_TOKENS` entry (the only source for `copilot-cli:` names). **Stderr (R7):** the SDK logs the runtime's stderr at WARNING (`copilot._jsonrpc`), so the `copilot` logger is given a `NullHandler` and no propagation before the first client starts. Alternatives: bill in tokens at the pinned price and ignore the AI units (the tokens are not the bill on either transport, and the CLI states none); keep `gh-cli` auth (no Copilot entitlement); pass the token to the SDK as `github_token` from code (reads a credential the plan said no code reads; the runtime reads the same variable itself); a deny-list environment (revision 1; an unlisted variable such as `COPILOT_PROVIDER_BASE_URL` or `GITHUB_ASKPASS` would pass); admit the bare `serverTools` provider tag now (its value appears nowhere in the SDK); re-attempt a failed call that carried spend (pays twice, D110); let a requeue retry the overshooting pin. **Owner decisions of 2026-10-06 on the review's open points:** (a) the SDK transport only, now: `copilot-cli:` stays designed and unbuilt (Copilot 2/3 deferred, not dropped, until the firm's PC shows the SDK cannot be installed; an SDK-only build refuses a `copilot-cli:` name at readiness); (b) any answer carrying `serverTools` is billed and refused until N177 decides per model family; (c) migration 0048 (F584), `BUDGET_CHARGE_OVER_RESERVATION` and the terminal park with a successor run are accepted. The build order for the SDK-only scope is the plan's R8 (Tasks 2, 3, 5, 6, 7). Evidence: the plan's R1–R8; the tests named there, written when the tasks are built.
- F474 (2026-10-01) — `uv run pip-audit --strict` failed on the base lock: `pyjwt 2.14.0 GHSA-42vr-xj54-vc7v`, fixed in 2.15.0. `pyjwt` arrives through `mcp[crypto]`, `databricks-mcp` and `databricks-langchain`, not through the SDK; the advisory is new, so every PR's security job would have failed. Action: `uv lock --upgrade-package pyjwt` (2.14.0 → 2.15.1). Evidence: `uv run pip-audit --strict` → "No known vulnerabilities found".
- F475 (2026-10-02) — Answer keys checked as whole evidence lines. F235 made an accepted citation one whole evidence line (`WHOLE_LINE`), and the matrix scores a key by exact equality with an accepted quote (`caos/qualification/matrix.py` `_matches`), so a key that is a fragment of a line, or runs onto the next, can never be met. `tests/test_qualification_on_disk.py` checked only that a key was a unique run, which let thirteen such keys into the CZR sets (`czr-2026q2-earnings-update`, `czr-2026q2-liquidity`, `czr-2026q2-covenant-refinancing`, `czr-2026q2-lite-covenant-refinancing`). Action: each is restated as its whole source line, unique in its document; the committed-key test now also requires every key's words to equal exactly one evidence line of the document as admission writes it (each token line cut into its `token_groups` blocks). Every older committed set already passed. Evidence: the test fails on the previous keys and passes on the restated ones; the four set digests move. The assertion compares the exact string, as the matrix does.

## The synthetic test mandate, 2026-10-02 (owner decision of 2 October 2026)

- D80 (2026-10-02) — The CZR LITE portfolio set (`qualification/czr-2026q2-lite-portfolio/`) needs portfolio context, and no real portfolio is available: on `ccl-fy2025-portfolio` CP-0 refused CP-L10 for want of holdings and exposure, a mandate and limits, an eligible-security universe, current security-market evidence and the applicable executed governing security documents. The owner decided on 2 October 2026 to adopt the synthetic "Test CLO I" mandate from `REF_CP-6A_Portfolio_Debate_Inputs.xlsx`, adapted to carry a CZR position. It is admitted as owner-adopted test input, `TEST_CLO_I_Mandate_and_Exposures_2026-10-02.txt` (text SHA-256 `864ee90ab21372bc…`, raw workbook `05a32699b39cedce…`), labelled SYNTHETIC everywhere: in its own first lines, its register row's document and note, the set's `RESULT.md` and `DOCUMENTS.md`. It may never ground a real decision: no figure in it is a real holding, and a run over it qualifies the route's handling of a mandate, not a position. The register row `test-clo-i-mandate-2026-10-02` has `status: in_hand` and a note naming it synthetic; its `source` is a provenance string naming the workbook, not a URL. This does not contradict `ccl-portfolio-mandate-exposures`, which calls the same workbook "a methodology reference, not live portfolio evidence": it still is not live evidence, and that row stays `to_source`; D80 admits it only as labelled test input. The set also carries the notes' indenture as their governing document; no eligible-security universe exists in the workbook beyond its lien buckets (C-09, C-10), and none is invented. The adaptation proposes USD 5,000,000 par of the CZR 6.50% 2032 notes (CUSIP 12769GAC4) against the workbook's 29 May 2026 NAV of USD 630,928,502, 0.7925% of NAV under C-01's 2.5% single-name limit; the price is FINRA's real 1 October 2026 last trade. Alternatives: leave the set as an owner-gap refusal like `ccl-fy2025-portfolio` (it would measure the refusal again, not the ready path with a mandate); wait for a real portfolio extract (`ccl-portfolio-mandate-exposures` stays `to_source`; no date).

## Live-run fixes, 2026-10-02 (owner decision of 2 October 2026: "Apply your recommendation to all. Run only one module at a time, and patch the faults before moving on to the next.")

- F476 (2026-10-02) — Markdown emphasis around a body quote. The 2 October live runs wrote a cited line as `**For the period 2026**.`; the body-quote check carried the same line in quotation marks or a backtick (F148, F149) but refused it in emphasis, a host defect recorded as the model's answer. Action: `*` and `_` join `_QUOTATION` in `caos/methodology/handoff.py`, so `*`, `**`, `_` and `__` are edge typography exactly as the quotation marks are (unpaired and mismatched markers are accepted as unbalanced quote marks already are); only the two outer tokens may wear them. Evidence: `test_a_quote_the_body_wraps_in_markdown_emphasis_is_still_quoted` carries each spelling and refuses the same line with an inner word changed. Nothing widens what may be cited; `verify_citations` is untouched.
- D81 (2026-10-02) — Evidence presentation the model can quote from (option 1 of the 2 October root-cause analysis, adopted by the owner's decision above). Of the 41 citations refused on the 2 October live runs, 19 were one whole sentence taken from a longer evidence line (lines of 167 to 909 characters) and 17 named the wrong page, 14 of them off by exactly one: the model was never told what "one evidence line" is, and the `page:` header was printed once per run of up to 60 lines (a `.txt` page), often kilobytes above the line it covered. Decision: (1) `_evidence_section` repeats the run's header, unchanged (source_id, page and any `hidden` note), before every eighth line of a run counted from the run's first line (`caos/methodology/invocation.py` `_HEADER_EVERY`, `_evidence_blocks`), so a line's page is never more than seven lines up; `evidence_sizes` still counts every host byte (W3), a line's size now a function of its position in its run, and since positions count from the run's start the sizes of any leading lines of each page are the pin's own, which `selection.gate_view` cuts by. (2) `icm/shared/prompt/final_check.md` defines an evidence line once: all the text between two blank lines in the EVIDENCE section, a whole paragraph or a whole table row; `matched_text` copies the entire line, any leading bullet or footnote marker and any trailing `|` included, never only a sentence of it; `page` is the page in the nearest evidence header above the line; a body quote keeps the source's own wording ("we", "our", "us"), never rephrased into the third person. Alternatives: accept a whole sentence of a line as a citation (option 5; not applied, revisit only if the live loop still shows fragments); a header on every line (about eight times the cost below). Cost, measured offline with the host's own extraction (`prepare_pack`), `gate_view`, `_evidence_section` and JSON escaping over the committed CZR sets: +63,281 evidence-section bytes on `czr-2026q2-covenant-refinancing` (2,440,779 to 2,504,060), the largest +69,853 on `czr-2026q2-full-credit-assessment` (2,541,436 to 2,611,289); those deltas are measured, while the whole request for the largest set, about 2.8 MB against `MAX_REQUEST_BYTES` (4,194,304), is an estimate, not a measurement. Evidence: `tests/test_evidence_selection.py::test_the_header_is_repeated_every_eight_lines_of_a_run`, `::test_each_lines_size_is_its_share_of_the_evidence_section`, `tests/test_handoff_invocation.py` (the final check's wording).
- F477 (2026-10-02) — D81 is a deliberate departure from the legacy `prompt` parity group: the final check's citation sentence changed in all 27 cases (+311 bytes each); no fixture run is longer than eight lines, so the header repeat moves no golden. Only the `prompt` group was regenerated, from the legacy snapshot's own code with the change mirrored (D34's procedure; `tests/parity/goldens.py` refuses a manifest written from `caos`, F57): a scratch clone of the snapshot at `b5a3932f` with `vendor/deploy-v` replaced by the fork, its prompt constants read from `icm/shared/prompt/`, and the host's source-preparation text, citation-register header, withheld-workbook note (D38), final-check arguments and RELATIVE_VALUE/LITE fixtures mirrored; that copy reproduced every committed `prompt` golden byte for byte before D81 was applied to it (the final-check block and the eight-line repeat in its `_evidence_section`), then `tests/parity/generate_goldens.py --package server --root <copy> --out tests/parity/golden --group prompt`. The 27 regenerated cases equal what `caos` renders. Host pins: `icm/HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` and `caos/methodology/host_pin.py` regenerated by `scripts/host_manifest.py`.
- D82 (2026-10-02) — Retry feedback that says what was wrong, and two guided retries per node (option 2 of the 2 October root-cause analysis, adopted by the owner's decision above; it amends N52's "never by text" and D30's one second attempt). Evidence: of the 41 citations refused on the 2 October live runs, 19 were a sentence of a longer evidence line and 17 named the wrong page, yet the retry was told only "citation N is not one evidence line of its cited page"; 4 of the 6 second attempts broke a different rule than the first, which with one retry stopped the node; and 7 of the 13 handoff refusals carried no guidance at all. Decision: (1) a citation refused `CITATION_NOT_LOCATED` is placed by the host's own anchoring code (`caos/evidence/citations.py` `find_line`, `LineFinding`): `_page_run` (`ANY_RUN`) on the cited page, and a unique run inside one longer delivered shown line names that line; failing that, `verify_citations` under `WHOLE_LINE` on each other page of its source the node was given names those pages; a quote with no `ANY_RUN` run inside the delivered lines of any delivered page is absent; anything else (twice on a page, across two lines, part of a line of another page) is not placed and keeps the rule's wording -- never a guess. Only pages delivered to the node are read: a cited page it was not given is never probed, so what that page holds cannot change the line. The search is help, not a verdict: any refusal from it (`EVIDENCE_PACKING_MISMATCH`, say) leaves the citation unplaced rather than costing the retry, and only the first `MAX_FEEDBACK_CITATIONS` unlocated citations are searched. `handoff.anchoring_line` (with `LineHint`) then says, per citation and for at most `MAX_FEEDBACK_CITATIONS` of them: `citation N of T is part of a longer evidence line of its cited page, which begins "<first 12 words>"; quote the whole line`, the words (`HINT_WORDS`) the delivered block's own, whitespace-split and joined by one space, read from what the node was delivered (`canonical._line_hint`); `citation N of T is one whole evidence line of page P, not of its cited page`; or `citation N of T is in no evidence line of its source this node was given`. Ambiguous and not-delivered keep their wording. The line is held to `MAX_ANCHORING_CHARS` (four vendor lines' room, 2,048 characters), dropping placements from the last back, each citation keeping its number under the rule's wording, so a retry's prompt grows by a bounded amount. The search is scoped to the cited source: a quote of another delivered source is told absent from its own, which is true. These words reach only that one prompt, never a log, refusal or row, like every feedback line (D30). (2) `canonical._feedback_source` gives a node's next attempt feedback when its latest attempt was refused with a `SECOND_ATTEMPT_CODES` code and the node holds at most `GUIDED_RETRIES` (2) such refusals, so its 2nd and 3rd attempts are each told of the attempt just before them and every later attempt is ordinary; still read from the ledger, so a crash between a refusal and its retry gives the same lines, and the prospective (priced) prompt and the attempt's rebuilt one count the same attempts. `runtime._with_second_attempt` loops at most `1 + GUIDED_RETRIES` passes, the ledger deciding each retry; a retry the ceiling cannot pay for leaves the refusal before it standing. No prompt golden carries feedback (none has a `SECOND ATTEMPT` section), so no golden moved. The host block `icm/shared/prompt/validator_feedback.md` now reads "This is a retry of this same request", true of both retries (its marker names are unchanged; no golden carries the block); `icm/HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` and `caos/methodology/host_pin.py` regenerated by `scripts/host_manifest.py`, and the 27 stage contracts by `scripts/icm_stages.py --write` from `caos/icm.py`'s condition. Tests that counted one retry now count two (`tests/test_execution_billing.py`, `test_canonical_runtime.py`, `test_lite_cp1c_contract.py`, `test_lite_route_e2e_negative.py`, `test_lite_full_credit_screen_route.py`, `test_loop_charges.py`, `test_runtime.py`, `test_worker.py`). Evidence: `tests/test_whole_line_citations.py::test_find_line_places_a_quote_the_whole_line_rule_refused`, `::test_a_placed_line_is_shown_by_its_first_words_as_delivered`, `::test_a_search_that_cannot_read_a_page_leaves_the_citation_unplaced`; `tests/test_second_attempt.py::test_the_anchoring_line_places_each_unlocated_citation_it_can`, `::test_an_unanchored_citation_gets_the_second_attempt_naming_it` (case b, end to end), `::test_part_of_a_line_gets_the_second_attempt_naming_it` (case a), `::test_a_second_refusal_gets_a_retry_told_of_the_attempt_before_it`, `::test_a_third_refusal_stops_the_run_with_no_fourth_attempt`, `::test_two_guided_retries_per_node_whichever_codes_refused`, `::test_the_third_attempt_survives_a_crash_after_the_second_refusal` (the resumed 3rd prompt equals, byte for byte less each run's own identity, a sibling run's that never crashed; prospective and rebuilt request sizes equal).
- D83 (2026-10-02) — The call deadline and the upstream-section bound (option 3 of the 2 October root-cause analysis, adopted by the owner's decision above; it amends F89's 240 s, brief D5's "a lease outlives two call deadlines", F110's 52 KiB and D29's default run ceiling). Evidence: `complete` holds one deadline over the whole call (ST-9) and `_invoked` abandons the call at it, recorded `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`; default-tier `gpt-6-luna-pro` calls took 131–229 s and 5 of 19 ran past 240 s, priority-tier 84–175 s with none past it, and the 65,536-token reservation at about 155 tokens a second is about 420 s. CP-1's accepted answers ran to 50,489–85,559 bytes on the CZR sets (9 of 11 over 53,248) and 55,474–76,186 on CCL of 23 September (6 of 6 over), so the next node refused `UPSTREAM_SECTION_OVER_CEILING`. Decision: (1) `caos.provider.TIMEOUT_SECONDS` is 420 s; `MIN_RESEND_SECONDS` stays half of it (210 s), `WORKER_STALE_AFTER` stays the deadline plus a minute (480 s), and E10's `MODEL_CALL_SECONDS` in `scripts/enterprise_deploy.py` waits 440 s; the test-only OpenRouter adapter and `chat_model` carry the same constant. (2) The lease is unchanged. `reserve` is a fenced write that renews the lease to 600 s immediately before the call unit (`LEASE_SECONDS`); the call ends by 420 s, leaving 180 s for the bill (unfenced, under `call_hold`), the answer's checks and the fenced `accept_attempt` (F485: a liveness budget, shared with the work before the call too, not a safety invariant), so the rule asserted in `tests/test_worker.py` and `tests/test_models.py` is now `LEASE_SECONDS - TIMEOUT_SECONDS >= 180` in place of D5's two deadlines, which 840 s would break. A lease lost past that point still pays nothing twice: the bill has committed, `CALL_HOLD_SECONDS` (1,200 s past the reservation) keeps a second worker off the node while the call may still bill, and `replay_billed` settles the charged answer. Each D82 guided retry is its own attempt and reservation, so it renews the lease again. (3) `MAX_UPSTREAM_HANDOFF_BYTES` is 98,304 (96 KiB). The widest node is CP-5 of FULL_CREDIT_32, 16 direct upstreams: at the bound beside its 169,493 bytes of delivered authority it encodes to 1,750,342 bytes, leaving 2,443,962 of the 4 MiB request for evidence and for the upstream citation register, which that figure excludes and which is unbounded (F486); the widest-node test in `tests/test_handoff_invocation.py` now asserts the 16-upstream node was measured, that it fits with at least half the request left, and that the largest observed CP-1 answer fits with 8 KiB of room, and its docstring names CP-5, not CP-3. (4) The bundle's default `run_ceiling` is 120.00 (owner, 3 October 2026), amending D29's 100.00: at the default price `databricks-claude-opus-5,0.000005,0.000025,2026-09-22` the widest profile at the section bounds costs 96.122820 and one worst-case call 22.609920, 118.732740 ≤ 120.00, which `tests/test_run_ceiling.py` asserts. Alternatives: streaming with lease renewal (N14, still unmeasured on the workspace); the priority tier alone (a run configuration, not a host bound); restructuring CP-1's duplicate tables in the bundle fork (not recommended in option 4). No parity golden encodes either number.
- F478 (2026-10-03) — The CZR conversions carried layout as text. The staging converter (`scratchpad/edgar/convert.py`, outside the repo) ended every table row with ` |`, kept a list bullet glued to its first word (`•Consolidated`), and glued a footnote marker set in its own padded span to its word (`(a)The`), so a model quoting the line had to reproduce a separator, a glyph or a missing space the page does not show. Action: a row now ends at its last cell; a leading `•`, `◦` or `▪` is dropped; one space follows a leading `(a)`/`(1)`-style marker glued to a word (not `(a)(i)`, not a figure such as `(2)%`). All eleven EDGAR conversions were re-run (3,038 row terminators, 177 bullets, 85 markers); the TRACE observations and the synthetic mandate (D80) are not conversions and keep their bytes, so the mandate's keys keep their row terminator. Every copy in the eleven `qualification/czr-2026q2*` sets is replaced byte for byte; 19 distinct keys (62 key rows) whose source line changed are restated as the new whole line (F475), same words and figures; register notes, `RESULT.md` provenance hashes and set digests, `COMMITTED_SET_DIGESTS` and `DOCUMENTS.md` move with them. No size crossed 500 KiB, so the large-file pins stand. Evidence: the converter run twice gives identical SHA-256s; each conversion's non-whitespace character stream (less `|`) equals the HTML's visible text less the dropped bullets, and every changed line is explained by the three edits alone; `tests/test_qualification_on_disk.py` passes on the restated keys.
- F479 (2026-10-03) — The test-only OpenRouter adapter sends a reasoning effort and pins a provider. The owner asked to probe cheaper models (`openai/gpt-6-luna` at effort high, `openai/gpt-6-luna-pro` on the `openai/flex` tier, `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash` at low on a named host), and the adapter sent neither. Action: `tests/openrouter_adapter.py` reads `OPENROUTER_REASONING_EFFORT` (`minimal|low|medium|high|xhigh`, anything else a `RuntimeError`) and `OPENROUTER_PROVIDER` (comma-separated provider names or endpoint tags) at call time and sends `reasoning: {"effort": ...}` and `provider: {"order": [...], "allow_fallbacks": false}` through the `ChatOpenAI` `extra_body`; unset, the request is unchanged. The identity names what ran (AR-15) as one plain string: `openrouter/<model>/<effort|none>/<max tokens>`, or `openrouter/<model>@<order>/<effort|none>/<max tokens>` with a pin, so `--expect-identity` stays a string compare. Production is untouched: `CAOS_REASONING_EFFORT` stays refused (N2). Evidence: `tests/test_openrouter_adapter.py`.
- D84 (2026-10-03, owner-authorised: option 4 of 2 October, "you are authorised to edit vendor files to improve performance and reliability" of 3 October) — Vendor fork r5, the two clarity fixes (build `3715187a`, manifest `717915f3`; D31's procedure). **CP-1's audit sections:** `REF_CP-1_STEPS.md` Step 12 item 5 said "the single Audit Appendix ... holds ALL audit items as sub-sections", while `validate_handoff.py` refuses anything but the six H2s once each ("H2 headings must be exactly once and in canonical order: Audit Summary -> Analysis -> Evidence Trace -> Source Registry -> Gaps & Conflicts -> QA Validation"); N3's CP-1 attempts 3 and 4 wrote Evidence Trace and Source Registry as `####` and were refused `HANDOFF_MALFORMED`. The item now says those four audit sections are canonical H2s, quotes the validator's rule, says a `###` or `####` one in place of the H2 fails (the validator checks only H2s), keeps the registers they draw on (T4.1, T4.11, T4.12, T4.13) in the analytical appendix, and puts limitation flags in the front matter; it no longer names Source Gate / Readiness or Limitation Flags as appendix registers, neither being a T4 register (Task 5 review, M2, M3). **Register binding:** `completeness_check.find_registers` binds a table to a heading only among the last four non-blank lines above it (`recent[-4:]`), which no module text stated; N4's CP-0 attempt 5 put five blockquote lines between `#### T6 — Evidence Trace` and its table and was refused `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE` (`T6: required register missing`). The canon's analytical-appendix rules (`CANON_SHARED.md` § TABLE_DESIGN_STANDARDS, delivered to every module whose `SKILL.md` names the canon, which is every module with registers) now state it: the heading or caption is one of the four non-blank lines directly above the header row, every non-blank line counts (prose, blockquote, a `table-id` comment), notes go below the table. No checker changed. - D88 (2026-10-03, Task 5b vendor review V2, owner-authorised under the same day's authorisation to edit vendor files; presentation only) — Vendor fork r6: the tagged-table readers tolerate what well-meaning answers wrote and refuse what they refused before. **Findings:** `cp_tables.parse_tables` raised for the whole document on one bad row, which `completeness_check.check` turned into every unconditional interface table "missing" (P1 CP-1 #1: one 14-cell row of the 15-column period register, ten lines of which eight were consequences; N3 CP-1 #2 nine lines); any line but a blank or comment between a tag and its table broke the bind silently, a `####` heading included (P1 CP-1 #3's readiness tag above `#### CP-MODEL Readiness`); a separator cell needed three hyphens; a row split at an escaped `\|`. **Change** (`skills/cp-0-source-readiness/scripts/cp_tables.py`, synced to its 22 copies, and CP-MODEL's `validate_cp_model_inputs.py`): `read_tables` returns the tables and, per malformed tagged table, its first fault -- a short or long row as "row N (first cell `…`) has K cells, header has M -- table row width differs from its header", plus how many more rows differ -- and reads every other table; `parse_tables` is `read_tables` refusing the whole document on any fault, as before, so the host's `handoff_tables` still serves no partial set (`TABLES_MALFORMED`) and the host's feedback ordering (`handoff._consequence`, which keys on "differs from its header") is unchanged; `completeness_check.check` appends each table's fault and reports "interface table missing" only for a table with no fault. A tag crosses heading lines (`###` to `######`) to its table, but only as a tolerance: a tag bound across a heading binds only a well-formed table and only an id no tag binds the old way and no second tag crosses to, and is otherwise dropped as before; prose, an H1 or H2, or another tag between still breaks the bind. A separator cell is one or more hyphens, optionally colon-aligned. A body row whose split at every `|` is not its header's width is split at the unescaped pipes, each `\|` read as `|`; a row that split to its header's width is read as before. CP-MODEL's `parse_stable_tables` skips heading lines after a marker as it skips blank lines, takes the same separator cells and splits a row the same way, so the readers still agree on every table CP-1's check accepts (a comment between tag and table is still refused there, D85). **Guarantee:** everything either reader accepted before is accepted and read the same (one exception, fix round 1's C2 below: a register row whose escaped `\|` had hidden a critical placeholder), held against fork r5's reader over 6,000 random documents; a malformed tagged table is still refused by the check, by `parse_tables` and by CP-MODEL. Replay of the review's answers under the new checkers: P1 CP-1 #1 10 to 4 lines, N3 CP-1 #2 9 to 3 (the third is a duplicate `cp1.cp_model_segment_allocation` tag, still refused), P1 CP-1 #3 3 to 2, N2 CP-1 #1 5 to 4, every other case unchanged. CP-MODEL's `parse_stable_tables` takes the same crossing conditions. Agreement: over 20,000 random documents, each table the r6 reader newly binds across a heading is the table its tag was written for, every newly bound id is tagged once, CP-MODEL reads it alike and the register locator reads the same cells; over the reviewer's 200,000-document fuzz no document both readers accept is read differently, and every remaining divergence is CP-MODEL refusing a document in which `cp_tables`, as in r5, drops a tag silently (before prose, or crossed into a malformed table) and which a newly tolerated separator or row lets it accept. Unchanged from r5: a tag directly above the next table binds it, whichever table the writer meant. Cost: a tag directly below an untagged table, with no heading between, crosses nothing, so a readiness tag written that way is not tolerated across a heading; P1 CP-1 #3's is (the table above it was bound by a tag that crossed), and the replay counts are unchanged. - D94 (2026-10-03, Task 5b vendor review V14, owner decision of 3 October: "apply all") — A whole evidence line cited on the wrong page is anchored at its true page. The Task 5b review counted ten refusals among the 36 answers refused on 2 and 3 October that a line unique within its source's delivered evidence would have cleared: a line the node was given, copied whole, cited under the wrong page. Decision: under `WHOLE_LINE` (the rule an answer is accepted under), when a quote is no line of its cited page (`CITATION_NOT_LOCATED`), `verify_citations` searches every other page of its source that holds a block the node was given (`_delivered_pages`, one read per distinct delivery and only on this path; an undelivered page is never read) with the same whole-line search (`_shown_line_run`, ambiguity counted over each whole page); exactly one matching line across them, and that line delivered, anchors the citation there (`_true_page`). Never a tolerance: the stored `page` and rectangles are the true page's, so every anchored citation is still one whole evidence line of the page it names (invariant 11), and the body-quote check is unchanged. The page the module named is recorded beside it as `AnchoredCitation.cited_page` (None where the quote is where it was cited): written into the canonical record only when set (`record_bytes`), so every record stored before reads back byte for byte, and a stored `cited_page` that is null, not a page or the page itself refuses (`_anchored`). It is served on the Analysis and Book `CitationView` (`cited_page`, null otherwise; wire schema, frontend shape and fixtures updated) and travels with the record wherever the record goes (Report, deliverable). Two matching lines (on one page or two), a match on a line the node was not given, a page it was not given, and part of a line still refuse `CITATION_NOT_LOCATED`, as before, so the D82 guided retry and its `find_line` hint are unchanged for them (`find_line` asks each page at that page alone, never re-anchoring). Every reader reaches the live verdict, each by its own check: `replay_billed` rebuilds the node's own delivery (its selection, or `gate_view` for CP-0) and re-runs `_answer` on the stored response, so it repeats the whole search; the proof and the deliverable only hold the run's captured blocks, which may carry copies on pages the node was never given, so they never search (fix round 1, C1): `verify_stored_citations` locates each citation at the page the record stores, by the record's rule, within the captured blocks, and keeps its `cited_page` only when that page holds no such line -- a record claiming any other re-anchoring no longer equals the record (`CITATION_MOVED`), and one storing a page the quote is not on refuses with the citation's own code (`CITATION_NOT_LOCATED`), as before D94. The uniqueness among the node's pages is judged at acceptance and at `replay_billed`, not re-judged by those two readers. Placing a quote for a guided retry (`find_line`) never re-anchors: one search per delivered page, as before (fix round 1, I1; 360 delivered pages, one quote on none: 0.08 s, where re-anchoring inside the placement took 3.45 s). The search reads the other delivered pages in one query (`TokenIndex.read_pages`), so a quote found on no line of its cited page costs two round trips more at acceptance (its delivered pages, then their tokens) however many pages were delivered (M1). Only `WHOLE_LINE` re-anchors: records accepted under `ANY_RUN` or `WHOLE_LINE_AS_STORED` are located as they always were, and no record accepted under `WHOLE_LINE` changes (each anchored where it was cited, which is tried first). No migration: citations live in the record blob. Evidence: `tests/test_whole_line_citations.py::test_a_whole_line_cited_on_the_wrong_page_is_anchored_at_its_true_page` (true page and rectangles, `cited_page`; two pages, an undelivered line, an undelivered page never read, a fragment and the older rules refused), `::test_find_line_places_a_quote_the_whole_line_rule_refused`, `tests/test_second_attempt.py::test_a_wrong_page_citation_is_anchored_at_its_true_page` (end to end: accepted on the first attempt, the record's page and boxes the true page's), `tests/test_handoff_record.py::test_a_record_keeps_the_page_a_reanchored_citation_was_cited_on`, `tests/test_verification.py::test_refuse_maps_each_step_to_the_callers_code_or_lets_it_through`, `tests/test_analysis_section.py::test_a_reanchored_citation_is_served_with_the_page_it_was_cited_on`; fix round 1: `tests/test_reanchor_readers.py::test_live_replay_proof_and_deliverable_agree_on_a_reanchored_citation` (a node given pages 1-2 of a source whose page 3 repeats the line: live, `replay_billed`, the proof and the deliverable's reader agree), `tests/test_whole_line_citations.py::test_a_stored_citation_is_checked_where_it_is_stored`, `::test_placing_a_quote_reads_each_page_once_and_never_re_anchors`, `tests/test_handoff_record.py::test_a_record_keeps_the_page_a_reanchored_citation_was_cited_on` (a `cited_page` under any rule but `WHOLE_LINE` refuses, M2). The drawers say "Cited p.N, found p.M" for such a citation (`frontend/src/evidence/Reanchored.tsx`, M3; `evidence.test.tsx`, `evidence-page.test.tsx`).

The lines each block alone held move to that phase's "### CP-Lxx binding rules" list: its `table_inventory` (`TLxx.1`-`TLxx.4`), `permitted_front_table` (the screen's own name), `reader_question`, `required_decision_drivers` and `required_risk_catalyst_trigger_fields`. Everything else in a block repeated the profile (appendix contract, disqualifiers, evidence markers, payload fields, evidence classes, H2 sections, shared presentation rules), the phase's binding rules (`topic_ids` are its `canonical_topics`), its output contract (the screen's section heading), its authority prohibitions (`prohibited_conclusions`) or the companions list (the schema paths). The absorbed-phase intros now say the rules are in the profile with the registers. **Measured first**: the twelve rules, parsed by the checker's own tree reader from the old blocks, pass on all six stored CP-L10 answers (the N5 attempts of 2 October, six distinct bodies: every `TLxx.2` holds its six topics once and every `TLxx.3` its `OVERALL` row), so the rules are no stricter than what the model already writes, and the SKILL text requires the same ("Assess every topic exactly once"; a screen's posture is its `OVERALL` row's `assessment`). **Replay** of the 144 stored answers (107 CP-0, 21 CP-1, 6 CP-L10, 5 CP-3D, 3 CP-1A, 2 CP-5) through `validate_handoff`, the completeness check and, for CP-1, CP-MODEL's validator: identical before and after; no answer is newly refused. `SKILL.md` falls from 70,418 to 54,022 bytes. **Re-pin:** `verify_package.py --refresh` (95 vendor tests, 2 integration skips, PASS; no authority component moved, so no `--rebuild-authorities`), `scripts/host_manifest.py`, the build and manifest pins; `MEASURED` CP-L10 154,317 to 137,921 bytes (-16,396, about 4,100 tokens of every CP-L10 request), CP-0 and CP-5 unchanged. Parity, by D34's procedure (the legacy snapshot with the fork synced and the host's text mirrored, verified first to reproduce all 27 `prompt` goldens and the 16 `forecast` goldens byte for byte): `lite__CP-L10` shrinks 16,388 characters (the bytes less four em dashes) and `lite__CP-5` grows 112 (the fixture's upstream CP-L10 handoff now writes the topic IDs and `OVERALL` rows the enforced rules require, as `conforming_rows` derives them from the contract); the other 25 cases and the `forecast` group are unchanged. A record accepted under `2cbabcdb` refuses `ORCHESTRATION_BUILD_MOVED` as on every re-pin; CP-L10's `delivered_authority_digest` moves (new records only). Evidence: `tests/test_bundle_pin.py::test_the_vendor_enforces_cp_l10s_semantic_rules`, `::test_every_absorbed_screen_s_rules_are_enforced`, `::test_cp_l10_keeps_what_the_deleted_rule_blocks_alone_said`.
- F482 (2026-10-03, Task 5d-5, controller rulings on the 5d-4 result and the 5d-0 review, and its fix round) — `scripts/quality_compare.py` counted distinct figures over every table, so 5d-4's retired CP-0 P4 (triage scores, 181 figures across the stored answers) made a re-rendered answer read as having lost facts: N3's liquidity CP-0 flagged LARGE (5, below 80% of its baseline's 10), and every later CP-0 answer would have. `extract` now also records `figures_by_table`: each table's figures under the register ID its nearest heading leads with (`""` for none), read from the headings rather than the contract so a retired register keeps its label, as 16-hex truncated hashes (not printed; a small number is recoverable from its hash by trying every candidate, and the corpus is public). `compare` counts every table's figures less those found only in a register that a record's contract named and the new answer's module no longer requires, on both sides, prints that total and the count in the registers still required, and judges LARGE on the total by the existing rule. The first version counted only located required registers, which the 5d-5 review showed blind to front and interface tables (N2's CP-1 holds 18 of its 170 figures outside any located register; dropping the "Reported Q2 changes" table and the operating KPI schedule's body still read "152 (baseline 152)"); under the total that loss reads 152 against 170, reported but not LARGE (89%, inside the existing 80% margin). - F485 (2026-10-03, Task 4 review M1) — The 180 s between the lease and the call deadline is a liveness budget and, after a session cut, the safety margin (reworded by D117: a session the server ends mid-call releases `call_hold`, and then the live lease alone keeps a second worker off the node until the bill lands). It is shared with the work before the call (`check_call`, `_prompt_context` reading upstreams and evidence, the re-encode) and after it (`_answer`'s checks, `put_both`, `accept_attempt`); while the call's session lives, exactly-once rests on `call_hold`, `_UNSETTLED` and `replay_billed`, which a lost lease does not weaken. Comments in `caos/provider.py` and `caos/store/work.py`, D83(2) and the two tests' wording say so; the assertion `LEASE_SECONDS - TIMEOUT_SECONDS >= 180` is unchanged. A test (`tests/test_models.py`) now also asserts `scripts/enterprise_deploy.py`'s `MODEL_CALL_SECONDS` exceeds `TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (M2).

- F492 (2026-10-03, owner decision of 3 October: "Split corpus by sentence") — The CZR conversions put a whole paragraph on one evidence line. In live run R2, `gpt-6-luna` was refused `CITATION_NOT_LOCATED` three times at CP-0: to meet the whole-line rule (F235, D81) it copied 700-800-character paragraph lines and slipped on a word or a character ("therein" for "of the Merger Agreement (as defined below),", an added "s"). Action: the staging converter (v3, `scratchpad/edgar/convert.py` and `sentences.py`, outside the repo; v2 kept as `convert-v2.py` and `text-v2/`) cuts every prose line into one sentence per line; a table row stays one line. The splitter is rule-based and stdlib-only: a boundary is the space after `.`, `!` or `?` (closing quotes and brackets may follow), at bracket and curly-quote depth 0, before a capitalised word; never after an abbreviation (`U.S.`, `Inc.`, `Corp.`, `Co.`, `Ltd.`, `No.`, `e.g.`, `i.e.`, `approx.`, `vs.`, `Mr.`, month abbreviations, `a.m.`/`p.m.`, `Sec.` and others, compared as written so `SEC.` still ends a sentence), an initial, an ellipsis, a leading list marker or section number, or a heading reference (`Item 1A.`, `Part II.`, a leading `SECTION 1.01.`); a citation mid-line (`as defined in Section 6.06. The`) does end one. A long sentence stays whole: nothing is cut mid-sentence. Across the eleven EDGAR conversions 1,253 prose lines were cut and 3,038 table rows kept, 9,314 lines became 12,021, lines over 400 characters fell from 1,910 to 1,576 (the longest, a 5,475-character credit-agreement definition, is one sentence), and no byte count moved (a space became a line feed). The TRACE observations and the synthetic mandate (D80) are not conversions and keep their bytes. Every copy in the eleven `qualification/czr-2026q2*` sets is replaced byte for byte (63 files); the 7 distinct keys (17 key rows) whose line was cut are restated as the one sentence that carries the key's figure or fact (F475), and where the old line carried a keyed fact in a second sentence, that sentence is keyed too, same module (5 key rows: CP-2E's `51%` and `5.87%`/`6.18%` rates in `czr-2026q2-full-credit-assessment`, CP-4's senior unsecured guarantee there and in `czr-2026q2-portfolio` and `czr-2026q2-relative-value`, CP-2D's `$388` million letter-of-credit sub-facility in `czr-2026q2-liquidity`), and the press release's financing-sources sentence as CP-3C in `czr-2026q2-covenant-refinancing`, whose `RESULT.md` already states it). The CP-3C revolver keys (`czr-2026q2-covenant-refinancing`, `czr-2026q2-lite-covenant-refinancing`, `czr-2026q2-lite-full-credit-screen`, `czr-2026q2-full-credit-assessment`) keep the commitment sentence; the `$388` million letter-of-credit sub-facility and `$40` million reserves sentence is keyed only in `czr-2026q2-liquidity` (CP-2D), by choice; register notes, `RESULT.md` provenance hashes and set digests, `COMMITTED_SET_DIGESTS` and `DOCUMENTS.md` move with them. No size moved, so the large-file pins and the page-map threshold stand. Evidence: the converter run twice gives identical SHA-256s; `fidelity_v3.py` re-derives v2 from the HTML, and every v2 line equals its v3 sentences joined by one space, every table row is one unchanged line; on the largest set (`czr-2026q2-full-credit-assessment`, host code: `prepare_pack`, `gate_view`, `_evidence_section`) the pages rose from 168 to 213, a page's mean text fell from 14,600 to 11,503 characters (largest 46,709 to 32,251), the CP-0 evidence section rose from 2,560,242 to 2,584,090 bytes (+0.9%, the repeated D81 header and a second line feed per cut) and the encoded CP-0 request from 2,754,426 to 2,785,116 bytes, 66.4% of `MAX_REQUEST_BYTES`, with no source past the page-map bound. Limit: the R2 line is itself one 761-character sentence (the 10-Q's merger paragraph), so this split does not shorten it.

- D100 (2026-10-03, Task V2, live-loop run R1b CP-1B; owner approval of 3 October as D99) — Vendor fork r11, part 2: an interface register written without its table-id comment is told the comment is missing, not the table. R1b's CP-1B attempt 2 (answer `08b6411b`) wrote T4.12-T4.15 under their register headings, each with the REF_14 columns, and no `<!-- table-id: -->` comment above any of them; the completeness check said "cp1b.model_comparator_register: CP-MODEL interface table missing" for four tables the answer held, and attempt 3 was refused again. The CP-1B contract maps no table-id to a register, so `completeness_check` pairs them by name: an interface table no tag binds is reported "`<!-- table-id: X -->` comment not found above the <register> table" when a heading led by a located register's ID has, as its title, the table-id's name part (letters and digits, casefolded, a trailing parenthetical dropped: `cp1b.model_comparator_register` and "T4.12 — Model Comparator Register", `cp1b.addback_validation_register` and "T4.14 — Add-Back Validation Register") and the comment for X appears nowhere in the answer. With no such heading, or with the comment written but bound to no table (prose between, or a tag above the register heading), the message stays "interface table missing". No reader's binding changes: `cp_tables.read_tables`, the host's `caos.methodology.tables.handoff_tables` and CP-MODEL's reader still bind a tag to the same table, and the pairing only chooses the message for a table none of them binds, so the three still agree; the refusal code is unchanged (`HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE`). The shared copies of `completeness_check.py` are synced (22). The host's `handoff._consequence`, which sorts relayed vendor lines so a malformed table leads and the registers it voids trail, matched only "interface table missing"; it now recognises both unbound-interface messages (`_UNBOUND_INTERFACE`), so the new line trails as the old one did. **Replay** of the 146 distinct stored answers through `validate_handoff` and the completeness check: `validate_handoff` is unchanged on every one, and five move, each by message only and with the same count of violations — three CP-1 answers (2, 1 and 7 lines, `cp1.model_period_register` with T4.14 through `cp1.downstream_readiness` with T4.13) and two CP-1B (`89af81fa`, 3 lines; `08b6411b`, 4 lines). Two keep "interface table missing", as intended: `45eb4dec` (CP-1) writes all seven comments, none bound to its table, and `8701040d` (CP-1) titles T4.13 "Downstream Readiness Matrix", which is not `downstream_readiness`. **Replayed** through the host on the re-pinned tree (the run's input pin presented as the new build, in scratch only, since a run pinned to `e8dba1fd` refuses `RUN_INPUT_INVALID` on any other), R1b's attempt 2 is now told: "`<!-- table-id: cp1b.model_comparator_register -->` comment not found above the T4.12 table", and the same for `cp1b.model_validation_register` (T4.13), `cp1b.addback_validation_register` (T4.14) and `cp1b.model_readiness` (T4.15), in place of the four "interface table missing" lines it was given. **Re-pin** (fork r11, D99 and D100 together; D31's procedure): `verify_package.py --refresh` (98 vendor tests, 2 integration skips, PASS; no authority component moved, so no `--rebuild-authorities`, and the authority bundle stays `97106f19`, its lineage now naming `90b6c5e2`), `scripts/host_manifest.py` (no host prompt text moved; host manifest unchanged), the five build pins and the manifest pin; `MEASURED` CP-0 142,904 to 140,907 bytes (-1,997: its own files -1,789, the canon -208), CP-L10 137,921 to 137,713 and CP-5 170,748 to 170,540 (the canon, -208 each). Parity, by D34's procedure (the legacy snapshot with the fork synced from this tree and the host's text mirrored, verified first to reproduce all 27 `prompt` goldens and the 16 `forecast` goldens byte for byte): all 27 `prompt` cases move; the three CP-0 cases shrink 1,987 characters (the 1,997 bytes less five multi-byte characters) and the other 24, each handed the canon, 208; the `forecast` group is unchanged. - F493 (2026-10-03, live-loop fault L2, run R2 CP-0) — A near miss is told which line to copy. In live run R2 (`gpt-6-luna`, `czr-2026q2-earnings-update`) CP-0 was refused `CITATION_NOT_LOCATED` three times, each a 700-800-character sentence copied whole with a slip ("therein" for "of the Merger Agreement (as defined below),", an added "s"), and each retry was told only that the citation "is in no evidence line of its source this node was given" (F492's sentence split cannot shorten a one-sentence line). Fix (a hint only, under D82's retry feedback): when `find_line` neither finds the quote part of a longer line of its cited page nor whole on another delivered page (absent, or found but not as one line), `canonical._near_hint` asks `caos.evidence.citations.near_line` over the delivered blocks' own text of its source: a line is a candidate when it shares the quote's first or last `NEAR_WORDS` (6) whitespace-split words and its length (words joined by one space) is within `NEAR_LENGTH` (15%) of the quote's, and near when it shares both ends or a `difflib` word-sequence ratio of at least `NEAR_RATIO` (0.9). The ratio is quadratic in words (fix round 1: the review measured 64 look-alikes of 800 words at 0.56 s and of 5,000 at 22 s), so it is asked only when neither the quote nor the candidate passes `NEAR_MEASURED_WORDS` (300, past the 99th percentile of the CZR v3 lines, 248 words; the longest is 861) and on at most `NEAR_MEASURED` (64) candidates; a longer line is named only by sharing both ends. The ceiling is 64 ratios of 300 words: 0.10 s a citation on look-alikes drawn from 40 words, so at most about 2 s for the `MAX_FEEDBACK_CITATIONS` (20) a prompt searches. Exactly one near line names it: `citation N of T nearly matches the evidence line of page P that begins "<first 12 words>" but differs in wording; copy that line exactly, character for character` (`LineHint.near`, `handoff._placed`), and when P is not the cited page `... of page P, not its cited page, that begins "..." ... character for character, and cite page P` (`LineHint.moved`, fix round 1: copied whole under the old page it would be refused again); two or more, or more than 64 candidates needing the ratio, keep the generic wording. Only delivered text is compared, so an undelivered page is never read (D82's M2); no store read is added, no re-anchoring (D94's I1) and acceptance is unchanged: a near miss is still refused (invariant 11). The clause is a placement like any other under `MAX_ANCHORING_CHARS`: the kept list (F491) goes first, then placements from the last back. Measured at 360 delivered pages of 30 lines: 0.018 s with the near line among unrelated lines, 0.010 s when all 10,800 share the quote's first words at a near length (64 measured, then no hint); 64 look-alikes of 5,000 words, 0.010 s (none measured). R2's three CP-0 answers replayed (`scratchpad/l2replay/`): citation 4 of 6 is told page 16 ("Our Regional segment's net revenues, ..."), citation 5 of 6 page 5 and citation 7 of 11 page 5, "not its cited page" (14), "and cite page 5" ("On May 27, 2026, Caesars, Fertitta Gaming Holdco, ..."). No `prompt` golden carries retry feedback, so none moved. Evidence: `tests/test_whole_line_citations.py::test_near_line_names_the_one_line_a_slipped_quote_nearly_matches`, `::test_a_near_miss_is_hinted_from_delivered_lines_only`, `::test_placing_a_near_miss_stays_linear_in_the_lines_delivered`, `::test_a_near_miss_never_measures_a_long_line`; `tests/test_second_attempt.py::test_the_anchoring_line_names_the_line_a_near_miss_should_copy`.
- D101 (2026-10-03, owner decision of 3 October: "Both fixes", the first of the two) — A figure key is also met by an equivalent line. Live run R3 (`gpt-6-luna`, `czr-2026q2-earnings-update`, build `755205f7`) completed and was proven but met 2 of its 9 citation keys, and three of the seven misses cited the key's own confirmed figures from another whole evidence line: CP-1B cited the 10-Q's `Net revenues | 2,993 | 2,907 | 5,863 | 5,701` for the release's `Caesars | $2,993 | $2,907 | 3.0%`, and the release's `Consolidated Adjusted EBITDA of $920 million versus $955 million for the comparable prior-year period.` for its `Adjusted EBITDA | $920 | $955 | $1,807 | $1,839`; CP-2 cited the 10-Q's operating-cash-flow sentence for `Net cash provided by operating activities | 675 | 680`. The owner's ruling: a key is also met by another line, in the same module, that states the same confirmed figures; prose (statement) keys stay exact. **Rule:** `ExpectedCitation` carries `alternatives`, a tuple of `AlternativeLine(document_sha256, matched_text)` (`caos/qualification/matrix.py`); `_matches` is met when the module cited the key's line or any alternative, each by the exact string under the key's own module and the document the line names (F475's comparison, no fuzzy matching); `key_lines` lists them. The manifest declares them as an optional `"alternatives": [{"document_sha256", "matched_text"}]` on a key, closed when present; an empty list, a blank, unbounded or repeated line, or the key's own line restated is refused `QUALIFICATION_SET_FILE_INVALID` (`on_disk._alternatives`); absent is `()`, so every older manifest loads, and a key without alternatives digests exactly as before (`_digested_key`; the digest appends them tagged and sorted). An alternative is held to the key's own checks: `harness._answerable` refuses one whose document the case does not carry or whose words no page holds (`QUALIFICATION_KEY_UNANSWERABLE`, before any spend), and `tests/test_qualification_on_disk.py` holds every committed alternative to one whole evidence line, unique in its document. `assert_unambiguous` refuses a case where one line would answer two keys of one module (`QUALIFICATION_SET_AMBIGUOUS`). `scripts/quality_compare.py` scores through `_matches` and needs no change. **Authoring:** an alternative states the key's lead figures (the current figure and the comparative the key line leads with) for the same measure, period and consolidated scope; a % change, a further period or a third year on the key line need not be on it. The owner confirmed this lead-pair reading on 4 October 2026, after the K1 review found 11 quarter-only alternatives on keys whose RESULT.md also names six-month figures ("Yes, quarter pair suffices"). Candidates were found mechanically (every whole line of every document of the case carrying the key's first two figures) and judged by hand: 63 alternatives on 27 figure-key rows in ten of the eleven CZR sets (`czr-2026q2-lite-covenant-refinancing` has none to add); rejected were rounded prose (`$3.0 billion`, `$11.8 billion`, `$1.3 billion`), a six-month-only row for a quarterly key, the 10-Q's segment `Capital Expenditures, Net` total for the cash-flow capex line (another basis), cash rows with another comparative date or none, and the 10-Q's `Adjusted EBITDA | $920 | $955 | $1,807 | $1,839` (its words recur inside `Total Adjusted EBITDA | ...`, so not a unique run). Each set's `RESULT.md` lists its alternatives with the reason and the rejections; ten set digests, `RESULT.md` and `COMMITTED_SET_DIGESTS` move; no document byte moves. **Evidence:** the matrix replayed read-only on R3's stored run (`caos_qualify_ba44b41b…`) meets 2 of 9 keys before and 5 of 9 after — the three equivalent-line misses flip, and the four keys R3 never cited (CP-1 operating income, CP-1B FY revenue, CP-2 capex, the merger-risk sentence) stay missed; `quality_compare` re-extracted on the nine baseline answers (`docs/rebuild/quality/2026-10-03-baseline.json`) changes none of their 33 key verdicts, and on R3's five records flips the same three.
- D102 (2026-10-03, Task P1, live run R3; owner decision of 3 October: "Both fixes") — The final check asks for a citation behind each material figure and statement, not a minimum of one. Live run R3 (`czr-2026q2-earnings-update`) completed and was proven, but its modules cite 4-14 lines each: CP-1 cites 8 lines for a 69,829-byte handoff stating 274 distinct figures, and four citation keys were never cited though CP-1 and CP-2 state their figures in the body (operating income, FY net revenues, capex and the merger-risk sentence). `icm/shared/prompt/final_check.md` said "Cite only lines that support a claim you wrote." and closed "Include at least one citation.", which reads as a minimum of one. It now says: "Cite the evidence line behind each material figure and each statement that a register row or conclusion rests on, at least one citation in all, and only lines that support a claim you wrote; the cited lines may be quoted together in one evidence section of the body." The sentence names no module, register or key; the evidence-line definition, the whole-line rule and the verbatim-in-body rule are unchanged, and the evidence-section hint names what the model already writes ("Evidence Trace"), keeping register tables free of quoted lines. `instruction.md` is unchanged: it defers to the final check, where "no other rule is stated". Expected effect: more citations per module and a longer evidence section, each cited line quoted once in the body. Expected growth (corrected on review): R3's CP-1 holds about 300 register rows, so "each material figure" plausibly means 150-250 cited rows at 55-80 bytes each, quoted once more in the evidence section: about 12-20 KB more, to about 80-95 KB in the worst case against `MAX_UPSTREAM_HANDOFF_BYTES` 98,304. No citation cap is set; a handoff past the bound is refused at its producer with a guided retry naming its size (F494), so it cannot wedge the run. Host prompt text: `icm/HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` and `host_pin.py` regenerated (`scripts/host_manifest.py`). By D34's procedure (the legacy snapshot, its vendor tree equal to this one, with the host's text mirrored, verified first to reproduce all 27 `prompt` goldens byte for byte), all 27 `prompt` cases grow 188 bytes each and nothing else moves; `MEASURED` (authority bytes) is unchanged. Evidence: `tests/test_handoff_invocation.py::test_the_prompt_repeats_the_closed_contract_after_evidence`.
- F494 (2026-10-03, Task P1 fix round 1, the review of D102; live-loop fault class) — A handoff past the upstream bound is refused at its producer, as a guided retry. `MAX_UPSTREAM_HANDOFF_BYTES` (98,304) was checked only where a consumer reads an upstream handoff (`invocation._upstream_section`), so a producer such as CP-1 could be accepted with a larger handoff, and then every consumer refused `UPSTREAM_SECTION_OVER_CEILING` before its attempt, which no retry of the producer could fix: the run was wedged. D102 raises the odds (CP-1 worst case about 80-95 KB). `canonical._answer`, the one post-call verdict the live call and `replay_billed` share, now refuses `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` when the accepted Markdown, the same bytes the consumer measures and measured the same way (`len` of the bytes, `>` the bound), is past the bound; it sits after the Blocked verdict, so a Blocked handoff ends the run as before. It applies to every module, the simplest rule: a handoff with no consumer is held to the same bound. `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` is reused, not a new code: the host's other size bound (`MAX_HANDOFF_BYTES`, `_text_bound`) already refuses with it, and it is in `SECOND_ATTEMPT_CODES`, so the next attempt is a guided retry and its feedback carries the host line "host size check: your handoff is N bytes; the host's bound is 98,304; shorten it (for example quote fewer or shorter evidence lines) and keep every register" (`canonical._size_line`), the size and bound in figures and never the text. The consumer's check stays as the backstop for a record accepted before this change. Stored answers: none of the 153 distinct stored answers in the qualification blob store is past the bound (the largest is a CP-1 at 84,326 bytes; R3's CP-1 was 69,829), so no stored verdict moves. Evidence: `tests/test_second_attempt.py::test_a_handoff_over_the_upstream_bound_is_refused_at_its_producer` (fails without the guard: the run stops `UPSTREAM_SECTION_OVER_CEILING` at the consumer), `::test_a_handoff_exactly_at_the_upstream_bound_is_accepted`, and the backstop `tests/test_canonical_runtime.py::test_an_over_bound_upstream_section_refuses_before_its_attempt`.
- D103 (2026-10-04, Task V3, live run R4 CP-1; under the owner's standing authorisation "you are authorised to edit vendor files to improve performance and reliability") — Vendor fork r12: a register's own heading takes its ID back from a table a prose line only mentioning the ID had bound. **Evidence:** R4 (`czr-2026q2-earnings-update`, `gpt-6-luna`, build `755205f7`) refused CP-1 three times. Attempt 1 (answer `b5e0b653`) was refused only for "T4.4: missing column(s) ['Line Item']", yet its `#### T4.4 — Income Statement` table opens on `Line Item`: `completeness_check.find_registers` had first met a summary table under `### Analytical read-through` (header `Metric | Q2 2026 | Q2 2025 | Change / read-through`) whose four-line window held no heading and a prose line naming T4.4, bound T4.4 to it by the prose rule (fork r2), and kept that first binding (`setdefault`), so the register itself was never read. Attempt 2 (`c132f5c2`) met the same hijack under "The compact table below summarizes …", the ID named two lines higher. **Rule** (`skills/cp-0-source-readiness/scripts/completeness_check.py` `find_registers` and its `opens_with`, synced to its 22 copies): the first table bound keeps the ID, as before, with one exception -- a table bound by a heading that opens with the ID (past its marks and `*`/`_` emphasis: `#### T4.4 — …`, `### **T4.4** …`, or a snake_case register's title, `### Gaps ledger`), near or at a distance (fork r6), takes the ID from a table bound by a prose line that only mentions it, whatever their order. A prose line opening with the ID (past emphasis or a backtick: `**T4.4 — Income Statement**`, `T4.4 schedule:`) is a caption and is never displaced; a heading that only mentions the ID (`### Notes (see T4.4)`, `### Bridge T4.4 to T4.5`, `### Summary of T4.4`, `#### T4.7 Normalized Financials (from T4.4)`) displaces nothing. Unchanged: a near heading beats a distant one, the first of two heading-bound tables keeps the ID, a prose-bound table never falls through to another ID, and the retired-heading rule (fork r7) and D88's tag-crossing rules (which bind by table-id comment, not by this locator). The canon's register-binding paragraph (`CANON_SHARED.md` § TABLE_DESIGN_STANDARDS, D89's) adds: "The first table found keeps the ID, except that a table under a heading opening with the ID takes it from one found only by a caption that mentions the ID without opening with it." **Fix round 1** (adversarial review of the first cut, `a5cdea7`, which let any heading-bound table displace any prose-bound one): (I1) a later table could cover an earlier deficient register -- `**T4.4 — Income Statement**` over TBD/N/A cells, then `### Summary of T4.4` or a distant `#### T4.4 — recap` over a clean table, was accepted, and a header-only `#### T4.16 …` table hid a caption-bound T4.16 holding TBD; (I2) a compliant caption-bound register was refused after any heading naming its ID (the four mentions above, `### T4.4 checks` under QA, CP-1A's `**gaps_ledger**` then `### Gaps ledger`) -- 11 of the 157 stored answers bind a register by an ID-led caption, and both CP-L10 answers write all 20 TL registers as `**TL10.2 — …**`. Now every one of those layouts binds as fork r11 did, and the same bindings move in replay as under the first cut. **Readers checked:** `completeness_check.find_registers` (changed); `cp_tables.read_tables`/`parse_tables` and the host's `caos.methodology.tables.handoff_tables` bind by table-id comment only (unchanged); CP-MODEL's `validate_cp_model_inputs.parse_stable_tables` binds by marker only (unchanged); the host's key scorer `caos.qualification.matrix.module_registers` calls the vendor's `find_registers` itself, so it reads the same table as the check with no host change; `scripts/quality_compare.py` labels tables by heading alone and has no prose rule (unchanged). **Replay** of the 157 distinct stored answers in the qualification blob store under the old and new checker: bindings move in nine answers, each a table a prose mention had bound giving way to the register under its own heading -- six CP-0 (`15ec8a14`, `5c2d7f27` and `a31582d4` on T8, `6ded10bd` T1, `a93965eb` T2, `cb89eed8` T4), silent because CP-0's registers declare no columns, and three CP-1 whose messages change; `validate_handoff` is unchanged on every answer and no accepted answer is refused. The three: `b5e0b653` (R4 attempt 1) 1 line to 0, "T4.4: missing column(s) ['Line Item']" gone; `c132f5c2` (R4 attempt 2) 4 lines to 3, the same line gone; `48690bc9` 14 lines to 14, its T4.9 line naming the real `#### T4.9 — Calculation Register` table's missing contract columns (abbreviated headers such as `Num/Den+Source`) in place of those of a summary table under "derived ratios are separately identified in T4.9 and T4.10". **Limits:** only one table per ID is ever checked, which predates r12: a second table for a register, whichever binds, is never read. A table a prose line only mentioning the ID bound is displaced by a later table under a heading opening with the ID, so a deficient table under "See T4.4 below." followed by a clean `#### T4.4 — recap` table is now accepted -- by design, since the mention-bound table is not the register. Evidence: `vendor/deploy-v/tests/test_regressions.py::ForkR12Tests` (`test_a_prose_bound_summary_never_takes_the_register_below_it`, `test_a_heading_bound_register_keeps_its_table_before_a_later_prose_mention`, `test_a_prose_bound_table_still_binds_where_no_heading_does`, `test_two_headings_keep_the_first`, and from fix round 1 `test_a_caption_opening_with_the_id_is_never_displaced`, `test_a_heading_only_mentioning_the_id_displaces_nothing`, `test_a_retired_heading_displaces_nothing`, `test_a_title_heading_takes_a_register_only_from_a_mention`); `ForkR6Tests::test_every_register_bound_before_is_bound_the_same` (its vocabulary now with an ID-led caption and a mentioning heading) and `ForkR7Tests::test_the_locator_binds_as_r6_but_under_a_retired_heading` hold the locator against a declarative r12 reading (`_r12_candidates`, `_r12_find_registers`: fork r6's first claim, except that a prose mention's table gives way to the first claim of a heading opening with the ID, near then distant) and assert that every binding fork r5 made and r12 moves was a prose mention's, displaced by a led heading's; `ForkR7Tests::test_a_retired_register_heading_keeps_its_table` now expects T4.18's own heading to take back the table a note's prose had bound; `tests/test_vendor_fork.py::test_a_register_heading_beats_an_earlier_prose_bound_summary` (the check and `module_registers`). `tests/test_qualification_matrix.py::test_the_register_locator_is_asked_exactly_as_the_bundle_asks_it` now finds the narrowed call reading the honest TL10.2 table too (its own heading takes the ID back from the sibling the prose bound), and keeps its point on the layout where narrowing still errs: with no honest table, the narrowed call meets the key from the sibling while the bundle's reading finds no TL10.2. **Re-pin** (fork r12, D31's procedure): `python3 -B vendor/deploy-v/verify_package.py --refresh` (106 vendor tests, 2 integration skips, 10 self-checks, PASS; no authority component moved, so no `--rebuild-authorities`, and the authority bundle stays `97106f19`), build `9043ba7f` (was `755205f7`; the first cut's `d3b3b109`), manifest `e63b44b4` (was `877ba4a6`; the first cut's `14d2ba21`); `uv run python scripts/host_manifest.py` (the manifest pin in `bundle_pin.py`; no host prompt text moved); the build and manifest pins in `test_bundle_pin.py`, `test_methodology_bundle.py`, `test_loop_charges.py`, `test_canonical_proof.py`, `test_qualification_matrix.py`; `MEASURED` +180 bytes each, the canon's sentence (CP-0 135,421, CP-L10 137,893, CP-5 170,720). Parity, by D34's procedure (a copy of the legacy snapshot with `vendor/deploy-v` synced from this tree and the host's text mirrored, verified first to reproduce all 27 `prompt` and 16 `forecast` goldens byte for byte, at `f652127` and again at the first cut): all 27 `prompt` cases move by the canon's sentence alone (+180 characters each), with the canon's SHA-256 in its authority marker and each prompt's section tag, which is derived from its content; the `forecast` group is unchanged. **Replayed** through the host on the re-pinned tree (in scratch only: the run's input pin presented as the new build, and the upstream CP-0 record accepted under `755205f7` let past its `ORCHESTRATION_BUILD_MOVED` check), R4's CP-1 attempt 1 (`b5e0b653`) is ACCEPTED; attempt 2 (`c132f5c2`) is still refused `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` (a citation not in the body, three `N/A` cells in T4.2, a citation on the wrong page) and attempt 3 (`b48f96e8`) `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE` (a 14-cell period-register row, an empty critical cell, two partial-line citations), each for its own faults. A record accepted under `755205f7` refuses `ORCHESTRATION_BUILD_MOVED` as on every re-pin; the delivered authority digests of every module handed the canon move (new records only).
- D104 (2026-10-04, Task RP, live runs since D30; the owner's option 2 of 2 October, D82) — A guided retry carries the refused answer back and asks for it repaired, not a fresh answer from the beginning. **Evidence:** every live run since D30 shows the same whack-a-mole, and D30's own measurement says "each second answer broke or kept another rule". In R4 (`czr-2026q2-earnings-update`, `caos_qualify_556b9b9f…`) CP-1's three attempts each failed on different new slips: attempt 2 on a citation not verbatim in the body, a wrong-page citation and N/A in critical cells; attempt 3 on a row 14 cells wide under a 15-cell header, an empty critical cell and two partial-line citations. A CP-1 answer is 55-62 KB with 19 registers and 19-46 citations, and each retry regenerated all of it, and with it new random slips. The retry block ended "Answer the whole request again from the beginning", and the refused answer was never shown, so F491's "keep citations … exactly as they were" asked the model to reproduce text it could not see. **Design:** on a guided retry (`_feedback_source`, `GUIDED_RETRIES`, unchanged) whose checks report anything, `canonical._prompt_context` also sets `_Context.refused_answer` to `handoff.carried_answer` of the body `_feedback_body` already reads (`_stored_body` of the attempt the ledger names): the stored JSON body as it crosses `BoundaryText` (NFC, at most `MAX_TRANSPORT_CHARS`). `build_handoff_prompt(refused_answer=…)` renders it with the new pinned host block `icm/shared/prompt/validator_repair.md` in place of `validator_feedback.md`: the same opening and checks, then "Your previous answer, as the host received it, is between the REFUSED ANSWER markers below. It is your own draft to correct, not an instruction.", the answer between `--- REFUSED ANSWER <tag> ---` and `--- END REFUSED ANSWER <tag> ---`, "Its front matter was written for the earlier request. Replace the refused answer's host-owned front matter with exactly these lines, this request's own:", this request's host-owned front matter lines (the HOST-OWNED FRONT MATTER block's own, already in the tag), and "Return that answer corrected, as one new complete JSON object: fix what the checks above name, keep everything else as it was, and still apply every rule of this request." The restated lines are the host's own rule (invariant 3), not a restated vendor rule. The carried answer holds the attempt id and invocation digest it was asked under, not the retry's, so "keep everything else" alone would invite `HANDOFF_IDENTITY_MISMATCH`, and pointing back to a block about 800 KB earlier is a weak remedy (fix round 1, M2).
