- F509 (2026-10-05, live-loop fault L7, run LCR4 CP-3C; D30, D82 and D104's feedback class) — A register row a cell short or long is named to the guided retry. In live run LCR4 (`czr-2026q2-lite-covenant-refinancing`, `caos_qualify_9867fdb8…`) CP-3C was refused `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE` four times (`78104e3b…`, `e2ba81c2…`, `c5653d23…`, `7668199b…`), each told only "completeness_check: T3D.2 row 8: critical column 'Source Trace' holds a disqualifying placeholder ''". Row 8 ("CEI Senior Secured Notes due 2032") had 11 cells under the 12-cell header: the model left out Credit Implication, so its source text sat in that column, and the vendor's `find_registers` padded the row with an empty Source Trace. The model saw its source text in the row and every repair retry (D104) kept it. The vendor names a row of another width only for a tagged interface table (`cp_tables._read_table`), never for a register. `handoff.feedback_lines` now adds a host line for every data row of a register `find_registers` binds (asked as `check()` asks it) whose cell count, as the vendor's own `_row_cells` reads it (escaped pipes included), is not its header's: "host table check: T3D.2 row 8 has 11 cells under a 12-cell header; a cell is missing or extra, so its later columns shift (the last column, 'Source Trace', reads empty)", or, for a long row, "(the cell past the last column, 'Source Trace', is dropped)". The lines come after the other host lines and before the vendor's, at most `MAX_WIDTH_ROWS` (5) of them plus one counting the rest, each crossing `BoundaryText` like a vendor line. A table a `<!-- table-id: -->` tag binds that the vendor already reports as "differs from its header" gets no second line. Advisory only: acceptance is unchanged and the vendor check still decides. Replayed on LCR4's four answers, each now carries the host line ahead of the vendor's. Evidence: `tests/test_register_width.py`.
- F510 (2026-10-05, live-loop fault L8, run LFCS1 CP-5; D30, D82 and D104's feedback class) — CP-5's guided retry is told which owner restrictions it dropped. In live run LFCS1 (`czr-2026q2-lite-full-credit-screen`, `caos_qualify_81c7bf72…`) CP-5 was refused four times: `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE` on attempts 1, 2 and 4 (`72c6aa9e…`, `c93e53a8…`, `c739f393…`) and `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` on 3 (`eddf42b3…`, a dangling marker). The `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE` refusals came from `verification.verify_owner_restrictions` through `canonical._forecast_inputs` under `CREDIT_SCREEN_SELECTION`: CP-5 must carry every direct owner's `limitation_flags` and `validation_warnings` as sets and stay `Restricted` while an owner is. That refusal made no feedback line, so every retry was blind. Attempts 1 and 2 left out 39 owner items (24 distinct), and attempts 3 and 4 left out 31 (23 distinct); all were omissions, with no near-copy (best difflib ratio 0.76); qa_status was Restricted throughout. The comparison is now `verification.owner_restriction_gaps` (`OwnerGap`: owner, list, item), which `verify_owner_restrictions` refuses on, so the refusal and the feedback cannot drift; acceptance is unchanged (exact set inclusion, `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE`). `canonical._owner_lines` adds host lines to CP-5's and CP-CF's retry, before the anchoring line and the vendor's lines: "host owner check: CP-5's front matter must carry each upstream owner's limitation_flags and validation_warnings exactly; missing from limitation_flags: «FULL_UNDERWRITING_SOURCE_SET_NOT_RETAINED» (from CP-0, CP-L10, …), …", then "also missing from …" lines, each item quoted once with every owner that carries it and packed up to `MAX_FEEDBACK_CHARS` a line, at most `MAX_OWNER_LINES` (6) plus one counting the items not shown; and "host owner check: CP-5's qa_status must be Restricted while an upstream owner's is; Restricted: …" when that rule fails. The items are the upstream owners' own front matter, already in the prompt's upstream handoffs (D30). Replayed on LFCS1, each of the four answers gets three or four owner lines that name every missing item. With D104's carried answer, the retry has the whole list to add. Evidence: `tests/test_owner_feedback.py`.
- F511 (2026-10-05, live-loop fault L9, runs LCR2 CP-L10, LCR3 CP-3C and LFCS2 CP-1A; test adapter only) — The test-only OpenRouter adapter streams. Three live runs stopped `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` on a call that left no generation and no charge, after about 195 s (LCR2, CP-L10), 299 s (LCR3, CP-3C) and 294 s (LFCS2, CP-1A), short of `TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (420): large-request nodes on sets carrying the ~1 MB conformed credit agreement, at effort high (`docs/rebuild/runs/live-2026-10-03/{LCR2,LCR3,LFCS2}-*`). A request sent whole sends no byte back until the answer is done; an upstream idle cut near 300 s was one candidate, unconfirmed (calls of 334 to 358 s completed, and the drops came at 138 to 300 s; F513 names the cause on the next drop). `tests/openrouter_adapter.py` now builds its `ChatOpenAI` with `streaming=True` and `stream_usage=True` (`stream_options.include_usage`), so OpenRouter's keep-alives and tokens flow while the model works and `invoke` still returns one `AIMessage`. In JSON mode, the mode every module call asks for (`canonical`, `gateway_smoke`), langchain-openai streams through the OpenAI client's chat-completion stream and ends on the final completion, so the message carries the whole text, the usage (reasoning tokens included) and `response_metadata["id"]`, the provider's completion id `caos.models._claimed_id` reads and the qualify harness reconciles by. Without JSON mode the stream carries no completion id and `caos.models` falls back to its host-minted id, as it already does for any answer that names none; no caller asks for that mode. JSON mode, the F479 `extra_body` (effort, provider pin), the ceiling, the deadline and `max_retries=0` are sent as before, and the identity string is unchanged. `openrouter_chat_model` gains a keyword `http_client`, the test seam for a fake transport. Production (`caos/`) is untouched. Evidence: `tests/test_openrouter_adapter.py` (an SSE fake with a keep-alive comment and a choiceless usage chunk, through `ChatCompletions.complete`).
- F512 (2026-10-05, RCA-PROV; amends F511; test adapter only) — The streamed adapter read OpenRouter's stream wrong, so L9's first live call was refused `PROVIDER_RESPONSE_INVALID` (usage missing). OpenRouter repeats `role` on every delta and sends its usage in a frame that repeats the `finish_reason` in one choice, where OpenAI sends an empty `choices` (streaming reference, "The final usage chunk"). langchain-openai 1.6.3's JSON-mode stream (`beta.chat.completions.stream`) summed the roles into `assistantassistant`, so `_get_generation_chunk_from_completion` saw no `AIMessage` and dropped the usage, and the merged `finish_reason` became `stopstop`; F511's fake sent `role` once and a choiceless usage frame, so it passed. `tests/openrouter_adapter.py` now builds `OpenRouterChat`, a `ChatOpenAI` whose `_generate` sends the request `ChatOpenAI` builds with `stream` and `stream_options.include_usage` set and reads the frames with `streamed_message`: the first choice's content deltas (reasoning deltas never join the answer) up to the first stated finish, that finish once, the usage frame's whole counts and the stream's id; `disable_streaming` keeps the library's own stream from running. An SSE `error` event raises the client's `APIError`, typed `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`; a stream ending without a usage is refused `PROVIDER_RESPONSE_INVALID` with no charge, and one with a usage but no finish is refused the same way and carries the charge (`caos/models.py:198-205`). Evidence: `tests/test_openrouter_adapter.py` (transcripts per the streaming and errors references: keep-alive comments, repeated roles, reasoning deltas, both usage-frame shapes, an error event after partial output and as the only event, a cut stream, a stream without usage; and `test_a_real_openrouter_stream_reads_whole` over `tests/fixtures/openrouter_sse_sample.txt`, the raw stream of one live call of 5 October, which shows `role` on every delta and the usage in a frame that repeats the finish in one choice).
- F513 (2026-10-05, RCA-PROV; live runs LCR2, LCR3, LFCS2, FCA1) — A call that got no answer left no trace of why. `ChatCompletions.complete` mapped a vendor error by status class and swallowed anything else the client raised, or the deadline, into `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`, with no diagnostic body (none exists for a refused call) and no line; the four drops (195 s, 300 s, 295 s and 138 s, none billed) cannot be told apart from the store or the captures. Measured against the pre-F511 non-streamed adapter, five distinct wire events all end as the same bare code: a `200` whose body is only an OpenRouter `error` object (`TypeError` from the client's parse; OpenRouter commits the `200` before the provider answers, so a provider failure after that arrives this way), a `502` or `524` status, a reset mid-body (`APIConnectionError` from `RemoteProtocolError`) and a truncated body (`JSONDecodeError`). `complete` now writes one stderr line per unanswered call, as the worker prints its codes, in a single `write` with its newline: the code, `call=vendor|raised|escaped|deadline` (`escaped`: a `BaseException` the sender does not hold back, as `suppress(Exception)` never did), the class and up to three causes as identifiers, the status, the provider error's `code` as a three- or four-digit status and its `metadata.error_type` only when it is one of the types OpenRouter documents, each else `?`, and the seconds since first send; never a message, body or prompt. The sender holds back exactly what `suppress(Exception)` did, its exception-group split included, and keeps only the failure's facts, never the exception, so the raising frames go with the call. The line is a fail-open (adversarial audit of round 1): a failure reading the facts gives `class=? ...`, and a failed write drops the line; the refusal and the recorded outcome are those the call had before the line existed. Not covered: `databricks_langchain` raises `ValueError(response.error)` only on its Responses-API path, which caos never enables, and that error is a pydantic `ResponseError`, not a mapping, so it would print `error_code=- error_type=-`. Evidence: `tests/test_models.py` (`test_an_unanswered_call_names_its_class_on_stderr_and_no_text`, the deadline and refused-status tests, the token rules, the one write, the facts-only hold, the `suppress(Exception)` parity) and `tests/test_execution_billing.py::test_a_failing_diagnostic_never_changes_the_recorded_outcome` (an unwritable stderr, a raising `body`, an unprintable code: the same refusal and recorded outcome as before F513, against Postgres).
- F514-F519 were never allocated: the parallel RCA agents skipped them; not renumbered.
- F520 (2026-10-05, Task RCA-OPS, sweep for D103's class: loose text read where a structural reading exists) — The host register hint reads an ID whole, as the vendor's locator does. `handoff._absent_ids_line` (N52) told a guided retry which register IDs an answer "never writes" by asking `register not in text`, so `T4.1` was "written" by any `T4.10` to `T4.19`: a CP-1 answer lacking only T4.1 got no hint while `completeness_check` refused "T4.1: required register missing". Now `_writes_id` matches the ID with `find_registers`' own `id_re` boundary (not inside a longer ID; a sentence's full stop after it ends it). Hint only; no acceptance moves. Evidence: `tests/test_register_ids.py` (fails before the change on the T4.1 case).
- F521 (2026-10-05, Task RCA-OPS, live run R6 `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`, `caos_qualify_bdd7ca2f…`) — `scripts/qualify_reclaim.py` lists the harness's `caos_qualify_<hex>` databases, their sizes and runs, and prints `DROP DATABASE` only for one whose every run a capture JSON's `run_ids` names; it drops nothing. **Cause of R6:** the Docker (colima) VM disk reached zero free; at 05:20:11 UTC CP-1B attempt 2 was charged and its artifact accepted, then 0.26 s later CP-2's evidence read (`read_run_blocks`, whose CTE spills about 3.4 MB to `pgsql_tmp`) failed "No space left on device" (postgres log), mapped to `STORE_UNAVAILABLE` before any CP-2 attempt row or reservation: failed closed at the right point, no spend unrecorded, run left RUNNING. The harness creates one database per set (29 to 118 MB, mostly `source_tokens`) and by design (DQ-12) never drops one; 27 held 2.2 GB, beside 14.1 GB of unused images and 7.5 GB of unlinked anonymous volumes on a 39.1 GB disk. Evidence: `tests/test_qualify_reclaim.py`.
- F522 (2026-10-05, RCA-CP3C, run LCR6 CP-3C; F509's feedback class) — The width line names the short row by its first cell and shows how its last columns read. In live run LCR6 (`czr-2026q2-lite-covenant-refinancing`, `caos_qualify_f463e399…`, run `158fd948…`) CP-3C was refused `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE` on attempts 1–3 (`bd0c06cb…`, `6605ba31…`, `e7918dbf…`) and `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` on 4 (`a81f1654…`). Replayed offline, all four answers fail the same check: T3D.2 row 7 ("CEI Senior Secured Notes due 2032", the row LCR4 also broke) has 11 cells under the 12-cell header, Credit Implication left out, so the vendor pads an empty Source Trace (`completeness_check`: "critical column 'Source Trace' holds a disqualifying placeholder ''"). Attempts 2–4 each carried F509's line ("T3D.2 row 7 has 11 cells under a 12-cell header …") and the vendor's; the model rewrote row 7's text each time and kept it 11 wide. Attempt 4 was refused first on a dangling `[C40]` with 39 citations: it replaced citations 31–32 and dropped one despite the anchoring line's "keep citations … exactly as they were" (model). The check is no false positive (the row is short as the vendor reads it) and the contract is satisfiable: LCR5 passed CP-3C and LCR6's answers cite three of the set's four CP-3C keys. `handoff._width_message` now names the row by its first cell and adds how its last three columns and a long row's first dropped cell read, each the model's own cell cut at a word to 40 characters: "T3D.2 row 7 («CEI Senior Secured Notes due 2032») has 11 cells … (the last column, 'Source Trace', reads empty); as read, 'Refinancing Pressure' holds «Long-dated maturity, but collateral…», 'Credit Implication' holds «10-Q p. 9; Indenture p. 42.» and 'Source Trace' holds nothing". A cell that hides text or will not cross `BoundaryText` drops the quotes, never the line. A cell with no space is cut at 40 characters; a line whose readout would pass `MAX_FEEDBACK_CHARS` keeps F509's sentence without it, never cut mid-quote. The quotes go into the retry request only, as the vendor's lines and D104's carried answer do. Advisory only: acceptance is unchanged. Evidence: `tests/test_register_width.py`.
- D109 (2026-10-06, owner approval of 6 October 2026 of the controller's N146 recommendation, then the owner's ruling of 6 October on CP-2G's horizon; Task T-N146; N144, N146) — A run carries a pinned command: an optional, closed, per-module `qualifiers` map and an optional CP-0 `objective`, in run-input format version 3 and its `input_fingerprint` (invariant 10), delivered as a host-owned "current command" section to the named node only. **Owner's ruling, as relayed:** "The CP-2G forecast horizon is always derived from the run's latest reporting period: Latest period Q1, Q2 or Q3 of year Y: horizon FY(Y), FY(Y+1), FY(Y+2). Example: Q1-26 gives FY26, FY27, FY28. Latest period Q4 or FY of year Y: horizon FY(Y+1), FY(Y+2), FY(Y+3). Example: Q4-26 or FY26 gives FY27, FY28, FY29." **Base period:** the LTM to the latest reported quarter (`Q2 2026 LTM`), or that fiscal year (`FY2026`) when the latest period is Q4 or FY. The owner stated no base period; this rule is the controller's reading of the vendor card's own example (`cp-2g-forward-credit-model/SKILL.md:27`, `[forecast horizon: FY26-FY28] [base period: Q1 2026 LTM]`). **Rule** (`caos/methodology/qualifiers.py`): the names a module takes are closed (`QUALIFIER_FIELDS`: CP-0 `objective`; CP-2G `forecast_horizon`, `base_period`, `cases`), and CP-2G's must also be stage fields of its verified `SKILL.md` UX contract (`ux_stage_fields`, read at the pin, invariant 4); any other module or name, a module the pinned route does not carry, a name stated twice, or a value that is not one bracket-free line of at most 1 KiB refuses `RUN_QUALIFIER_INVALID`. On a route carrying CP-2G (FULL_CREDIT_ASSESSMENT, RELATIVE_VALUE, DISTRESSED_RESTRUCTURING) a horizon or base the pin does not state is derived from the subject's `reporting_period` (`derived_scope`: `FY2025`, `FY 2025`, `FY25`, `Q2 2026`, `Q2-26`; any other spelling refuses `REPORTING_PERIOD_UNREADABLE` at the pin, never a guess) and stored marked `derived`; a stated value is marked `pinned` and always wins; `cases` is never derived. The stored command is re-read on every load: canonical, closed, every module on the pinned route, and every `derived` value exactly the rule over the pinned period, else `RUN_INPUT_INVALID`. **Wire break:** `PinRunInput`'s body shape changed: `qualifiers` and `objective` are now required (`wire.py:1600-1607`), so a client posting only `{subject, research}` gets 400 `REQUEST_INVALID`.
- F523 (2026-10-06, Task T-N146; D109, N144, N146) — The host had no channel for a vendor command qualifier, so every route through CP-2G stopped Blocked or ended Restricted with no forecast (FCA2, RV1; RCA-CASE §1). **Store:** migration `0045_run_command` adds `run_inputs.command_json` and admits format version 3 (subject plus command; `run_inputs_command_by_format` keeps it NULL for 1 and 2); every new pin is version 3 (`RunCommand`), versions 1 and 2 keep their bytes, keys and fingerprints. **Prompt:** `HostIdentity.current_command` carries the node's own part; `icm/shared/prompt/current_command.md` renders the vendor's card (`Run CP-2G [forecast horizon: FY2026-FY2028] [base period: Q2 2026 LTM]`) and who stated each value, inside the tag, for CP-0 and CP-2G only when the command names them (a conditional block in their stage contracts; host pins regenerated by `scripts/host_manifest.py`). No `prompt` golden moved: the goldens build identities without a command. **Record:** the identity's `current_command` is written only when set, so `RECORD_CODEC_VERSION` is 3 and `scripts/rollback_check.py` refuses a rollback across it. **Wire:** `PinRunInput` gains `qualifiers` (`RunQualifier` list, at most 4) and `objective`, both required as every request field is; the workspace sends none yet (N159). **Harness:** cases take `qualifiers` and `objective` keys, digested only when present (no committed set digest moved), pinned and compared at eligibility (`case_command`, `stated_in`). The FCA set states nothing: its Q2 2026 period yields FY2026–FY2028 and Q2 2026 LTM. Tests in `tests/test_qualifiers.py` (the rule, exhaustive over its whole domain), `tests/test_run_command.py`, `test_the_pin_takes_a_command_and_answers_its_mistakes_as_the_callers`, `test_a_case_states_its_command_and_an_unknown_qualifier_refuses_the_file`.
- F524 (2026-10-06, T-N146 fix round 1; mx-adversary findings 1, 3 and 4 on D109) — A command value could carry text no reader sees, and a bracket lookalike could fake a card entry. Unicode tag characters, U+FEFF and U+200B passed `qualifiers._text` (it asked `BoundaryText`, which keeps them by design), so they were pinned, shown in the gate preview an approver cannot read them in, and prompted to CP-0 and CP-2G, where a model echoing them would be refused `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` for the host's defect (AI-2, EV-5). Only ASCII `[` `]` were refused, so `［cases: …］` could pose as a second card entry. Now every value and the objective are refused `RUN_QUALIFIER_INVALID` when `hides_text` finds anything (with the one-line rule this is `handoff.INVISIBLE` too) or when they carry any open or close punctuation but the parentheses; `forecast_horizon` must be one FY range in one width, end not before start (`FY2026-FY2028`, `FY26-FY28`), `base_period` the rule's forms (`Q2 2026 LTM`, `FY2025`), and `cases` at most 64 letters, `/`, `-` and spaces. The same rule re-reads a stored command. The pin command composes each value and the objective to NFC at the edge, as `_composed_brief` does (W4), where it refused a decomposed objective. Tests `test_hidden_text_in_any_command_value_is_refused`, `test_each_cp2g_value_has_its_own_grammar`, `test_an_objective_refuses_bracket_lookalikes_and_decomposed_text`, `test_an_objective_keeps_parentheses_and_composed_text`, `test_hidden_text_never_reaches_the_pin_preview_or_prompt`, and the `hidden` and NFD cases of `test_the_pin_takes_a_command_and_answers_its_mistakes_as_the_callers`.
- F525 (2026-10-06, T-N146 fix round 1; mx-adversary finding 1, the same gap before D109) — A run subject's `issuer_name` and `reporting_period` could carry text no reader sees: `run_inputs._subject_text` asked `BoundaryText`, which keeps tag characters, U+FEFF, U+200B and U+2060 by design, so they reached every node's host-owned front matter and the gate preview, and a model copying the front matter back exactly would be refused `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` for the host's defect (AI-2, EV-5). `_subject_text` now also refuses what `hides_text` finds (with its one-line rule, that is `handoff.INVISIBLE` too), so `valid_subject` refuses it for the API (`REQUEST_INVALID`) and the qualification loader (`QUALIFICATION_SET_FILE_INVALID`), the pin refuses `RUN_INPUT_INVALID`, and a stored pin carrying one no longer verifies on load. Predates this branch: the subject has been pinned this way since §42.1 (migration 0011). Tests `test_a_subject_with_hidden_text_is_not_valid_but_a_visible_one_is` and the five new cases of `test_an_invalid_subject_refuses_before_anything_is_pinned`.
- F526 (2026-10-06, T-N146 fix round 1; mx-adversary finding 2 on D109) — A qualification set whose command no pin would take was admitted and half-written. `harness.assert_admissible`, which `scripts/qualify.py` asks before it creates a database, judged no command, so a CP-2G route whose period the owner's rule cannot read (`1H26`), a CP-2G qualifier on a route without CP-2G, or CP-0's objective stated both as a qualifier and as `objective` passed it, and `prepare` then created, admitted, started and pinned the cases ahead of the faulty one (two cases, two runs and one pinned input) before refusing. `assert_admissible` now asks `_commands`: each case's command judged by the pin's own `run_inputs.pinned_run_command` against its resolved route and subject, so the set refuses `RUN_QUALIFIER_INVALID` or `REPORTING_PERIOD_UNREADABLE` with nothing written; and the case-file loader refuses the objective stated both ways (`on_disk._command`, `QUALIFICATION_SET_FILE_INVALID`). Docs: N144 and the FCA RESULT.md say a fresh run (FCA3), since FCA2's pin predates D109 and cannot resume into a command; the `RunQualifier` docstring names CP-0's `objective`. Tests `test_a_set_whose_command_cannot_be_pinned_is_refused_before_any_write`, `test_pinned_run_command_is_the_pins_own_command_for_a_route_and_subject`, and the both-ways case of `test_a_case_states_its_command_and_an_unknown_qualifier_refuses_the_file`. **Fix round 2 (re-audit):** F524's bracket deny-list (`Ps`, `Pe`) let square-bracket pieces in `Sm` and `So` through an objective (U+23A1–U+23A6, U+23B4, U+23B5, U+231C–U+231F), so `decision⎦ ⎡forecast horizon: FY2030-FY2032` drew a fake qualifier in CP-0's card. The objective is now a closed allow-list: letters, digits, the ASCII space and `. , ; : ' " - – — / & % $`. Parentheses and combining marks are no longer accepted, and every other value already has its own ASCII grammar, so the deny-list is gone. `run_inputs.run_command` is renamed `pinned_run_command`, because `caos.store.commands.run_command` already names the governed unit (one term per concept). Tests `test_an_objective_refuses_bracket_lookalikes_and_decomposed_text` (the new cases) and `test_an_objective_takes_letters_digits_and_its_own_punctuation`.
- D110 (2026-10-06, owner decision of 6 October 2026: the owner approved the controller's recommendation on every outstanding decision, N148's among them; Task T-N148; RCA-PROV §4) — A drop the provider declared is re-attempted once, automatically, and is no longer a parked run. **Rule:** a node whose call ended `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` as a declared drop (`DropKind.DECLARED`: a vendor error carrying a status or the provider's own error object -- a response body or an SSE `error` event -- with nothing generated) gets one automatic re-attempt in all (`canonical.DROP_REATTEMPTS = 1`), in `runtime._with_second_attempt`, gated on the ledger like D30's guided retry: `canonical.drop_reattempt_due` reads `node_attempts` and is due when the latest attempt is a declared drop, unexplained or explained `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`, and the node holds at most one declared drop in all. Never a call abandoned at its `deadline`, one the client ended by raising (`raised`, `escaped`, including a non-streamed `200` whose body is only an error, which the client cannot parse), a `vendor` error with neither status nor error object (a reset, a client timeout), or a stream the provider failed after it had begun to answer. **Money and ordering:** the re-attempt is an ordinary pass -- a fresh attempt and a fresh reservation beside the dropped attempt's, which is never released (invariant 8); a ceiling that cannot cover both leaves `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` standing, as a guided retry's ceiling leaves its refusal. Invariant 6: the accepted-attempt ledger stays the truth and a node is accepted once; a crash between the drop and the re-attempt leaves the next pass's fresh attempt as the re-attempt, and a second declared drop at the node, before or after a crash or an operator's requeue, stops the run. A drop owes nothing (`_UNSETTLED`), so it never holds its node. The re-attempt spends no guided retry, and a dropped guided retry is repeated as that guided retry (`_feedback_source` passes over declared drops). **Supersedes** RCA-PROV §4's "a drop is a run stop by design". Tests: `tests/test_drop_reattempt.py`, `tests/test_postgres_races.py::test_a_crash_between_a_declared_drop_and_its_re_attempt_accepts_once` and its `a declared drop` shape in `test_a_charged_answer_holds_its_node_exactly_when_the_next_pass_settles_it`.
- F527 (2026-10-06, Task T-N148; D110; F513's kind, typed) — The seam says how an unanswered call ended. `Completion.drop_kind` (`DropKind`: `declared`, `vendor`, `raised`, `escaped`, `deadline`; None for an answered call and for any refusal whose ending was not stated) is set by `models._unanswered` beside F513's stderr line, which is unchanged (`call=vendor` for both vendor kinds). `models._declared` splits a vendor error: a status of 100 to 9999 or a non-empty error object is declared, an `APIConnectionError` never is, and an error that raises while it is read is not (fail closed: no re-attempt). `DropKind` is the ledger's vocabulary, so it is defined in `caos/store/outcomes.py` and re-exported by `caos.provider`: the seam depends on the store, never the reverse. Tests: `test_an_unanswered_call_carries_how_it_ended`, `test_a_deadline_and_an_escape_are_never_declared`, `test_an_answered_call_states_no_drop`.
- F528 (2026-10-06, Task T-N148; D110) — The kind reaches the ledger as a typed field. Migration `0046_call_drop_kind` adds `call_outcomes.drop_kind` (the five values) and `call_outcomes_drop_unanswered`: a row with a drop kind has no charge, no generation and no stored body, whoever writes it. `CallOutcome.drop_kind` is written by `_record`, compared on an exact replay (a different kind is `CALL_OUTCOME_CONFLICT`) and checked by `outcomes._validate_drop` (`CALL_OUTCOME_INVALID` for an untyped kind or one beside a charge, generation or body). `execute_handoff` carries it through `canonical._drop_kind`, which keeps a provider's stated kind only for a refused call that left none of the three: a provider that states a drop beside a charge is believed on the money and the bill commits, with no re-attempt. `NodeAttempt.drop_kind` is what `node_attempts` reads. Test: `test_the_outcome_keeps_its_drop_kind_and_refuses_one_beside_an_answer`.
- F529 (2026-10-06, Task T-N148; D110; test adapter only) — A stream error after the provider began to answer is not a declared drop. `tests/openrouter_adapter.py` raised the client's `APIError` for an SSE `error` event whether or not content had streamed first, so the seam could not tell an error before any content from a cut after it. `OpenRouterChat._generate` now notes the first frame that carries anything generated (`generating`: a delta value under any key but `role`; a reasoning delta counts, since the provider had begun and may bill it) and raises `CutAfterContentError` from the `APIError` after it; the error keeps the provider's error object as its body, so F513's line still says its code and type (`call=raised class=CutAfterContentError cause=APIError ... error_code=502 error_type=provider_unavailable`). Before any content the `APIError` passes as before and is declared. Tests: `test_a_mid_stream_error_is_unavailable_and_carries_no_text` (both shapes), `test_only_a_frame_carrying_something_generated_has_begun`.
- F530 (2026-10-06, Task T-N148 fix round 1; the adversarial audit of D110, probes in `scratchpad/audit-n148/`; closes N162 (1)) — Four findings, each fixed test-first. **(1) A crash after the re-attempt's drop bought a third call.** When the worker died after the re-attempt's declared drop was billed but before the stop was written, the resume pass started a fresh attempt, because nothing before a call read the drop count. `outcomes.drop_stop_owed` is now asked before any attempt (since round 2, by `runs._start` under the run lock): if the node's latest attempt is a declared drop, the node holds more than `DROP_REATTEMPTS` of them, and no `RUN_PARKED` event stands later in the run's stream than that drop's own outcome, the pass refuses `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` with no call, and the worker parks the run as the dead worker would have done. The two are told apart by stream order: the operator's requeue can only follow a park, and that park sits after the drop, so the requeue's pass calls again. To read that order, each outcome now names the seq of its own `CALL_OUTCOME_RECORDED` event (`call_outcomes.recorded_seq`, in 0046, required on every drop; `events.append` returns the seq it took, and `_record` appends before it inserts). A run with no work row belongs to a direct caller, and its rerun is that caller's own decision. A crash after the re-attempt's call but before its bill leaves no outcome, so it is no declared drop; the next pass makes an ordinary third call, as after any crash mid-call, and every call keeps its reservation (three at CP-0, five in the LITE run). **(2) A 4xx counted as a drop.** `declared` was recorded beside any refusal, so a 400, 402, 413 or 422 (`PROVIDER_CALL_INVALID`) spent the node's re-attempt, and `_feedback_source` resent the same guided retry on every requeue. `declared` is now recorded only beside `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` (`models._unanswered`, and `canonical._drop_kind` for a provider's stated kind). Both readers go through `canonical.declared_drop`, which requires the refusal to be `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` or not yet written. **(3) N162 (1): an unreadable vendor error escaped untyped.** A `status_code` (or `response`, or `headers`) that raised when read escaped `ChatCompletions.complete` ahead of the bill: the run parked INTERNAL_FAULT with no outcome and no F513 line. These reads now go through `models._read`, which raises the private `_UnreadableError`. `_vendor_ended` turns that into `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`, kind `raised`, so the call is recorded and named on stderr; a `Retry-After` whose `get` raises waits the default. **(4) A 200 counted as declared.** `_declared` now accepts only a failure status, 400 to 599 (`FAILURE_STATUSES`). When no status exists at all, it accepts a non-empty mapping as the error's `body`, never as an argument; a `200` the client could not validate is `vendor`. Tests: `tests/test_drop_reattempt.py` (the seam's statuses and bodies, the ledger with a declared 4xx, `test_a_4xx_on_a_guided_retry_is_not_repeated_as_that_guided_retry`, `test_a_4xx_does_not_spend_the_nodes_re_attempt`, `test_a_vendor_error_that_cannot_be_read_is_unavailable_and_recorded`) and `tests/test_postgres_races.py` (`test_a_crash_after_the_re_attempts_drop_makes_no_third_call`, `test_a_crash_after_the_re_attempts_call_before_its_bill_keeps_every_reservation`). **Round 2 (the re-audit, `audit-n148/t/test_audit_n148_rerace.py`):** (5) The stop was decided before the lock. A pass read the ledger while another worker's re-attempt was still in flight; that drop was billed (bills are not lease-fenced) before the start, and `_UNSETTLED` counts a recorded drop as settled. The result was a third call with no park. `DROP_REATTEMPTS`, `declared_drop` and `drop_stop_owed` now live in `caos/store/outcomes.py`, and `runs._start` asks the rule under the run lock in the unit that would insert the attempt, refusing `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` with nothing written; the read-time `runtime._refuse_spent_drop` is gone. The in-flight window: while the holder keeps its call hold (`CALL_HOLD_SECONDS`, 1,200 s, past the 420 s call), `_UNSETTLED` keeps the node. Past that, `outcomes._counted` gives a drop billed after a later attempt at its node a row but no kind, so a wedged holder's late drop can neither spend the re-attempt nor owe a stop. That third call is the indeterminate one of a crash before the bill, with its reservation held. (6) A status is compared only once it is an `int` itself (`_rate_limited`, `_status_refusal`), so a status whose `==` or hash raises is no status: `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`, `vendor`. Tests: `test_a_re_attempts_drop_billed_after_another_workers_read_makes_no_third_call` (two workers, two connections, threads), `test_a_drop_billed_after_the_node_moved_on_is_kept_but_not_counted`, `test_a_status_that_raises_when_compared_is_no_status`. **Round 3 (re-audit P1, P1b):** `_record` ran `_counted` before its replay comparison, so a drop written with its kind and then replayed exactly after a later attempt started (`bill`'s retry after a lost commit answer) was refused `CALL_OUTCOME_CONFLICT`. A replay is now compared before any counting, against the outcome as given or the same bill with its kind withheld; `_counted` applies only on insert. Tests: `test_an_exact_replay_of_a_kept_drop_after_a_later_start_is_a_no_op`, `test_a_bill_retried_after_a_lost_ack_and_a_later_start_is_a_no_op`.
- D111 (2026-10-06, owner decision of 6 October 2026 approving the controller's recommendation on N151 and N154, with authority to edit the vendor bundle for these changes only; Task T-R13) — Vendor fork r13: a line opening with a register's ID beats a line only mentioning it, a fixture marker counts only in a front-matter value or written alone on a line, and a critical cell a short row left empty is named with the row's width. The three rules are F531 (N151 V1–V3), F532 (V4) and F533 (N154), all in `skills/cp-0-source-readiness/scripts/completeness_check.py`, synced to its 22 copies. The canon's register-binding paragraph (`CANON_SHARED.md` § TABLE_DESIGN_STANDARDS, D89's and D103's) now reads: "a register table is found by the four non-blank lines directly above its header row: the nearest that opens with a register ID (`#### T6 — Evidence Trace`, `**T6 — Evidence Trace**`), else the nearest that names one, headings before captions each time. … The first table found keeps the ID, except that a table under a heading or caption opening with the ID takes it from one found only by a line that mentions the ID without opening with it." **Replay** (`scratchpad/r13/replay.py`, old checker from `d95cea8`, new from this fork) of the 368 distinct stored answers in the qualification blob store (366 with a module skill), through `find_registers` (the contract's IDs and the default pattern) and `check()`: one binding moves, `d2688647` CP-0 T8, from the summary table under "Analytical read-through" (`Source | Family | …`) to the answer's own `**T8 — Recommended Run Command Sheet**` table (`Sequence | Module | …`), as RCA-OPS predicted for V2; it is silent in the check (CP-0's registers declare no columns) and no qualification set keys CP-0 T8, so no score moves. No V1, V3 or V4 binding or message moves in any stored answer. `check()` changes in 27 answers, 46 lines, each "critical column 'X' holds a disqualifying placeholder ''" on a short row becoming "critical column 'X' is empty because the row has n cells under a m-cell header" (LCR4's and LCR6's T3D.2 rows among them); every answer keeps its count of violations, so no verdict changes. Through the re-pinned host, LCR6's CP-3C answers `5335fa9a` and `8101b818` now carry F522's host line and the vendor's "T3D.2 row 7: critical column 'Source Trace' is empty because the row has 11 cells under a 12-cell header". **Readers checked:** `find_registers` (changed) and the host's `caos.qualification.matrix.module_registers`, which calls it (same reading, no host change); `handoff._width_faults` matches `find_registers`' tables by header and rows (unchanged shape: the cell counts live in the new private `_locate_registers`, which `check()` reads); `cp_tables.read_tables`, the host's `caos.methodology.tables.handoff_tables` and CP-MODEL's `parse_stable_tables` bind by table-id comment or marker (unchanged); the vendor's `_register_titles` and the host's F520 `_writes_id` read IDs by their own patterns (unchanged). **Re-pin** (D31's procedure): `python3 -B vendor/deploy-v/verify_package.py --refresh` (111 vendor tests, 2 integration skips, 10 self-checks, PASS; no authority component moved, so no `--rebuild-authorities`, and the authority bundle stays `97106f19`), build `d8a307a9` (was `9043ba7f`), manifest `812ba297` (was `e63b44b4`); `uv run python scripts/host_manifest.py` (the manifest pin in `bundle_pin.py`; no host prompt text moved, `HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` unchanged); the build and manifest pins in `test_bundle_pin.py`, `test_methodology_bundle.py`, `test_loop_charges.py`, `test_canonical_proof.py`, `test_qualification_matrix.py`; `MEASURED` +83 bytes each, the canon's paragraph (CP-0 135,504, CP-L10 137,976, CP-5 170,803). Parity, by D34's procedure (a copy of the legacy snapshot with `vendor/deploy-v` synced from `d95cea8` and the host's text mirrored, verified first to reproduce all 27 `prompt` and 16 `forecast` goldens byte for byte, then synced from this fork): all 27 `prompt` cases move by the canon's paragraph alone (+81 characters each, 83 bytes with the added em dash), with the canon's SHA-256 in its authority marker and each prompt's section tag, which is derived from its content; the `forecast` group is unchanged. A record accepted under `9043ba7f` refuses `ORCHESTRATION_BUILD_MOVED` as on every re-pin; the delivered authority digests of every module handed the canon move (new records only). Evidence: `vendor/deploy-v/tests/test_regressions.py::ForkR13Tests`; `ForkR6Tests::test_every_register_bound_before_is_bound_the_same` and `ForkR7Tests::test_the_locator_binds_as_r6_but_under_a_retired_heading` now hold the locator against a declarative r13 reading (`_r13_candidates`, `_r13_find_registers`) and assert that a binding r12 made by a line opening with the ID moves only to another table such a line claims.
- F531 (2026-10-06, D111, N151 V1–V3; RCA-OPS's minimal inputs; fork r13) — A register's own heading or caption takes its ID from a table a line only mentioning the ID had bound, and wins its table's window from a nearer mention. Fork r12 let only a heading opening with the ID displace, and only a table a prose mention bound, and the window took the nearest heading naming any ID before any prose: (V1) `### Bridge T4.4 to T4.5` over a two-column table bound T4.4 by heading and kept it, so the `#### T4.4 — Income Statement` register below read "missing column"; (V2) a table under "The summary below draws on TL10.2." kept TL10.2 from the `**TL10.2 — Covenant register**` table below; (V3) under `**T4.4 — Income Statement**` and `Figures reconcile to T4.5.` the table bound T4.5 and T4.4 was "missing". `find_registers` now (a) gives the table to the nearest heading opening with an ID, else the nearest caption opening with one, else, as before, the nearest heading then the nearest prose line naming one (a retired heading still keeps prose out, fork r7); (b) marks as a mention any binding whose line, heading or prose, does not open with the ID; (c) lets a table under a heading or caption opening with the ID take a mention's ID, near or at a distance, the first such table winning. "Opens with" is one test for headings and captions: past heading marks, `*`/`_` emphasis and a backtick, or a snake_case register's title on a heading (r12 let headings skip no backtick). Unchanged: a binding a line opening with the ID made is never displaced; a mention displaces nothing; two led tables keep the first. The replay moves one stored binding (D111). Evidence: `ForkR13Tests::test_v1_a_heading_only_mentioning_the_id_gives_way_to_one_opening_with_it`, `::test_v2_a_caption_opening_with_the_id_takes_it_from_a_mention`, `::test_v3_a_line_opening_with_an_id_beats_a_nearer_mention_in_the_window`, each asserting the r12 reading's wrong binding first.
- F532 (2026-10-06, D111, N151 V4; fork r13) — A fixture marker is a front-matter value or a line of its own, never a phrase in prose. `_fixture_violations` refused a declared `fixture_document_substrings_casefold` substring anywhere in the unfenced body, so "The pack is issuer filings, not an integration fixture or synthetic test input." drew two "fixture marker" violations. It now matches a substring in any front-matter string value (list items and nested values included, one per line so none spans two), or a body line that, its heading, quote and list marks, emphasis, backticks and closing `.`/`:`/`;`/`!` dropped and spacing collapsed, equals the substring (casefolded): "Integration fixture.", `> **Integration Fixture**`, `1. Integration fixture`. Fenced lines stay out, and the front-matter flag and warning checks are unchanged. A sentence that names a marker ("Built from an Integration Fixture pack.") no longer disqualifies; the vendor self-check and `tests/test_bundle_pin.py::test_the_fixture_markers_are_split_from_the_thin_evidence_marker`, which expected it to, now assert the new rule. 0 of the 368 stored answers hit the old or the new rule. Evidence: `ForkR13Tests::test_v4_a_fixture_marker_is_a_front_matter_value_or_a_line_of_its_own`.
- F533 (2026-10-06, D111, N154; runs LCR4 and LCR6 CP-3C, F509, F522; fork r13) — The vendor's message for a critical cell a short row left empty names the row's cell count against the header's. `find_registers` pads a short row, so a merged cell pushed the source trace into Credit Implication and the check said "T3D.2 row 7: critical column 'Source Trace' holds a disqualifying placeholder ''" for a row whose source trace the model had written. Where a blocklisted empty value comes only from padding (the column's last header position lies past the row's own cells), the line is now "T3D.2 row 7: critical column 'Source Trace' is empty because the row has 11 cells under a 12-cell header". Message only: the same cells violate, one line each, so acceptance is unchanged; a cell written empty keeps the placeholder message, and a long row or a short row that empties no critical cell is judged as before (F522's host line remains the only notice of those, N163). The cell counts come from `_locate_registers`, which `find_registers` now wraps; its return shape is unchanged. Evidence: `ForkR13Tests::test_n154_a_short_register_row_names_its_width_against_the_header`, the vendor self-check, `tests/test_register_width.py::test_a_short_register_row_is_named_before_the_vendors_empty_cell`.
- F534 is unused: the numbering passes from F533 to F535 (D112 sits between).
- D112 (2026-10-06, owner decision of 6 October 2026: the owner approved the controller's recommendation on every outstanding decision; N156 option (b), run LCR7) — CP-0's P5 page-coverage claims are held to the pages the host delivered. **Evidence:** LCR7's accepted CP-0 (`caos_qualify_052f7dec…`, attempt 2, artifact `e6927a60…`) gave the conformed credit agreement `c27bddbf…`, delivered WHOLE at 67 pages, "1–59 shown", judged it BLOCKED because "the delivered text ends at the Section 8.16 heading", and marked CP-L10 DO NOT RUN; the host had delivered all 3,981 lines, Section 9.27 on page 66 included (N156, RCA-PAGES). **Design:** `caos/methodology/coverage.py` reads, bounded, the P5 register's table rows (at most `MAX_P5_LINES` lines past its heading, up to the next heading); a row only when its first cell is a source id, and a claim only when a whole cell, or one `;`- or sentence-separated part of it no longer than `MAX_CLAIM_CHARS`, is a range from page 1 (`1–59 shown`, `Pages 1–67`, `page anchors 1–6 shown`, `Evidence pages 1–34`). A claim the parser cannot read is not a fault; nor is a range past the last page. `canonical._answer` refuses a CP-0 answer `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` when a source delivered WHOLE (not a page map) is claimed to end below its last delivered page (`_coverage_faults`, after F497's demand check, the same producer-guard shape), and the guided retry (D30, D104) carries one `host coverage check:` line per source: its id and filename, the claimed and delivered pages, and the last numbered heading line delivered (`last_heading`: `ARTICLE`/`SECTION`/`Item`/`PART` and a number, then a stop, the line's end or a capitalised title). The prompt goldens, `icm/shared/prompt` and `HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` are unchanged: the line is host feedback text like F497's, not a pinned block. **Replay of the 41 stored CP-0 answers of the 30 surviving live runs:** 23 carry readable claims; LCR7's two attempts fault on `c27bddbf` (59 of 67) only; LCR5 and LCR6 (8 claims each, 1–67 for the credit agreement) do not. Four other accepted answers under-claim a page with content: C6 (34 of 67), FCA1 (3 of 4 and 3 of 6), LFCS1 (5 of 6), LRV1 (58 of 59). Since fix round 1 (F535) only a claim leaving out a page that holds a numbered heading is a fault, so of these only C6 (Sections 2.04 to 9.27 left out) is retried; LRV1's page 59 is lender signatures. Over-claims (nine runs, e.g. LCR6's "1–67" for the 60-page indenture) are left alone (N165). Fix F535.
- F535 (2026-10-06, D112; run LCR7 CP-0, N156) — `coverage.py` (`coverage_claims`, `coverage_faults`, `last_heading`, `coverage_message`) and its two hooks in `canonical.py`: `_coverage_faults` in `_answer` and `_coverage_lines` in `_prompt_context`'s host lines. Tests `tests/test_coverage_check.py`: LCR7's stored P5 register replayed verbatim (one fault, `c27bddbf`, 59 of 67), LCR5's and LCR6's accepted ones (no fault), unreadable and bounded claims, the register and line bounds, the heading reader, the message, and on the three-page `paged` harness a gate answer claiming pages 1–2 refused `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` and retried with the line, then accepted, the route completing. **Fix round 1** (review of D112): (1) a short claim is a fault only when a page past it holds a line `last_heading` reads as a numbered heading (`heading_pages`, the same detector), so a retry of a ~500k-token CP-0 is not spent on pages of signatures or a pricing grid; replaying the 41 stored CP-0 answers, LCR7 (both attempts) and C6 still fault, and FCA1, LFCS1 and LRV1 no longer do. (2) `coverage_message` keeps the line within `MAX_FEEDBACK_CHARS`, dropping the heading and then the filename (F522's way), so `_bounded`'s cut never removes the closing instruction; a 200-character filename and a 160-character heading made a 614-character line before. Tests: the heading rule on LRV1's and C6's shapes, `heading_pages`, the length fallback, and end to end the paged report (no heading) accepted on "1–2 shown" while the same report with `SECTION 3.01.` on page 3 is retried with that heading named. F536 and F537 were not used.
- F536 (2026-10-06, D112, N156 review L1) — `coverage_faults` kept a source's first readable range, so an unrelated cell such as `| 1-3 |` ahead of `Pages 1-67` faulted the source at 3 and misquoted the claim in the retry. It now takes the largest claim per source. Test `test_an_unrelated_early_range_does_not_hide_the_fullest_claim`.
- D113 (2026-10-06, owner decision of 6 October 2026: the owner approved the controller's recommendation on every outstanding decision; N153 and N158, Task T-N153) — A run's evidence is laid out in a stable order and opens every prompt, so a run's calls share it as a prefix a provider's prompt cache can read. **Evidence:** N152: 230 of 231 live calls carried a cache-write charge and 1 read the cache. Rebuilt offline with the host's own builders (scratch `n153/measure.py`, read-only on the run databases, under the r12 bundle the runs were pinned to), two nodes' prompts shared 40 bytes and a retry about 1.7 KB with its first attempt: the section tag, a digest over the whole prompt with its front matter, stood in the first 200 bytes. And LCR2 to LCR7's identical CP-0 requests laid the credit agreement out anywhere from token 58,017 to 284,852 (N158), because evidence was ordered by the `source_id` minted at random on admission. **Design:** (1) `read_run_blocks` orders the pin by filename (`COLLATE "C"`, so no server locale reorders it), then document digest, `source_id`, block (F538). (2) The EVIDENCE section opens the prompt, its content unchanged, before the instruction, the tag rule, the front matter and every other section, which keep their order and the whole-prompt tag (F539). (3) Its two markers carry their own tag, `evidence_tag`: the digest of the evidence section the run's whole pin renders, so every node opens alike whatever part of the pin its gate row hands it, and repeats every other node's bytes for as long as their evidence agrees (F540). No evidence line can carry the digest of a pin it is part of. The three host blocks that said where the evidence was now say where it is: `instruction.md` (the evidence "opens this prompt, above"; the citation rule is in the final check "below"), `tagged.md` (names the evidence's tag, and only the two EVIDENCE lines that open the prompt carry it as host markers) and `current_command.md` ("the evidence above"). **Parity:** the one divergence from the legacy assembly is this move, recorded in `tests/parity/test_prompt.py` and `tests/parity/cases.py`. The 27 `prompt` goldens are regenerated by D34's procedure, from a copy of the legacy snapshot whose prompt text and fixtures are mirrored from this tree and whose `build_handoff_prompt` mirrors the move and nothing else (scratch `n153/regen.sh`, `n153/mirror_order.py`), first verified to reproduce all 27 `prompt` and 16 `forecast` goldens of `8e31d70` byte for byte. Every case moves by +144 characters, the three blocks' wording, with its evidence bytes equal; `forecast` is unchanged. `HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` and `host_pin.py` are regenerated (`scripts/host_manifest.py`), so a model-route run pinned before this commit refuses `AUTHORITY_BYTES_MISMATCH` at its next node, as on every host re-pin. **Measured offline** (same builders; before is `8e31d70`, after is this branch), bytes each call shares with the call before it, summed: E3 (4 calls, 3.36 MB) 1,778 to 1,903,653; LCR6 (7 calls, 17.2 MB) 6,862 to 13,292,300; FCA2 (22 calls, 32.9 MB) 17,569 to 14,459,267, and with the best earlier call 17,574 to 19,524,501. Every retry now repeats its first attempt's evidence (LCR6 CP-3C attempts 2 to 4: 2,215,935 bytes each). A FULL route's consumers share only as far as their selections agree from the first member (FCA2 CP-1, CP-1A, CP-2E, CP-2: 45 bytes). Across runs over the same documents the evidence is laid out alike (LCR5, LCR6 and LCR7 give one digest once each `source_id` is replaced by its filename, against three before), but it is not byte-identical, because each admission mints new `source_id`s (N166). **Risk accepted:** the evidence tag can be reproduced. No document line can carry it, since it is the pin's own digest, but a run's earlier answers have seen it. An upstream handoff can therefore copy the two EVIDENCE marker lines, tag and all, around a forged line, and a consumer meets them inside UPSTREAM. The tag rule makes that copy content of the UPSTREAM section, and the tag rule itself now follows the evidence. The risk is accepted because a citation is anchored only against the evidence the host delivered to the node, never against prompt text (invariant 11): a quote of the forged line is kept unverified (`test_copied_evidence_markers_in_an_upstream_anchor_nothing`). The whole-prompt tag still cannot be reproduced, since it covers the front matter and the attempt. **Live probes (controller, 6 October; nine calls to `openai/gpt-6-luna` through OpenRouter, provider pinned to openai, on E3 prompts sharing their first 633,996 bytes, scratch `n153/cache_probe.py` and variants):** (a) automatic caching, with and without `prompt_cache_key` or `session_id`: every call wrote the whole prompt to the cache (`cache_write_tokens` about the prompt) and read nothing, an identical prompt resent within minutes included; (b) explicit mode (`prompt_cache_options: {mode: "explicit", ttl: "30m"}`, a `prompt_cache_breakpoint` on a text block holding the shared prefix): the write shrank to the prefix (153k tokens), and the next call with the same prefix still read 0; (c) caching off (explicit mode, no breakpoint): `cache_write_tokens` 0 and $0.0202 against $0.0251, 20% less on input. OpenRouter documents cache writes for GPT-5.6+ at 1.25x and reads at 0.25x or 0.5x, not 0.1x. On this provider, therefore, the evidence-first order gives no measured cache saving. It stays for layout stability across runs and retries, and for a production endpoint that may cache (unmeasured), and the test adapter turns caching off (F545). Fixes F538 to F540 and F545.
- F538 (2026-10-06, D113, N158) — `caos/evidence/read.py` `_RUN_BLOCKS_QUERY` orders by `filename COLLATE "C"`, `document_sha256`, `source_id`, `block_id`, where it ordered by `source_id`. Tests `tests/test_run_evidence.py`: a pack whose ids are minted against filename order (`ingest.uuid4` patched), with two members sharing a filename and one that sorts first only by byte value, is delivered by filename, digest and block; the same pack under ids drawn in opposite orders in two cases lays out alike; the existing one-query read now expects filename order. Both new tests failed on the old order.
- F539 (2026-10-06, D113, N153) — `build_handoff_prompt` opens with the EVIDENCE section under its own tag and moves nothing else; `icm/shared/prompt/instruction.md`, `tagged.md` and `current_command.md` say where the evidence now is; pins and goldens as D113 states. Tests `tests/test_handoff_invocation.py`: the evidence opens the prompt under its own tag, its digest, which the tag rule names; a node's second attempt, its guided retry, another node and the gate each start with the first call's whole evidence block, and other evidence does not; the forged-marker count now counts the two tags apart. Test doubles and helpers that read a prompt's first words or its first front-matter marker read the front matter past the evidence, as the tag rule tells a model (`canonical_fixtures.fields_from_prompt`, `_module` in `test_second_attempt.py`, `test_canonical_runtime.py`, the qualification harness's `_Completions`, which took the last `source_id:` line as the evidence's), `test_hidden_text.py` masks both tags, and tests that sliced "from the EVIDENCE marker to the end" slice the opening section.
- F540 (2026-10-06, D113, N153) — `invocation.evidence_tag(delivered, pack_tag=None)` (the given pin tag, else the digest of what `delivered` renders, so `build_handoff_prompt` gains no branch and stays at complexity 15) and `build_handoff_prompt(pack_tag=...)`; `canonical._context` derives the tag from the run's whole pin before `_selected` narrows it and `_prompt` passes it. Measured on FCA2 before this fix, a tag over each node's own selection parted two nodes at the tag itself (13 bytes shared). Tests: on the paged harness, a consumer handed pages 1-2 opens with the gate's marker line and its whole evidence is a prefix of the gate's prompt (failed before: two tags); a `pack_tag` replaces the evidence's own tag in both markers and the tag rule. **Fix round 1** (review Q3): `_context` no longer renders the pin for its tag. - D116 (2026-10-06, owner decision of 6 October 2026: the owner approved the controller's recommendation on every outstanding decision, N157's among them; Task T-N157; N157, N167) — Every node's request is bounded by its model's context, not only by the transport. **Evidence:** FCA3-dec (db `caos_qualify_b36be001…`) failed its first CP-0 call with the router's HTTP 400 after 2 s, nothing billed (F513: `PROVIDER_CALL_INVALID call=vendor class=BadRequestError status=400`). Rebuilt offline with the host's own builders the request is 4,033,648 bytes, the reserved size to the byte, inside `MAX_REQUEST_BYTES`; it is 890,638 o200k tokens, but the router counts prompts at request bytes / 4.02 on all 195 live calls of 3 to 5 October (min 4.019, max 4.030), so about 1,003,400 tokens, and with the 65,536 completion tokens over `openai/gpt-6-luna`'s 1,050,000. **The bound (pinned, declared):** `caos.provider.CONTEXT_TOKENS`, each endpoint's context by the endpoint name a run is priced and called under: `openai/gpt-6-luna`, `openai/gpt-6-luna-pro` and `openai/gpt-6-sol`, 1,050,000 each, the `context_length` of OpenRouter's keyless `GET https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/models` (read by the controller, 2026-10-05). `BYTES_PER_TOKEN_FLOOR = 3`; `request_ceiling(model) = min(MAX_REQUEST_BYTES, (context - MAX_COMPLETION_TOKENS) * 3)`, so 2,953,392 bytes for each. **An endpoint with no declared context keeps `MAX_REQUEST_BYTES`**, the bound every run had before D116, and a run driven on it prints once on stderr `CONTEXT_NOT_DECLARED endpoint=<name>` (the name alone, `-` for any name outside `[A-Za-z0-9._:/@+-]`; once per `run_route` call, so a resumed run prints it again, accepted; a stderr that cannot take it, broken or `None`, is passed over); preflight (E1, `scripts/preflight.py` through `scripts/enterprise_deploy.py`) prints a `WARNING` line, never a failure, for each configured or selectable endpoint without one. **Amended by D119 (6 October): the table now also declares every workspace endpoint below, at 1,000,000 tokens each, source "owner, 2026-10-06"; only `copilot:<model>`, routed variants and unlisted names stay undeclared.** That covered when written every approved workspace endpoint (`grok-4-7`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`, `gpt-6-luna`, `claude-opus-5-5`, `gpt-6-sol`, `deepseek-v4-1-flash`, `gpt-6-astra`, `gemini-3-8-flash`, `glm-5-3`, `glm-5-3-flash`), any `copilot:<model>` (D77), the repo default `databricks-claude-opus-5` (D29's "1M tokens" is not a source) and routed variants such as `:nitro`; declaring them from their own documentation is N170. **Review round 1:** the first version refused an undeclared endpoint `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`, which would have stopped every approved workspace endpoint and every Copilot model that ran before D116; the review caught it, and the fallback above replaced it. The floor is below every measured ratio: 3.82 to 4.64 request bytes per native prompt token over the 195 calls, 4.02 per router token, 4.53 per o200k token on FCA3's CP-0; a pack of numeric tables alone tokenises denser (the fund's N-PORT schedule, 2.54), which N170 keeps open. The context is declared in the host rather than derived from the price, because the price string carries none and a run's pin (F468) names the endpoint, which is the key here. A declared context no wider than the completion leaves no room and refuses `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`. **The fit:** `selection.pack_view` measures the node's first-attempt prompt as the request its model is sent and, past `request_ceiling - RETRY_RESERVE_BYTES` (64 KiB kept for a guided retry's check lines), shows the largest sources as page maps first: one share bound for every source (water-filling), each source past it cut to the largest uniform number of leading lines a page that fits, never below one, so every page still appears; it re-measures and lowers the bound until the request fits, and refuses `CONTEXT_OVER_CEILING` (the existing code) when every mapped source is at one line a page and it still does not, before any attempt or reservation. The gate starts from its §98 per-source bound (`GATE_SOURCE_BYTES`), so a pack that fits is shown exactly as before. Every node is fitted, consumers too (N167): `Assignment.model` carries the endpoint, set by `check_context`, `execute_handoff` (the provider's) and `replay_billed` (the call outcome's), so the pre-call check, the call, its verdict and a replay show the node the same lines; the accepted read that needs only the upstream (`_forecast_inputs`) is not fitted. **Declared to the model:** CP-0's page maps ride its source preparation metadata and `_PAGE_MAP_NOTE`, unchanged, which points to Step I rules 5 and 8 (T8's `<filename> pages <first>-<last>`); a consumer gets a new host section, `HOST EVIDENCE DELIVERY`, listing each mapped source with its map fields, stating that no withheld line is evidence, and that it cannot ask for pages itself, CP-0's T8 being the one channel, whose pages are themselves fitted to the same bound (N171). Both appear only when a map exists, so no prompt golden moved and `HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` is unchanged; the consumer text is a Python constant beside `_PAGE_MAP_NOTE`, its precedent, not a pinned block. **Supersedes** `tests/test_large_documents.py`'s "a 10-K named whole for a consumer is refused before any attempt": under the legacy 1 MiB ceiling that consumer is now shown the 10-K as its page map and accepted. **Measured (FCA3-dec's CP-0, same builders, read-only):** before, 4,033,648 request bytes, 890,638 o200k tokens (about 1,003,400 by the router's count, 1,344,549 at the floor); after, 2,866,918 bytes, 642,416 o200k tokens (about 713,200 by the router, 955,639 at the floor, each plus 65,536 under 1,050,000), with the credit agreement at 24 of 60 lines a page (1,605 of 3,981 lines), the 2032 indenture at 32 (1,915 of 3,567) and the fund prospectus at 49 (3,133 of 3,827), every other source whole. **Live check (controller, 6 October):** that fitted request, 2,866,967 bytes as the adapter sent it, returned HTTP 200 from `openai/gpt-6-luna`, first frame at 14 s. Fixes F546 to F548.
- F546 (2026-10-06, D116, N157) — `caos.provider.CONTEXT_TOKENS`, `BYTES_PER_TOKEN_FLOOR` and `request_ceiling`; `invocation.request_size` and `canonical._sent_prompt` (D104's drop of the refused answer) bound by `request_ceiling(provider.model)` rather than `MAX_REQUEST_BYTES`. - D117 (2026-10-06, owner decision of 6 October 2026; amends D83(1) and D83(2)) — The model-call deadline is 720 s and the worker lease 900 s. Evidence: the live run LCR9-dec (`qualification/czr-2026q2-lite-covenant-refinancing`) stopped at CP-3C on F513's line `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE call=deadline ... elapsed=420.0`; CP-3C at effort high took 327 s in LCR6. The test adapter streams (F511, F512), so a long call sends bytes as it works and is not cut by the gateway while silent. Decision: `caos.provider.TIMEOUT_SECONDS` 420 → 720 and `caos.store.work.LEASE_SECONDS` 600 → 900, so D83(2)'s `LEASE_SECONDS - TIMEOUT_SECONDS >= 180` holds unchanged. Those 180 s are a liveness budget and, after a session cut, the safety margin (F485 reworded): `call_hold` is a session-level lock on a connection idle through the call, so a session the server ends mid-call releases it, and only the live lease then keeps a second worker off the node until the bill lands. Store sessions now turn `idle_session_timeout` off for themselves (`caos.store.IDLE_SESSION_OPTION`), so idleness never ends one; a failover, a suspend or a lost socket still can. What follows from them: `MIN_RESEND_SECONDS` is half the deadline (360 s; two capped waits spend 40 s of the 720); `WORKER_STALE_AFTER` is the deadline plus a minute (780 s, a literal, asserted); E10's `MODEL_CALL_SECONDS` is a 740 s wait that spans event-stream tails: the app closes every tail at `TAIL_DEADLINE` (300 s), so E10 reopens a closed tail with `Last-Event-ID` (`RECONNECT_PAUSE_SECONDS`, the app's 0.5 s retry hint) until its wait runs out, where before it took the first close as its answer and failed at about 300 s; `chat_model` and the test-only OpenRouter adapter carry the constant as their client timeout; `CALL_HOLD_SECONDS` stays two leases (1,800 s past the reservation, 1,200 before), which `_UNSETTLED` reads, and still leaves at least one whole lease past a call that ran to its deadline. Unchanged and why: `require_lease` renews to the lease's own length on every fenced write, the last before the call being the reservation (`check_call` only reads the lease), so nothing renews on a timer and the 180 s still cover `check_call`, the prompt build, the bill, the checks and `accept_attempt`; the worker beats once per node (`BEAT_SECONDS` is the idle cadence only); `call_hold`, `replay_billed` and `drop_stop_owed` hold no time constant of their own; `STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_MS` (30 s) bounds the store's statements, never the call. No parity golden, `app.yaml`, `databricks.yml` or `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` states either number.
- F549 (2026-10-06, D117) — `caos/provider.py` `TIMEOUT_SECONDS` 720.0, `caos/store/work.py` `LEASE_SECONDS` 900 and `WORKER_STALE_AFTER` 780.0, `scripts/enterprise_deploy.py` `MODEL_CALL_SECONDS` 740.0, and the comments that named 420, 600 or 210 in `caos/provider.py`, `caos/store/work.py` and `caos/models.py`; the Copilot plan's operator note says the lease expires after 900 s and its two timeout asserts follow `TIMEOUT_SECONDS` rather than 240. Tests: `tests/test_models.py` pins the deadline (720.0) in three places and `MIN_RESEND_SECONDS` (360.0), and the re-send floor cases are at 630, 717, 358 and 358.5 s; `tests/test_worker.py::test_the_lease_outlives_the_provider_timeout` pins 900 beside the 180 s rule; `tests/test_worker_heartbeat.py` asserts the staleness floor. Two tests encoded the old numbers rather than their rule: `test_one_deadline_bounds_the_whole_call_and_the_lease_outlives_it` had each 429 arrive 100 s late, so at 720 s the third try was allowed and only `RATE_LIMIT_TRIES` stopped it; the lateness is now a quarter of the deadline, with the two bounds that make the re-send floor the stop asserted. `tests/test_worker.py::test_a_call_hold_outlives_the_lease_and_a_whole_call_past_it` pins `CALL_HOLD_SECONDS` at two leases (1,800 s) and asserts it leaves at least one lease past the call's deadline.
- F550 (2026-10-06, D117, adversarial review of `34b6574`) — (1) E10 (`scripts/enterprise_deploy.py` `_answered`, `_tail`, `_event_id`, `RECONNECT_PAUSE_SECONDS`) reopens a tail the app closed, after its last event id, until `MODEL_CALL_SECONDS` runs out; a reopen that is not an event stream fails the row naming what answered. Before, the first close at `TAIL_DEADLINE` was the answer, so any call past 300 s failed E10 with "no model call answered within 740.0s" (pre-existing since D83's 440 s, wider at 740). Tests: `tests/test_enterprise_deploy.py::test_e10_reopens_a_tail_the_server_closed_from_its_last_event_id`, `::test_e10_waits_its_own_budget_across_tails_and_no_longer`, `::test_e10_fails_when_a_reopened_tail_is_not_an_event_stream`; `tests/test_models.py::test_the_deploy_waits_longer_than_the_provider_call` now also asserts the wait spans a tail. (2) `caos.store.IDLE_SESSION_OPTION` (`-c idle_session_timeout=0`) on every `connect`, beside the keepalives already sent: with the database set to end idle sessions, the reviewer's probe had `call_hold` released mid-call and, with the lease lapsed, a second worker pay for the node again; with the option the hold survives and the second worker is refused `ATTEMPT_UNSETTLED` (one attempt, one ledger row). A server that refuses the option refuses the connection, answered `STORE_UNAVAILABLE` as any connect failure (fail closed). Test: `tests/test_store_schema.py::test_a_store_session_is_never_ended_by_the_server_for_being_idle` (a store session survives a 300 ms database idle bound for a second while a plain one is ended), and the options string in `::test_connect_asks_for_keepalives_and_an_optional_statement_timeout`. Round 2 (re-check of `7ff24eb`): (3) the checkpointer pool (`caos/graph/checkpoint.py` `_pooled`) sends `IDLE_SESSION_OPTION` too; it opens its own connections and took the database's idle bound, so a 300 ms bound ended every pooled session and the pool timed out handing one out (`tests/test_checkpoint.py::test_a_pooled_checkpoint_session_is_never_ended_for_being_idle`). (4) Each E10 reopen waits on its socket only for the time left of `MODEL_CALL_SECONDS` (`_tail`'s `seconds`), where it waited the whole 740 s again, about 1,480 s in the worst case; a reopen that fails (`READ_FAILURES`) or is not a stream is followed by one last run read before the row fails (`_last_word`), and the cursor is taken only from a whole frame (`_event_id`). Tests: `tests/test_enterprise_deploy.py::test_e10_bounds_each_reopen_by_the_time_left`, `::test_e10_a_reopen_that_hangs_ends_with_the_wait_and_reads_the_run_last` (a loopback listener that never answers), `::test_e10_reads_the_run_once_more_after_a_reopen_that_failed`, `::test_e10_resumes_after_the_last_complete_frame_only`.
- D118 (2026-10-06, owner decision of 6 October 2026, "re-run"; amends D110) — A provider-declared error that arrives after content has started also gets N148's one automatic re-attempt. Evidence: the live run LCR10-dec (`qualification/czr-2026q2-lite-covenant-refinancing`) stopped at CP-L10 on F513's line `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE call=raised class=CutAfterContentError cause=APIError status=- error_code=502 error_type=provider_unavailable elapsed=96.0`: an upstream 502 delivered as an SSE `error` event after content began, which D110 classed `raised` and never re-attempted. **Rule:** a call the provider failed after content began is a declared drop when the provider's own error object states, as its `code`, a 5xx or a 429, or, with no code, an `error_type` in `provider_unavailable`, `provider_overloaded`, `timeout` or `rate_limit_exceeded` (`server` was dropped by F566). It is re-attempted under every D110 gate unchanged: ledger-gated (`drop_reattempt_due`), a fresh attempt and reservation beside the held one, the spent-drop rule asked under the run lock (`drop_stop_owed` in `runs._start`), and at most one per node, shared with drops declared before content. Never re-attempted, as before: the host `deadline` (a 502 that arrives after it too), a reset or truncation with no provider error frame, `escaped`, a 4xx (a code of 400 to 499 other than 429, whatever type stands beside it), a code that is no status, a connection failure beneath the cut, or an error object that raises when read (fail closed). **Money:** the cut attempt keeps its reservation, which covers any partial bill the provider makes for what it streamed. Nothing from it is accepted: its completion carries no content and no charge, and 0046's `call_outcomes_drop_unanswered`, as amended by 0047 (F559), refuses a drop row with a charge or a stored body. Since F559 a declared cut's row may name the generation id its stream gave, so that its partial bill can be reconciled; no other drop row may. **Ledger kind (since F559):** the kind is `declared_after_content`, not `declared`. It shares the node's one re-attempt (`outcomes.DECLARED_KINDS`), but the call may have been billed for what it streamed. Its row may therefore name the stream's generation id (migration 0047), never a charge or a body. F513's stderr line still says `call=raised class=CutAfterContentError`. Tests: F551 to F553, F559.
- F551 (2026-10-06, D118) — The seam decides whether the provider declared a cut after content. `CutAfterContentError` moves from the test adapter to `caos/provider.py`, so the host owns the contract; `tests/openrouter_adapter.py` raises the same class and re-exports it. `caos/models.py` `_invoked` catches it before ST-8's hold and records `_Raised(_cut_kind(cut), _facts(cut))`. The cut is never sent again inside `complete`: it is not an `OpenAIError`, so the 429 resend loop cannot see it. `_cut_kind` reads the error object through `_error_body` and `_status` (F513's own readers) and returns `declared` or `raised` under the D118 rule, with `_TRANSIENT_ERROR_TYPES`. `_unanswered` keeps F513's line: `call=raised` for a declared cut, `call=vendor` for a vendor error, as before. Tests: `tests/test_drop_reattempt.py::test_a_cut_after_content_is_declared_only_by_a_transient_provider_error` (27 shapes: 502, 503 as digits, 500, 599, 429, the five types; 400, 402, 408, 499, 600, 200, a bool code, a non-status code, a 4xx type, an unknown type, no code and no type, an empty, absent or text body, a reset cause, a body whose read raises), `::test_a_declared_cut_past_the_deadline_is_the_deadline`, and `tests/test_openrouter_adapter.py::test_a_mid_stream_error_is_unavailable_and_carries_no_text[after-partial-output]` (LCR10's frames, now `declared`).
- F552 (2026-10-06, D118) — The run re-attempts a declared cut once and stops on a second drop. Tests: `tests/test_drop_reattempt.py::test_a_declared_drop_is_re_attempted_once_and_accepted_once[a-502-event-after-content]` (two CP-0 calls, two held reservations, one explained drop, one accepted artifact, kinds `declared` then none); `::test_a_second_drop_on_the_re_attempt_stops_the_run` (a cut then a cut, a 503 then a cut, a cut then an error event: two calls, two reservations, nothing accepted); `::test_an_undeclared_drop_stops_the_run_with_no_re_attempt[a-plain-cut]` and `[a-4xx-cut]` (one call, `raised`); and `tests/test_openrouter_adapter.py::test_a_cut_after_content_without_an_error_frame_is_not_declared` (a reset mid-stream after content: `raised`; a stream that stops short: `PROVIDER_RESPONSE_INVALID`, no drop kind; each sent once).
- F553 (2026-10-06, D118) — Exactly-once and the reservation counts across a crash hold for a declared cut. `tests/test_postgres_races.py`'s `_Dropping` takes `after_content`, so each drop is LCR10's 502 after content as the adapter raises it. `test_a_crash_between_a_declared_drop_and_its_re_attempt_accepts_once` and `test_a_crash_after_the_re_attempts_drop_makes_no_third_call` run both shapes. With one drop: B's fresh attempt is the re-attempt, CP-0 is accepted once, and the run holds `nodes + 1` reservations. With two: there is no third call, the run is parked `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`, and an operator's requeue calls again.
- F554 — Never assigned: the numbering jumps from F553 to F555 here (recorded 2026-10-07, whole-branch review LOW-2).
- F559 (2026-10-07, D118 fix round 1; the adversarial review of `claude/d118`, probes in `scratchpad/adv-d118/`, three Lows) — Each was fixed test-first.
  - **(L1) A 4xx error object was declared.** An SSE error event whose own `code` is a 4xx counted as a drop the provider declared and was re-attempted in two cases: when it arrived before content, and when the error frame itself carried the first content. `models._declared` now reads the body's code: a code of 400 to 499 other than 429 is never declared (`_against_the_request`), whatever type stands beside it. The adapter now sees every SSE event as the client's own decoder yields it (`openrouter_adapter._Watched` wraps `Stream._decoder`), the error event included. The client raises on that event before yielding its frame, so content inside the error frame is now content (`began`). `generating` no longer raises on a malformed frame.
  - **(L2) An inner code overrode the outer.** `models._error_body` unwrapped `{"error": {...}}` even when the outer object stated a code of its own. So `{"code": 400, "error": {"code": 502}}` after content was declared, and F513 printed `error_code=502`. The outer object is now unwrapped once, only when it states no code.
  - **(L3) Money visibility.**
    - A declared cut is now its own kind, `DropKind.DECLARED_AFTER_CONTENT` (`declared_after_content`). It shares the one re-attempt with `declared` (`outcomes.DECLARED_KINDS`, read by `declared_drop`, so by `drop_reattempt_due`, `drop_stop_owed` and `_feedback_source`).
    - Its outcome keeps the generation id the stream gave. `CutAfterContentError(body, generation_id=...)` carries the first id any event named. `models._cut` keeps it only for a declared cut and only if it is a producer identifier. `canonical._drop_kind` keeps the kind beside that id, and `outcomes._validate_drop` allows the id for this kind alone, never a charge or a body.
    - Migration `0047_call_cut_after_content` replaces 0046's `call_outcomes_drop_kind_check` (one more value) and `call_outcomes_drop_unanswered` (a generation id only beside `declared_after_content`). Versions are positional, and D118 lands before the Copilot transport's credit-price migration, which takes 0048.
    - `scripts/qualify.py` `_attempts` now names each attempt's `drop_kind` and `reserved` amount beside its charge and generation id, so a COMPLETE run's billed cuts and the most they can have cost are in the capture.
  - **Tests:**
    - `tests/test_drop_reattempt.py`: the cut shapes now `declared_after_content`, `outer-400-inner-502` and `outer-null-inner-502`, the body-4xx shapes before content, `test_a_declared_cut_keeps_the_streams_generation_id`, `test_a_declared_cut_shares_the_nodes_one_re_attempt`, `test_a_declared_cut_may_keep_its_generation_id_and_nothing_else` (the store and the table), the run-level kinds, and `test_a_billed_cut_is_visible_where_a_run_is_reconciled`.
    - `tests/test_openrouter_adapter.py::test_an_error_frame_is_read_whole_and_by_its_own_code`: 400 and 402 before content, 429 before content, a 400 or a 502 with content in the error frame, and an outer 400 with an inner 502.
    - `tests/test_postgres_races.py`: the after-content crash cases count `declared_after_content`.
- F566 (2026-10-07, D118 fix round 2; the re-audit of `ee7d3ee`, probes `scratchpad/adv-d118/test_adv_before.py`; one Low, one Info)
  - **(Low) A 4xx stated other than as an integer code was declared.** Before content, the error object `{"code": 400.0}`, `{"code": -400}`, `{"code": "invalid_request_error"}`, or one with no code and the type `context_length_exceeded` or `payment_required` (or no type at all) still counted as a declared drop and spent the node's re-attempt. F559 had excluded only a 4xx readable as a status.
    - `declared` is now positive evidence only, before content and after, through one rule, `models._declares`: a `code` that is a 5xx or a 429 status (an integer, or one written in digits), or, with no code, an `error_type` in `_RETRYABLE_ERROR_TYPES`.
    - That allow-list is drawn from F513's `_ERROR_TYPES`: `provider_unavailable`, `provider_overloaded`, `timeout`, `rate_limit_exceeded`. `server` is dropped, since it names no retryable class; a 5xx code beside it still declares.
    - Everything else is `vendor` before content and `raised` after.
    - `_against_the_request` and `_TRANSIENT_ERROR_TYPES` are gone. A status from the client (`status_code`) is judged as D110 and F530 decided.
  - **(Info) Records.**
    - D118's Money sentence now says a declared cut's row may name its generation id (F559, 0047).
    - N174's reconciliation query now selects every row with a generation id and no charge. That includes a declared cut billed after a later attempt started, which `_counted` keeps without its kind.
  - **Tests.**
