    - `tests/test_drop_reattempt.py::test_an_unanswered_call_carries_how_it_ended`: the twelve `body-*` shapes, F566's eight not declared and four declared.
    - `::test_a_cut_after_content_is_declared_only_by_a_transient_provider_error`: `server-type`, `context-type` and `400-float` are now `raised`.
    - `tests/test_openrouter_adapter.py::test_an_error_frame_is_read_whole_and_by_its_own_code`: `400-float-before`, `code-word-before`, `payment-type-before` and `timeout-type-before`.
- D119 (2026-10-06, owner decision of 6 October 2026, "1M"; closes N170; amends D116's table) — The workspace model endpoints are declared at **1,000,000 tokens** in `caos.provider.CONTEXT_TOKENS`, source "owner, 2026-10-06" (a declaration, not a vendor listing): the ten approved endpoints `grok-4-7`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`, `gpt-6-luna`, `claude-opus-5-5`, `gpt-6-sol`, `deepseek-v4-1-flash`, `gpt-6-astra`, `gemini-3-8-flash`, `glm-5-3`, `glm-5-3-flash`, and the bundle default `databricks-claude-opus-5` (`databricks.yml`, `caos.pricing.DEFAULT_ENDPOINT`); no other workspace name is selectable (checked against the allowlist in D116, `databricks.yml`'s `model_choices`, `.env.example`). Each one's `request_ceiling` is `min(4 MiB, (1,000,000 - 65,536) * 3)` = 2,803,392 bytes, so a pack past it is page-mapped (D116) rather than sent to meet the provider's refusal. The three OpenRouter test names keep 1,050,000 (2,953,392 bytes). `copilot:<model>` stays undeclared and falls back to the transport ceiling with its `CONTEXT_NOT_DECLARED` notice and preflight warning until the Copilot adapter (D77) exists. Preflight no longer warns for any workspace endpoint. The declaration is the owner's, not measured per endpoint: an endpoint whose real window is smaller meets the provider's refusal before generation, as FCA3-dec's 400 did; N170's second half (the 3-bytes-a-token floor against numeric-table packs, 2.54) stays open. Tests `tests/test_context_bound.py`: each of the eleven resolves to 1,000,000 and 2,803,392 with no notice; preflight clean for them; `copilot:` names keep the fallback and notice (also through the runtime).
- D120 (2026-10-06, owner decision of 6 October 2026; amends D117; N176) — The idle-session bound is turned off by a session `SET`, not a startup option, and a server that refuses it no longer refuses the store. Whether Lakebase accepts `-c idle_session_timeout=0` is unmeasured (N172); under D117 a server that refused it refused every store connection, `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`. **Rule:** every store session, `caos.store.connect`'s and the checkpointer pool's (its configure hook, `caos.graph.checkpoint.configure_session`), runs `caos.store.IDLE_SESSION_SET` (`SET idle_session_timeout = 0`) as its first statement, committed on the store's non-autocommit sessions so a caller's rollback never undoes it (`caos.store.idle_session_off`). If the server refuses the `SET` itself, by SQLSTATE (`IDLE_SESSION_SET_REFUSALS`: 42704 unrecognized parameter, 22023 invalid value, 0A000 feature not supported, 42501 insufficient privilege; never the message text), the session carries on without it. The process then prints `IDLE_SESSION_OPTION_REFUSED` on stderr once (the code alone) and skips the `SET` for the rest of its life. Any other error on the `SET` (a session ended under it, a statement timeout, a fault with no SQLSTATE) closes the session and refuses `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`; the pool treats that as a failed connection and never hands such a session out. No connect is ever repeated for this, so connect latency and failure detection are exactly what they were before D117: one attempt, the caller's own timeout. **Why not the startup option:** libpq reports a refused startup option with no SQLSTATE (`OperationalError`, `sqlstate` `None`; measured on PostgreSQL 17.11 with psycopg 3.2.12). The first version of D120 therefore found a refusal by asking again (with the option, without it, with it), and the review showed that a process's own concurrent connects at a connection limit made that probe drop the option for good. It happened in 5 of 5 trials with a role limited to 2 connections and 4 threads, though the server accepted the option throughout; it also repeated kernel connect timeouts (75 s became 150 s) and could take 4 attempts. A refused connect now says nothing about the bound, so the probe and its false drop are gone. **D117's guarantee holds:** a session-level `SET` wins over the database's and the role's `idle_session_timeout`, as the startup option did, so under a database-level bound of 300 ms a call held for a second keeps its `call_hold`, and a second worker past A's lapsed lease is refused `ATTEMPT_UNSETTLED` and pays nothing (the review's probe B, now `tests/test_postgres_races.py::test_a_held_call_outlives_an_idle_session_bound_on_the_database`). **Transaction pooler:** a session `SET` holds only on a session-mode connection. Behind a transaction pooler (PgBouncer `pool_mode=transaction`), the `SET` would run on whichever server session served that statement and could leak to or vanish from the next, and the same is true of `call_hold`'s session-level advisory lock. That is why `call_hold` needs session mode and the store must reach Postgres directly or through a session-mode pooler; the startup option shared that dependence. **Degraded mode:** with the `SET` refused, an `idle_session_timeout` on the database or the role can end a session mid-call again, releasing `call_hold`. Protection against that cut falls back to the lease's 180 s margin past the call deadline (`LEASE_SECONDS - TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, D83(2), F485), the pre-D117 behaviour, the same margin that already covers a failover, a suspend or a lost socket. Fixes F555.
- F555 (2026-10-06, D120) — `caos/store/__init__.py`: `IDLE_SESSION_SET`, `IDLE_SESSION_SET_REFUSALS`, `IDLE_SESSION_REFUSED`, `idle_session_off` and the process's memory of a refusal (`_IDLE_SESSION_REFUSED`, set once under a lock so the line prints once however many threads find it); `connect` sends only the search path and its statement bound as startup options, makes one attempt, and runs `idle_session_off` before returning, closing the session on any exception. `IDLE_SESSION_OPTION` is gone. `caos/graph/checkpoint.py`: `configure_session`, the pool's hook, runs `idle_session_off` and then the graph's search path; the pool's startup options keep only the statement bound. The comment in `caos/provider.py` names the `SET`. The review round replaced the first version (`fb09c2d`, a with/without/with connect probe), whose false drop at a connection limit and repeated kernel timeouts it measured. Tests (`tests/test_idle_session_option.py`, red first where they test new behaviour): One connect and no startup option; the `SET` wins over `PGOPTIONS` and survives a rollback. The server's own 42704, 22023 and (for a plain role setting a superuser-only parameter) 42501 refusals degrade with one line, keep the search path and statement bound, and later sessions skip the `SET`, each on one connect. 0A000 and 42501 degrade, while 57P01 and a fault without a SQLSTATE close the session and refuse. A real 22012 and a 57014 statement timeout on the `SET` close the session and refuse `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`, at the request edge too. A wrong password is one attempt. Four threads at a role connection limit of 2 never drop the `SET`. This failed on `fb09c2d`. The pool, local and platform, degrades with one line, and its hook closes and refuses on another error. `tests/test_postgres_races.py::test_a_held_call_outlives_an_idle_session_bound_on_the_database` fails with B paying again when the `SET` is skipped. `tests/test_store_schema.py::test_connect_asks_for_keepalives_and_an_optional_statement_timeout` asserts one attempt and the options without the idle bound. Seven hand-run mutants each fail the tests: no `SET`, no commit, degrade on any error, no memory, the line printed per finding, the session left open, 42501 dropped from the set.
- F588 (2026-10-07, test infrastructure) — The shared test Postgres (a 1.9 GB tmpfs) filled with 183 databases leaked by pytest processes killed mid-test (`DiskFull`). New disposable databases are `caos_test_<unix second>_<hex>` and `caos_test_template_<unix second>_<hex>` (52 and 62 bytes). `tests/pg_reaper.py` runs once per session, in the controller and never a worker, from `tests/conftest.py::pytest_sessionstart`, before any template is made: it drops a `caos_test_*` database that matches those exact patterns, has no connection in `pg_stat_activity`, and is timestamped and over two hours old, by `DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS` without `FORCE`. It refuses a server whose host or database name contains `dev`, `prod`, `databricks` or `lakebase`, and a failure is one stderr line (`pg_reaper: refused|failed code=...`), never the session's. Tests: `tests/test_pg_reaper.py`. Fix round: the reaper runs only against a server that is positively the test server (port 55437, or `CAOS_TEST_REAPER_ALLOW=1` in the environment for a test server on another port); it always refuses port 55436, any database other than `postgres` or a `caos_test` name, and any host or database naming dev, prod, databricks or lakebase; a `caos_qualify_*` database is never matched. Old-format, untimestamped names (`caos_test_<hex>`, `caos_test_template_<hex>`) are never reaped automatically: a template sits connection-free between clones, so the no-connection guard cannot tell it from a leak, and a session on another branch that predates F588 still makes them (a concurrent `claude/copilot` suite lost its template mid-run and got 1,600 setup errors); the old leftovers were cleaned once by hand. The reaper is fail-open by design: any exception ends it with the one line `pg_reaper: failed code=reaper_failed`, never the session's failure. `tests/test_idle_session_option.py` now scopes `PGOPTIONS` to the test body by an indirect `operator_options` fixture and asserts the autouse fixture's established value is unchanged after the body.
- F556 (2026-10-06, D77, addendum 2; the plan's re-spec R1.0, Task 2) — `session.tools_updated` is left off `caos.copilot._ALLOWED`, so any call carrying it is billed and refused (no finish reason). R1.0 admits it "only with an empty tool list", but `github-copilot-sdk` 1.0.16 types it as `SessionToolsUpdatedData` with the model alone ("identifying the model whose resolved tools were updated") and no tool list, so the condition can never be shown on the wire the SDK writes, and a fake carrying a list cannot be built through `SessionEvent.from_dict`. Whether to admit it, and on what witness, waits on the firm-seat spike (N177, N178). Test: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_anything_but_the_call_asked_for_states_no_finish_reason_and_keeps_its_bill[tools-updated]`.
- F557 (2026-10-06, D77, addendum 2; R1, Task 2) — `caos.copilot.reply_message` and `_failure` hold rules R1's text leaves open, each failing closed: a `session.error` anywhere refuses the finish reason, even beside an answer; a `model.call_failure` after the one settled result refuses it (R1.6 admits only a failure *followed by* the success); the session must end with an `assistant.turn_end` then a `session.idle` that is not aborted on the usage path too, not only without a usage (R1.7); a `fusion` block on any event refuses (R1 names four); a name claiming a Copilot platform in any case (`Copilot:`, `COPILOT-CLI:`) or with a second colon is refused, never an endpoint, and `copilot-cli:` parses (Design 1) for readiness to refuse in the SDK-only scope. Errors, for a call with no spend and no answer: an aborted `session.idle` is indeterminate even beside a `session.error`; the status is the session error's, else an `api` failure's, else the settled result's (`http_400`, `http_413`, `http_429`), `http_4xx`/`http_5xx` declared with no status; a `transport` failure declares nothing, whatever the settled result says; a session that idled with no error, no answer and no spend is returned and refused as an invalid response, not raised. `errorType` and `errorCode` are not carried at all. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py` (`REFUSED`: `failure-after-the-result`, `session-error-beside-the-answer`, `no-turn-end`, `fusion-on-call-start`, `fusion-on-failure`; `::test_a_name_that_claims_copilot_and_does_not_parse_is_refused`; `::test_a_failure_event_maps_to_its_status_class_and_declaration`; `::test_a_session_aborted_or_never_ended_is_indeterminate`).
- F558 (2026-10-06, D77, addendum 2; R2.3, R2.10, Task 2) — The AI-unit bill is read stricter than R2.3's text, each failing closed to an unknown charge (the reservation kept): the last `session.usage_checkpoint` must come after every event that can carry spend (`assistant.message`, `assistant.usage`, `model.call_final_result`, `model.call_failure`, `model.call_finished`), since a checkpoint taken before the answer would under-bill it; checkpoints must not fall, being cumulative; per-request `copilotUsage.totalNanoAiu` figures are summed exactly in `Decimal` and a malformed one is unknown; a zero credit price settles nothing (`settled_charge` returns None). For R2.10, a checkpoint whose figure is not a number counts as spend, so the call is never a drop. Mutation testing (mutmut 3.8.0 over `caos/copilot.py`, `caos.provider.reserving` and `caos.pricing.priced_request`, tests `tests/test_copilot.py` and `tests/test_pricing.py`): 803 killed, 3 survived, all equivalent (`<` to `<=` between the indices of two events of different types, which never coincide). `_CONDITIONS`' closures were replaced by `_LOADS` and `_loaded_nothing(loaded)` because closures built at import were beyond the mutation tool's reach. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_a_checkpoint_before_the_answer_does_not_bill_it`, `::test_a_settled_result_right_after_the_checkpoint_is_not_billed_by_it`, `::test_a_checkpoint_that_falls_is_no_running_total_and_an_unknown_charge`, `::test_every_per_request_figure_counts_against_the_checkpoint`, `::test_a_checkpoint_whose_figure_is_unreadable_is_spend`, `::test_a_charge_with_no_whole_ai_units_or_no_credit_price_is_unknown`.
- D121 (2026-10-07; the project rule that no dependency lands without a decision record; the controller's fix round on Copilot Task 2; closes N179) — `hypothesis==6.167.1` (MPL-2.0) joins the `dev` dependency group for property tests of deterministic money arithmetic: `caos.copilot.settled_charge`, the AI-unit mapping (`caos.copilot.reply_message`) and `caos.pricing.priced_request`.
  - It is a pre-approved test tool in the owner's global rules. Its one dependency, `sortedcontainers` 2.4.0, was already locked.
  - The version is the newest release more than 30 days old on the day it was added: 6.167.1, published 2026-08-30. 6.168.5 was published 2026-10-05, and a release that new is a supply-chain risk.
  - `pip-audit --strict` is clean. `.hypothesis/`, its example database, is git-ignored.
  - Alternatives:
    - Example grids on the standard library alone: no search and no shrinking. The property test is what found F564.
    - Mutation testing alone: it measures the tests and searches no inputs.
  - Evidence: the `test_property_*` tests in `tests/test_copilot.py` and `tests/test_pricing.py`.
- F560 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R2.3, R2.10; adversarial audit of `45d755c`, items 1 and 2) — Spend is read from every sign of work, and a bill only from a settled session.
  - **Never a drop.** `caos.copilot._spent` holds when the model worked or a figure states spend. `_worked` means any `assistant.message`, `assistant.usage` or `assistant.reasoning`, a successful `model.call_final_result`, or a successful `model.call_finished`. A spend figure is any checkpoint not stating exactly zero (unreadable included), or any shutdown figure stated and not zero (F562). Before, a per-request `copilotUsage` figure, or a settled success beside a zero checkpoint, ended `drop=declared`, which D110 would re-attempt and pay twice.
  - **Zero on a call that worked is unknown.** Before, a successful operation with no message and a zero checkpoint billed $0.00.
  - **The last checkpoint bills only once settled** (`_settled_after`). No billable event may follow it: `_BILLED` now also holds `model.call_start`, `assistant.reasoning`, `assistant.turn_start`, `assistant.turn_retry` and `user.message`. A `session.idle` or `session.error` must come after it, and no idle may be aborted. Before, a dispatch started after the checkpoint in a session that never idled billed the checkpoint.
  - **The finish needs a clean end.** No billable event may follow the last `assistant.turn_end`.
  - **Deviation:** a `session.error` counts as the end, beside an idle. R5's `SPENT_FAILURE` ends with one and no idle, and stays billed from its checkpoint. N180 measures whether an error is always followed by an idle.
  - Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py` `WORKED` and `UNSETTLED` (each audit probe, via `ChatCompletions` too: `drop_kind` None, asked once), `::test_a_dispatch_after_the_checkpoint_is_spend_never_a_drop`, and `::test_property_work_after_the_last_checkpoint_leaves_the_charge_unknown`.
- F561 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R1, AR-15; audit item 4) — The model proof reads every event that names a model or an effort.
  - **Names.** A `session.start.selectedModel`, `assistant.turn_start`/`turn_end`/`turn_retry.model`, `model.call_start.model`, `model.call_failure.model` or `session.shutdown.currentModel` that names another model refuses (`_MODEL_FIELDS`).
  - **Efforts and tiers.** Every `session.start` and `session.model_change` must state the pinned effort and no `autoTier` (`_session_agrees`). With no `assistant.usage`, a `session.start` is required as the effort witness.
  - **Tool signals on the usage** (`_no_tool`): `numToolCalls`, `toolCounts`, a `parentToolCallId`, or an `initiator` other than `user` refuses.
  - **Sub-agents.** An `agentId` on the envelope of the message, the usage or the settled result refuses (`_WITNESSES`).
  - Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py` `REFUSED_ROUND_1` and `ADMITTED_ROUND_1`.
- F562 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R1, R2.3; audit item 3) — `session.shutdown` is read.
  - **Refuses the finish** (`_shutdown_agrees`): a shutdown that is not `routine`, or whose `modelMetrics` name a model other than the pin.
  - **Must agree with the checkpoint** (`_shutdown_figures_agree`): its `totalNanoAiu`, when stated, must equal the billed total, and its per-model figures must sum to no more. Otherwise the charge is unknown.
  - **Counts as spend** when one of its figures says so (`_shutdown_figures`).
  - Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py` `DISAGREEING`, `::test_an_unreadable_shutdown_figure_is_spend`, `::test_a_shutdown_stating_zero_is_no_spend`.
- F563 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R1; audit item 5) — Each of these refuses the finish:
  - an `assistant.message` that is one chunk of several (`chunkCount` above 1, or `chunkIndex` above 0) or a tool's child (`parentToolCallId`);
  - a second `user.message`, or any `isAutopilotContinuation`;
  - any billable event after the turn's end, which covers an answer after a `session.idle` (`_ended`).
  - Tests: `REFUSED_ROUND_1` (`message-chunk-*`, `message-parent-tool-call`, `autopilot-continuation`, `lone-autopilot-continuation`, `two-user-messages`, `reasoning-right-after-the-turn-end`) and `UNSETTLED["answer-after-idle"]`.
- F564 (2026-10-07, D121; found by `test_property_a_credit_price_that_is_no_positive_decimal_charges_nothing`) — `caos.copilot.settled_charge` charged a zero count at `0E-1200` under a credit price too precise to multiply exactly, while refusing every other count at that price. A price whose digits leave no room for the largest count (`2**53 - 1`) in the exact context's 1,000 digits now prices no count. Past that check the arithmetic cannot trap, so the `except DecimalException` around it, which could never fire, was removed; the never-raises property covers the path. Test: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_a_credit_price_too_precise_for_any_count_charges_not_even_zero`.
- F565 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R5; spec review of `45d755c`) — The test fakes could carry a key the SDK does not know: `SessionEvent.from_dict` drops one without a word. `tests/test_copilot.py` `wire()` and `enveloped()` now assert that every non-None key sent survives `to_dict` at every depth. Every existing fake passed. Test: `::test_a_fake_carrying_a_key_the_sdk_does_not_know_fails`. Mutation testing after the round (mutmut 3.8.0; `caos/copilot.py`, `caos.provider.reserving`, `caos.pricing.priced_request`): 1,084 killed, 2 survived. Both survivors are equivalent, `<` to `<=` between the indices of two events of different types.
- F567 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R2.10; re-audit of `3b0da98`, item 1) — Spend evidence is an allow-list, so a call that may have spent is never a drop, which D110 would re-attempt and pay twice. The deny-lists `_WORK` and `_BILLED` let through `assistant.message_delta`, `assistant.reasoning_delta`, `session.fusion_completed` (`totalNanoAiu`), `workflow.run_settled` (`consumedNanoAiu`), `session.compaction_complete` (`copilotUsage`), `subagent.completed` (`totalTokens`) and `session.shutdown.agentMetrics`; each such call ended `drop=declared`.
  - **Rule.** `caos.copilot._spent` is now "some event is off `_QUIET`". `_QUIET` holds 12 types the SDK documents as metadata, telemetry or display: `session.start`, `user.message`, `assistant.turn_start`/`turn_end`, `model.call_start`, `model.call_failure`, `session.error`, `session.idle`, `session.info`, `session.warning`, `session.managed_settings_resolved`/`enforced`.
  - **Conditionally quiet** (`_quiet`): a settled result naming a failure, a checkpoint stating exactly zero, and a shutdown that metered no model, no agent and no spend.
  - Any other type, and any event whose data is unreadable, is spend. Zero on a call that may have spent is an unknown charge; `_worked` is gone, being `_spent`.
  - Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py` `UNSEEN_SPEND`, `::test_a_shutdown_reporting_a_sub_agents_spend_is_never_a_drop`, `::test_a_quiet_type_with_unreadable_data_is_spend`, `::test_property_any_type_off_the_quiet_list_is_spend_never_a_drop` (every `SessionEventType` off the list), and `::test_property_a_call_of_quiet_events_alone_spent_nothing`.
- F568 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R2.3, R1.7; re-audit item 2) — Settlement is an allow-list too, closing the same class of hole on the bill side.
  - **After the last checkpoint.** Only `_SETTLING` may follow it: `session.idle`, `session.error`, `session.shutdown`. Any other type voids the bill (charge unknown, reservation kept).
  - **Voids too:** a `session.error` with `eligibleForAutoSwitch`; a dispatch still open at the checkpoint, meaning a `model.call_start` with no `model.call_finished` or `model.call_failure` closing it (`_dispatch_open`); and any `agentMetrics` on a shutdown, since a sub-agent's spend cannot be reconciled.
  - **After the turn's end, for the finish:** only `_AFTER_TURN` may follow it: the checkpoint, the idle, the shutdown.
  - Tests: `LATE`, `OPEN`, `::test_two_dispatches_with_one_closed_leave_one_open`, `::test_a_sub_agents_metered_spend_leaves_the_charge_unknown`, `::test_property_any_unsettling_type_after_the_last_checkpoint_voids_the_bill` (every `SessionEventType` off `_SETTLING`), and `::test_property_any_event_type_off_the_allow_list_refuses_its_finish` (now position-aware: after the checkpoint, the charge is unknown).
- F569 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R1, AR-15; re-audit item 3) — Every field, at any depth, is held by the family its camelCase name puts it in (`_family`, `_FIELD_RULES`), so a field the SDK adds later in a family is held too. This replaces the per-event `_MODEL_FIELDS`, `_WITNESSES` and `_no_tool`, and the explicit `model_change`, `serverTools`, `isAuto` and `autoTier` checks.
  - **The families:**
    - `model`, `modelId`, `modelTo` and `*Model`/`*ModelId`: the pin.
    - `models` and `*Models`: only the pin.
    - `effort` and `*Effort`: the pinned effort.
    - `isAuto`, `autoTier` and `*AutoTier`: absent.
    - `agent*`, `*Agent*`, `*ubagent*` and `tool*`/`*Tool*`: absent.
    - `agentMode`: `interactive`.
    - `initiator`: `user`.
    - `previous*` is not held.
  - **Sub-agents:** an `agentId` on any event's envelope refuses.
  - **Closes:** `prompt_cache_break` with `modelTo` another model or `toolsAdded`; a recovered failure that is auto, at another effort or by another initiator; `parentToolCallId` on a dispatch or turn; `user.message.parentAgentTaskId`; and a shutdown's `agentMetrics`.
  - Tests: `FIELDS`, `FIELDS_ADMITTED`, `NAMED_FIELDS` (`::test_a_field_is_held_by_the_family_its_name_puts_it_in`), `UNSTATED`.
- F570 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R1.7; found by the property in F568) — A second `assistant.turn_end` after the bill left the finish stated. `_ended` now requires exactly one `assistant.turn_start` and one `assistant.turn_end`, because one prompt is one turn: a second turn is a second inference round, whichever side of the bill it falls on. Tests: `SECOND_TURN`.
- F571 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2, D121; re-audit item 4) — Round-2 test and mutation record.
  - **Properties.** They now sample every `SessionEventType` value the SDK defines (`ALL_TYPES`), not a list this module keeps, so a type the module never named is found. They pass under eight further seeds.
  - **Simplifications.** Checks the field rule now covers were removed (above). `_settled_result` and `_from_result` were narrowed, because on a call with no spend every settled result names a failure.
  - **Mutation testing** (mutmut 3.8.0 over `caos/copilot.py`, `caos.provider.reserving` and `caos.pricing.priced_request`): 1,154 killed, 2 survived. Both survivors are equivalent, `<` to `<=` between the indices of two events of different types.
- F572 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R2.3; re-audit of `44ff021`, items 1 and 5) — Every spend figure the session states anywhere is held against the bill, and a sub-agent's spend voids it wherever it is stated.
  - **Before:** `_figures_agree` read only `assistant.usage.copilotUsage` and the shutdown, so a session could state more spend elsewhere and still be billed at its lower checkpoint.
  - **Recognition by name** (`_unit`), at any depth (`_pairs`): `*NanoAiu` is nano-AIU, `used*AiCredits` is AI credits (×10^9), and `*PremiumRequests` is premium requests. No figure may exceed the last checkpoint (`_within`); premium requests may not exceed the checkpoint's own count. Anything else is an unknown charge, never the lower figure.
  - **Covers:** `session.fusion_completed`, `assistant.fusion_phase_*.usage`, `session.compaction_complete.compactionTokensUsed.copilotUsage`, `session_limits_exhausted.requested.usedAiCredits` and `session.shutdown` totals.
  - **Not spend:** credit caps and grants (`maxAiCredits`, `declaredMaxAiCredits`, `additionalAiCredits`). `cost` and `costPerBatch` state no unit (R2 names `cost` experimental) and are not read.
  - **Sub-agent spend voids everywhere** (`_sub_agent`): an `agentId` envelope, a `subagent.*` or `workflow.*` event, any `agentMetrics` or `consumedSubagents`, or a workflow's `consumedNanoAiu`, below the checkpoint too, as `shutdown.agentMetrics` already did (F568). The `_ai_units` docstring now says so.
  - Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py` `ABOVE`, `WITHIN`, `SUB_AGENT_SPEND`, `::test_premium_requests_stated_elsewhere_may_not_exceed_the_checkpoints`, `::test_ai_credits_used_are_read_in_nano_ai_units`, `::test_a_stated_spend_figure_that_is_no_count_leaves_the_charge_unknown`, `::test_a_completed_runs_spend_voids_the_bill_with_no_sub_agent_named`, `::test_every_spend_name_in_the_sdk_is_read_as_spend_or_named_a_cap` (every spend-named field the pinned SDK's own source defines), and `::test_property_a_spend_figure_above_the_checkpoint_anywhere_voids_the_bill` (every such name, every `SessionEventType`, any depth, any position).
- F573 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R1.6, R2.3; re-audit item 2) — A dispatch closes only on `model.call_finished`, "the final lifecycle outcome for one logical model dispatch". A `model.call_failure` is one attempt's telemetry, possibly a sub-agent's, and no longer closes one (`_dispatch_open`).
  - **Fakes adjusted to the SDK's semantics.** R5's `SPENT_FAILURE` now closes its dispatch with `call_finished("error")`; R5's shape left it open and would now void its bill. R5's `RECOVERED` is one logical dispatch recovered inside itself (`call_start`, failure, `turn_retry`, answer, `call_finished` success), as the SDK's doc for R1.6 says ("may include internal reconnect or fallback work"). R5's shape, with two `call_start`s and one finish, left a dispatch open.
  - Tests: `NOT_CLOSED`, `::test_a_failed_dispatch_closed_by_its_finish_bills_its_checkpoint`.
- F574 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R2.10; re-audit item 3) — More shapes are spend, never a declared drop.
  - **Now spend:** a checkpoint of zero AI units with premium requests; a shutdown stating only premium requests; a dispatch that never finished (`_spent` now also holds when `_dispatch_open`); a `model.call_failure` not from the top-level agent; and any event a sub-agent wrote (an `agentId` envelope).
  - **Now conditional** (`_QUIET_WHEN`): `model.call_failure` (only when `source` is `top_level`) and `model.call_finished` (only when not a success, which closes a failed dispatch). `_QUIET` has 11 unconditional types and 5 conditional ones.
  - **D110 stays open:** a call whose dispatch failed, finished and spent nothing is still a declared drop (`::test_a_closed_failed_dispatch_is_still_a_declared_drop`).
  - Tests: `RE_PAY`.
- F575 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R1; re-audit item 4) — These now refuse the finish:
  - a `model.call_failure` whose `source` is not `top_level` (`_condition_holds`);
  - any `interactionType` stated, since no source states the expected value (N182);
  - `mode` (the `session.idle` mode) other than `interactive`, held like `agentMode`;
  - `containsBuiltInFileEditRequest` true;
  - `isByok` true on any event.
  - Tests: `FIELD_GAPS`, `::test_an_idle_in_interactive_mode_is_admitted`.
- F576 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R1; re-audit item 4; superseded by F580 in fix round 4) — Recorded in round 3: `prompt_cache_break.modelFrom` and every `previous*` field were not held to the pin, on the reasoning that the dispatches name the model that answered. The round-4 probes (`audit4/probe_r4.py` M, `probe_r4c.py`, `probe_r4d.py`) disproved it: a dispatch whose `model.call_start` names no model, before the pin's own, let a cache break from another model through with a stated finish. F580 now holds these fields and requires every dispatch to name its model. Test: `MODEL_PROOF_ROUND_4["cache-break-from-another-model"]`.
- F577 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; found by mutation testing) — An event whose `type` is not text made `_quiet` raise a `TypeError` (a frozenset membership test on a list), and could do the same in `_settles` and `_ended`. `_kind` now returns None for a type that is not text, so such an event is no type: not quiet, not settling, never raising.
  - Mutation testing after the round (mutmut 3.8.0 over `caos/copilot.py`, `caos.provider.reserving`, `caos.pricing.priced_request`): 1,274 killed, 2 survived. Both survivors are equivalent, `<` to `<=` between the indices of two events of different types.
  - Removed as unreachable or redundant: the checkpoint exclusion in the figure walk, a `consumed*AiCredits` name the SDK never uses, and two `get(None)` guards.
  - Tests: `::test_an_event_whose_type_is_not_text_is_spend_and_never_raises`, `::test_every_field_is_found_at_every_depth_and_list_position`.
- F578 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; fix round 4, item 1) — Re-pay. Only a `model.call_finished` with outcome `error` ("a provider or transport error") may close a dispatch quietly. `rejected` follows a provider response ("rejected during post-response acceptance processing") and `cancelled` may follow a streamed one, so both are spend, never a declared drop (`_failed_result`). The quiet fakes that finished in `cancelled` or `rejected` now finish in `error`.
  - Tests: `RE_PAY_ROUND_4`, `::test_property_only_a_dispatch_that_finished_in_error_can_be_a_drop`.
- F579 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; fix round 4, item 2) — Under-charge. Each per-request spend block is now summed across event types and held against the last checkpoint (`_requests`): every `copilotUsage` at any depth of any event (an answer's usage, a compaction's) and each fusion phase's `usage`. Above the checkpoint, the charge is unknown. Aggregates (a fusion turn's total, the shutdown's) are still held alone and never added. A compaction at the full checkpoint left `WITHIN`, because added to the request it is above the bill.
  - Tests: `SUMMED_ABOVE`, `::test_per_request_spend_summed_to_exactly_the_bill_is_billed`, `::test_property_per_request_spend_bills_only_when_its_sum_is_within_the_bill`, `::test_every_sdk_class_stating_nano_ai_units_is_summed_or_held_otherwise`, `::test_only_a_fusion_phase_states_its_request_as_usage` (kills the one mutant the round first left alive, `and` to `or` in `_requests`).
  - Mutation testing (mutmut 3.8.0, each changed function run separately: `_admitted`, `_fields_agree`, `_nested_agree`, `_figures_agree`, `_requests`, `_failed_result`): 127 killed, 0 survived.
- F580 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; fix round 4, item 3; supersedes F576) — Model proof.
  - `modelFrom` and every `previous*` field (`previousModel`, `previousReasoningEffort`, `previousAutoTier`) are held to the pin like their family.
  - A `session.model_change` is admitted only from a `source` that is none, a user's command or picker, managed or repository settings, startup, the changeboarding shortcut, or `sdk` (this host). `automatic`, `agent`, `plan_mode`, `auto_tier_recommendation` and any future value refuse (`_CHOSEN`).
  - `session.start` must state `selectedModel` and `model.call_start` must state `model` (`_NAMING`). An unnamed dispatch could otherwise answer on another model.
  - Tests: `MODEL_PROOF_ROUND_4`, `FIELDS_ADMITTED["model-change-by-this-host"]`, `NAMED_FIELDS`, `::test_property_the_finish_stands_only_when_every_dispatch_names_only_the_pin`.
- F581 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; round-5 probe) — A `session.model_change` whose `source` was a list or dict made `_condition_holds` raise a `TypeError` (a frozenset membership test on an unhashable), so a spent call came back PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE with no charge. Only a text `source` is now compared; any other is no chosen source, so the event is refused and billed. The other membership tests on event data were checked: they are on text keys or use `==`.
- F582 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; D118, F566; Task 3, merging `claude/dec`) — A Copilot failure the runtime declared with no status is declared in D118's terms. D118 narrowed `models._declared` to positive evidence: a 5xx or 429 `code`, or with no code a retryable `error_type`; a 4xx however stated is never declared. Task 2's body `{"error": {"result": "http_5xx"}}` carried neither, so after the merge a runtime-declared 5xx became an undeclared `vendor` drop and lost D110's single re-attempt; and `http_4xx` was still declared against F566. `CopilotStatusError(None, declared="http_5xx")` now carries `{"error": {"result": "http_5xx", "metadata": {"error_type": "provider_unavailable"}}}` (D118's own word for "the provider, not the request, failed"), and `http_4xx` carries no body. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_a_status_error_carries_its_status_and_no_text`, `::test_a_failure_event_maps_to_its_status_class_and_declaration`, `::test_a_failed_call_with_no_spend_and_no_answer_is_a_drop_by_its_status`.
- F583 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R3, R7; Task 3) — The SDK transport's isolation, beyond the plan's text, from the pinned SDK's source.
  - **An explicit connection.** With no `connection`, `CopilotClient` reads `COPILOT_SDK_DEFAULT_CONNECTION` from the worker's own environment (`_resolve_default_connection(os.environ)`), and `inprocess` would load the runtime into the worker and reject `env=`. `_client()` always passes `RuntimeConnection.for_stdio(path=<the resolved runtime>)`, so neither that variable nor `COPILOT_CLI_PATH` can redirect the runtime. The runtime is `CAOS_COPILOT_RUNTIME` when set (an absolute path, else `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`), else the SDK's verified download (`copilot._cli_download.ensure_runtime_wrapper`, a private SDK function pinned at 1.0.16).
  - **The runtime's working directory.** The SDK starts the runtime in `working_directory or os.getcwd()`: the worker's own directory, where repository instructions could sit. The client is given the call's private directory as its working directory too.
  - **Events from the start.** The handler is registered by `create_session(on_event=...)`, before the session starts, not by `session.on` after it: the R1 mapping requires `session.start`, which a late handler could miss.
  - **A permission request is recorded where it was asked.** The SDK answers a permission request either from a `permission.requested` event (which also reaches the handler, and the mapping refuses) or by a direct RPC that emits no event. The deny-all handler returns `PermissionDecisionReject()` and appends `{"type": "caos.permission_asked", "data": {}}` to the call's events at that point: not a runtime type, so the allow-list states no finish and the spend rule keeps the bill (never a drop).
  - **One deadline for the whole call.** `asyncio.timeout(seconds)` bounds the runtime's start, the session's creation and the wait; on cancellation the session is aborted, bounded by 5 s. What the abort raises propagates instead of `TimeoutError`; both are indeterminate.
  - **A directory that could not be removed** is named on stderr as `COPILOT_HOME_NOT_REMOVED`, never its path: the documented fail-open of a removal the platform refused.
  - Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_the_runtime_never_auto_logs_in_and_sees_only_the_allow_listed_environment`, `::test_a_runtime_the_operator_names_is_started_when_its_path_is_absolute`, `::test_a_permission_request_during_a_call_is_denied_and_refuses_the_call`, `::test_only_the_main_agents_idle_or_error_ends_the_wait`, `::test_a_home_the_runtime_left_behind_is_named_on_stderr_without_its_path`, `::test_the_sdk_logger_reaches_no_handler`.
- F584 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R2.2; Task 3) — The credit price is stored by migration **0048** `0048_reservation_credit_price`, not 0047: D118 took 0047 (`0047_call_cut_after_content`) on `claude/dec`, which `claude/copilot` forked from at 144a145 before it landed, so `claude/dec` was merged into `claude/copilot` first and 0048 follows it (`MIGRATIONS` is positional; `docs/MIGRATIONS.md`). Both columns or neither, and a price above zero and finite (`reservation_credit_price_readable`). `reserve` reads the process's pin itself for a Copilot model (`caos.copilot.credit_price`), as R2.2 words it, rather than taking a sixth argument (PLR0913, no new suppression): a Copilot model with no valid pin refuses `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` before anything is written, and a later pin changes no reservation already taken. Tests: `tests/test_budget.py::test_a_copilot_reservation_stores_its_credit_price_and_reads_it_back`, `::test_a_gateway_reservation_names_no_credit_price`, `::test_a_copilot_reservation_with_no_credit_price_pinned_is_never_taken`, `::test_the_store_holds_a_reservations_credit_price_whole_and_positive`.
- F585 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R2.4, R2.6; Task 3) — Settlement and the terminal park.
  - `ChatCompletions` bills a Copilot message from `copilot.settled_charge` at `caos.provider.reserved_credit`, the credit price of the reservation in scope, never from its token counts; counts it does state must still pass `_charge`'s bound. The executor runs every call inside `reserving(amount, credit=...)` from the reservation `_within_reservation` verified, which also feeds the SDK's credit cap (R2.7). No reservation in scope, or one naming no credit price, settles no Copilot call: unknown, refused, reservation kept.
  - A known AI-unit charge above its reservation (one that names a credit price) is recorded in full by `bill`, then refused `BUDGET_CHARGE_OVER_RESERVATION` with one stderr line, `BUDGET_CHARGE_OVER_RESERVATION reserved=<d> charged=<d> nano_aiu=<n|->`, its AI units recovered exactly from the charge and the reservation's credit price. The worker parks the run with that code. A token-priced charge keeps its budget exit: its overrun is accepted and consumes the run's capacity (`tests/test_pricing.py::test_an_overrun_charge_stops_the_next_node_before_its_call`, which the first draft of this rule broke), since only an AI-unit bill escapes the pinned per-token price.
  - **The crash gap.** A worker that dies after the bill commits and before the refusal is explained would otherwise have `replay_billed` accept the stored answer on resume. The replay applies the same check and returns `REFUSED` with the same code.
  - `requeue_run` refuses a run parked with that code, under the run lock, so a successor run (`supersedes_run_id`) on a re-priced model is the only way on; the Run section's RETRY_RUN names the code instead of offering a retry (`RunFacts.stop_code`).
  - Tests: `tests/test_canonical_execution.py::test_a_charge_above_the_reservation_is_recorded_in_full_and_refused_typed`, `::test_a_billed_answer_above_its_reservation_is_never_accepted_on_replay`, `::test_a_gateway_charge_above_its_reservation_is_accepted_and_spends_capacity`, `tests/test_execution_attempts.py::test_a_run_parked_over_its_reservation_cannot_be_requeued_on_its_pin`, `tests/test_command_availability.py::test_a_run_parked_over_its_reservation_is_offered_no_retry`; properties `tests/test_copilot.py::test_property_a_settled_charge_is_within_its_reservation_or_named_exactly`, `::test_property_the_session_cap_covers_its_reservation_tightly`, `::test_property_a_copilot_charge_is_its_reservations_credit_never_another`.
- F586 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R4, D116; Task 3) — `caos.copilot.CONTEXT_LISTED` is a read-only mapping filled once per process by `declare_listed` (Task 5's readiness calls it): a second declaration, a key that is no runtime model id, a count that is not a positive `int`, or a pinned `copilot:` entry in `caos.provider.CONTEXT_TOKENS` above its listed value refuses `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`, and nothing is declared. `context_tokens` answers the listing for an SDK name, else the pinned entry under the full name; `request_ceiling` and `context_notice` consult it, with `output_cap` as the completion. Until Task 5 declares a listing, an undeclared `copilot:` model keeps the transport ceiling and its `CONTEXT_NOT_DECLARED` notice, as D119 left it. Tests: `tests/test_context_bound.py::test_a_listed_copilot_model_is_bounded_by_its_listing`, `::test_a_pinned_copilot_entry_answers_until_a_listing_does`, `::test_a_pinned_copilot_entry_above_the_listing_refuses_at_start`, `::test_the_listing_is_declared_once_per_process`, `::test_a_listing_that_is_no_context_is_refused_and_nothing_declared`.
- F587 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; Design 2, R2.5; Task 3) — The factory answers a `copilot:` name with `ChatCopilot` (the SDK transport, `ask_copilot`, by default) and never builds a gateway client for it; `identity_of` names `copilot/<model>/<effort|none>/<output_cap>`, the effort the name pins and never a separate one. A `copilot-cli:` name is refused `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` by `chat_model`, `identity_of`, `output_cap` and `request_ceiling` until its transport is built (owner decision (a)). `credit_price()` reads `CAOS_COPILOT_CREDIT_PRICE=<usd>,<YYYY-MM-DD>` as a rate is written (DF-10), above zero, exact spend, dated no later than today in UTC. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_a_copilot_model_is_answered_by_chat_copilot_never_the_gateway`, `::test_a_copilot_identity_names_its_platform_model_and_effort`, `::test_a_cli_copilot_name_is_refused_until_its_transport_is_built`, `::test_the_credit_price_is_a_dated_decimal_or_not_configured`.
- F589 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R2.6; Task 3 fix round 1, MEDIUM-1) — The terminal park could be skipped, and the pin paid again. `record_refusal` committed the explanation, and the park (`stop`) was a separate write; a worker that died or a store fault between the two left the run CLAIMED. The reclaim found nothing to replay and started a fresh attempt at the same pinned price, and the probe paid 0.5 twice against 0.000807986 reserved. The ledger now decides: `caos.store.budget.overspent(conn, run_id)` is true when any reservation of the run that names a credit price has a ledger amount above it. `start_attempt` refuses `BUDGET_CHARGE_OVER_RESERVATION` on any node of such a run, under the run lock and ahead of every other check (as D110's `drop_stop_owed` does), so the reclaim parks the run with that code instead of calling. A token-priced overrun is not counted. Tests: `tests/test_over_reservation_park.py::test_a_park_lost_after_the_refusal_still_pays_the_pin_no_second_time[died|store-fault]` (the audit's two probes), `::test_the_ledger_refuses_any_attempt_on_an_overspent_run`, `::test_a_token_priced_overrun_never_stops_an_attempt`.
- F590 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R1, R2.10; Task 3 fix round 1, MEDIUM-2) — The SDK transport's wait ended at the first main-agent `session.error`, so spend the runtime stated after it was never read. That covered an error `eligibleForAutoSwitch`, which `_settles` reads as one the runtime goes on from, and a checkpoint on the frame after an error. The probe's call (`p1_trailing_checkpoint.py`: a failed dispatch, its error, then a 900e9 nano-AIU checkpoint and an idle) became a declared drop that D110 re-attempts. The wait (`_settled`) now ends at the main agent's `session.idle`. An error the runtime may recover from ends nothing, so its call ends at the idle or at the deadline. An error it will not recover from ends the call after at most `_AFTER_ERROR_SECONDS` (2 s, inside the call's deadline), or sooner at the idle, so the frames after it are read. Whether the runtime always sends an idle after an error is unmeasured (N180); without one a failed call takes up to 2 s longer. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_spend_stated_after_a_session_error_is_read_and_never_a_drop`, `::test_an_error_the_runtime_may_recover_from_does_not_end_the_call`, `::test_after_an_error_the_wait_is_bounded_by_its_grace_not_the_deadline`.
- F591 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R3; Task 3 fix round 1, LOW-1) — The runtime is pinned and verified, and the worker's environment cannot choose it. With no `CAOS_COPILOT_RUNTIME`, `_runtime_entry` used the SDK's `ensure_runtime_wrapper`, whose cache root the worker's `COPILOT_CLI_EXTRACT_DIR` (also `XDG_CACHE_HOME` and `LOCALAPPDATA`) can move. That function accepts a complete cached bundle on presence and size alone; the release checksum covers only the downloaded zip (probe `p3_extract_dir.py`). Now both `CAOS_COPILOT_RUNTIME` (an absolute path to a regular file that is no link) and `CAOS_COPILOT_RUNTIME_SHA256` are required. The digest is `caos.copilot.runtime_digest(entry)`: SHA-256 over `<relative POSIX path>\0<file SHA-256>\n` for every regular file under the entry's directory, sorted, since the entry loads the native library and assets beside it. A link or any special file in the directory refuses. The digest is checked before the first call and re-read whenever any file's size or mtime moves. Anything else refuses `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` before a client starts. The SDK's own resolution is never consulted, which retires N183's item on that private function. The child's allow-list also sets `COPILOT_DISABLE_LOGIN_SHELL_ENV=1` (runtime 1.0.90's switch), so the runtime does not capture an analyst's login-shell environment. The runbook (Task 7) must give the operator `uv run python -c "from caos.copilot import runtime_digest; print(runtime_digest('<entry>'))"` beside `python -m copilot download-runtime`. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_the_runtime_digest_names_every_file_of_its_directory`, `::test_only_the_pinned_runtime_runs_and_the_environment_cannot_choose_it`, `::test_a_runtime_changed_after_it_was_verified_is_refused`, `::test_the_child_environment_is_exactly_the_allow_list`.
- F592 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R2.2, F468; Task 3 fix round 1, LOW-2) — The credit price is pinned per run, not per reservation. `_credit_for` read the process's pin at every reservation, so a redeploy between two nodes settled one run at two credit prices (probe `test_p4_credit_not_run_pinned.py`: 0.01 and 0.05) while its per-token price stayed pinned (F468). Now the run's first Copilot reservation takes the process's pin, and every later reservation of that run reuses it, read under the run lock in `_reserve` after `require_running`. A run whose pin is set needs no environment value; a new run takes the pin in force. The docstrings of `caos.pricing.CreditPrice` and `caos/store/budget.py` now say so. Tests: `tests/test_budget.py::test_a_runs_first_copilot_reservation_pins_its_credit_price_for_the_run`, `tests/test_over_reservation_park.py::test_one_run_settles_at_one_credit_price_across_a_redeploy`.
- F593 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R2.10, F590; Task 3 fix round 2, MEDIUM-2 residual) — The grace after a non-recoverable `session.error` running out with no `session.idle` was read as settled (`suppress(TimeoutError)`, no log). A checkpoint 2.2 s after the error was therefore lost, and the call became a declared drop that D110 re-attempts (probe `r2_m2_grace.py`). An unsettled session's spend is unknown, so `_settled` now returns whether the idle arrived. When it did not, `_asked` aborts the session and raises `CopilotUnsettledError` (its message the platform's name alone). `ChatCompletions` reads it as `raised`: charge unknown, refused `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`, reservation kept as the bill, F513's line `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE call=raised class=CopilotUnsettledError ...` on stderr. `raised` is never re-attempted. A session error followed by its idle inside the grace is returned as data, as before. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_an_error_with_no_idle_in_its_grace_is_indeterminate_never_a_drop`, `::test_a_session_error_ends_the_wait_and_is_returned_as_data`.
- F594 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R3, F591; Task 3 fix round 2, NEW-1) — The verified-runtime cache keyed on each file's (path, size, mtime_ns), which the owner can set back. Two cases were accepted without a re-hash (probe `r2_l1_digest_cache.py`): a same-size swap with its mtime restored, and a symlinked parent retargeted to a twin directory. The cache is gone, so every call hashes the whole runtime directory it is about to start; the real 1.0.90 bundle (103 MB, 42 files) took 57–87 ms per full digest on this machine, beside a call of seconds. The entry is resolved once with `os.path.realpath`, and the resolved directory is both what is hashed and what is started (the connection's path and the child's `PATH`), so a parent retargeted after the check changes nothing. The entry itself must not be a link. The window this left between the hash and the runtime's start is closed by F596: each call now starts a verified copy. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_every_call_reads_the_whole_runtime_again`, `::test_a_runtime_swapped_with_its_mtime_put_back_is_refused`, `::test_a_runtime_reached_through_a_retargeted_link_is_never_started`.
- F595 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R3, F591; Task 3 fix round 2, NEW-2) — A pinned `.js` entry ran `node` taken from the worker's `PATH`, which no digest covers (probe `r2_l1_js_node.py`). `CAOS_COPILOT_RUNTIME` must now be a native runtime executable inside the digested directory. Its first bytes must be ELF, Mach-O (32 or 64 bit, either byte order, or universal) or PE, and it must not have a `.js`, `.mjs` or `.cjs` name, which the SDK hands to `node`. Anything else refuses `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`, with the typed stderr line `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED reason=runtime_not_native` and never the path. `child_environment` no longer looks for `node`: `PATH` is the runtime's directory alone. The real 1.0.90 runtime is a 64-bit Mach-O and passes both checks in 54 ms. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_an_entry_that_is_no_native_executable_is_refused_with_its_reason` (script, `.js`, `.mjs`, data and empty), `::test_a_native_executable_entry_is_started` (seven formats); `::test_a_script_runtime_finds_node_and_nothing_else_on_its_path` and `::test_node_is_searched_on_the_workers_path_alone` are retired with the branch they tested.
- F596 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R3, F594; Task 3 fix round 3, the digest-to-start race) — The source runtime was hashed about 16 ms before it started. Swapping the entry inside that window ran the swapped binary in 20 of 40 attempts (probe `r3_race.py`), and the files the runtime opens after it starts, such as `runtime.node`, could be swapped for the whole call. Now `_staged` copies every file of the pinned directory into the call's private directory (`<home>/caos-runtime`), owner-only (mode masked to 0o700), and hashes each file's bytes as it writes them. The digest is therefore of exactly the copy that starts; the copy's entry must be native, its digest must equal the pin, and the copy is removed with the call's directory. The source can change at any time without effect, including a hard-linked entry written in place. A source file that vanished mid-copy refuses `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`. Cost on the real 1.0.90 bundle (103 MB, 42 files), copy plus hash: 103–154 ms per call, median 135 ms, on this machine. Probe re-run against the copy (`r3_race_copy.py`): an in-window swap ran GOOD; 40 calls under an external flipper started EVIL 0 times (24 GOOD, 16 refused where the copy caught the flip). A same-user process that writes into the call's own private directory between the copy and the start is outside this rule. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_the_runtime_started_is_a_verified_copy_in_the_calls_own_directory`, `::test_a_runtime_swapped_once_it_was_staged_changes_nothing`, `::test_every_call_reads_the_whole_runtime_again`.
- F597 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R3, F595; Task 3 fix round 3, executable format) — F595 checked only the first bytes, so an `MZ`-, ELF- or CAFEBABE-prefixed shell script passed on any host, as did a Mach-O library given as the entry (probe `r3_native_check.py`). The entry must now be the host's own executable, as its loader reads it (`_native`, with the host from `_host()`: `sys.platform` and `platform.machine()`). On macOS: a 64-bit little-endian Mach-O of the host's CPU type (arm64 or x86_64), thin or as that slice of a universal binary, with filetype `MH_EXECUTE`. On Linux: a 64-bit little-endian ELF of the host's `e_machine` (x86-64 or AArch64), type `ET_EXEC` or `ET_DYN` (a position-independent executable; a shared library is also `ET_DYN`, and Linux execve refuses one with no entry point). On Windows: `MZ` whose `e_lfanew` names a `PE\0\0` COFF header of the host's machine, executable and not a DLL. Any other host refuses. The names `.bat`, `.cmd` and `.ps1` are refused on every host, beside the `.js` family. A refusal prints `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED reason=runtime_not_native`. The real 1.0.90 runtime (arm64 Mach-O executable) passes, and its `runtime.node` (a library) does not. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_only_the_hosts_own_executable_format_is_native` (38 cases, macOS, Linux and Windows hosts, including an MZ-prefixed script on each), `::test_an_mz_prefixed_script_on_a_mac_is_refused_with_its_reason`, `::test_the_host_is_this_process_platform_and_machine`.
- F598 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R4; Task 5) — `CONTEXT_LISTED` refused any second declaration, and the in-process worker (`_boot`) configures again after a store fault, so its second readiness would have refused `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` and ended the worker thread for a fault that was the store's. The same listing declared again now changes nothing; a different one, or an empty one after a full one, still refuses (invariant 10). Tests: `tests/test_context_bound.py::test_the_listing_is_declared_once_per_process`, `tests/test_copilot.py::test_readiness_declares_each_listed_models_context_once`, `::test_a_listing_that_changed_since_it_was_declared_refuses`.
- F599 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R3, R2.1, R4; Task 5) — Readiness at worker start: `caos.copilot.require_ready(choices)`, called by `worker._configured` after `model_choices()` and before the store is read, refuses `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` (exit 2) unless every check holds:
  - No runtime starts when no approved model is a `copilot:` one. A `copilot-cli:` name is refused (owner decision (a)), and so is a name that claims Copilot and does not parse.
  - `credit_price()` must be set. `CAOS_COPILOT_HOST` (default `github.com`) must be a lower-case host name. The runtime must start from its verified pin (`_client`: `CAOS_COPILOT_RUNTIME`, `_SHA256`, the staged copy).
  - `get_auth_status()` must report `isAuthenticated` exactly true, `authType == "env"`, and `host` equal to the pin or to `https://<pin>`. Those are the only two forms accepted, since the type only calls it a "host URL" (N184). Nothing is listed for any other seat.
  - **Every** approved `copilot:` model must be offered, enabled by policy, at an effort it takes (`usable`), and priced at no less than its listing. This is the brief's rule, stricter than R3, where an unoffered model is printed `offered=n` and its run parks. The owner decides between the two (N184).
  - The price check (R2.1) takes the greatest of `inputPrice`, `cachePrice` (deprecated, counted in case a listing states only it), `cacheReadPrice`, `cacheWritePrice` and `cacheWrite1hPrice` across the base and `longContext` tiers, and the greater `outputPrice`. Each is scaled `/ batchSize x credit price x multiplier` (a missing multiplier counts as 1) and compared exactly as a `Fraction` of each float's shortest decimal form, never as a float (invariant 7). A listing with no batch size, no input or output rate, or any rate or multiplier that is not a finite, non-negative number cannot be priced and refuses (`price_check=unpriced`).
  - Each model's context (R4: `longContext.maxPromptTokens`, then the base tier's, then `capabilities.limits.max_prompt_tokens`, then `max_context_window_tokens`) is declared only once every check has held.
  - Printed to stderr: `copilot runtime=<version> host=<pin> login=<login>`, then one line per approved model, `offered policy usable efforts max_prompt_tokens max_context_window_tokens context price_check price_floor credit_price`. Each listed value is printed as an identifier or a whole count, else `-`. Floors are rounded up at 12 places. The token, the status message and the runtime's error text are never printed.
  - `max_output_tokens` is not printed. `CopilotClient.list_models()` returns `copilot.client.ModelInfo`, whose `ModelLimits` has no such field. The SDK's typed, experimental `client.rpc.models.list` does return it (`ModelCapabilitiesLimits.max_output_tokens`, `generated/rpc.py`), but readiness does not make that second, experimental call: the session caps output at 65,536 either way (corrected in fix round 1; N184).
  - Readiness gets `READY_SECONDS` (60 s). A runtime that raises `JsonRpcError`, `ProcessExitedError`, `OSError`, `RuntimeError`, `ValueError` or `TimeoutError` refuses with `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED reason=runtime_unready class=<name>`. A missing SDK refuses with `reason=sdk_not_installed`. Neither line carries the error's text.
  - Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py`, from `::test_no_copilot_model_starts_no_runtime` to `::test_the_worker_checks_readiness_on_every_approved_model_before_the_store`.
- F600 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R2.4; Task 5) — `scripts/gateway_smoke.py` accepts `ChatCopilot` only when its transport is `caos.copilot.ask_copilot`, so a scripted `ask` still exits 2. For a Copilot model it runs both calls under `reserving(priced_request(price, <JSON request bytes>), credit=credit_price())`, as the executor does. Without that, a Copilot charge has no credit price and is unknown, and the JSON call would refuse `PROVIDER_RESPONSE_INVALID`. It prints `charge=<d> nano_aiu=<n|->`. `scripts/qualify.py`'s docstring adds a Copilot example, which `tests/test_qualify_script.py::test_each_docstring_example_expects_the_identity_its_endpoint_resolves_to` holds to `identity_of`. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_the_production_smoke_takes_copilot_only_on_a_real_transport`, `::test_the_production_smoke_needs_a_credit_price_for_copilot`.
- F601 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R2.1, F599; Task 5 fix round 1, MEDIUM 1) — `_floors` took the greatest rate each tier happened to state. A base tier with no `inputPrice` (only a cheap cache-read rate), or a `longContext` tier with no price at all, was priced by its other figures. A $1e-9 pin got `price_check=ok` (probes P1 and P2), even though every session asks for `context_tier="long_context"`. A multiplier of 0 passed any pin (P3). Now a listing is priced only when both the default and the `longContext` tier are present, each stating its own `inputPrice` and `outputPrice`, and the multiplier, when stated, is finite and above zero. Anything else is `price_check=unpriced` and refuses. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_a_listing_readiness_cannot_price_refuses_the_worker` (`zero-multiplier`, `infinite-multiplier`, `base-no-input-price`, `base-no-output-price`, `long-no-input-price`, `long-no-output-price`, `no-long-context-tier`, `long-context-tier-unpriced`).
- F602 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R3; Task 5 fix round 1, MEDIUM 2) — `scripts/qualify.py` documents a Copilot run, and its `_configured_provider` built the provider without readiness. A `gh-cli` seat with a $1e-9 pin reached a session (probe P11). After the identity check and before any reservation, it now calls `require_ready({provider.model: provider.price})`, the worker's own check, and returns None with `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED: no provider; nothing was spent` when that refuses. A gateway model is unaffected, since readiness starts nothing for it. Test: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_a_copilot_qualification_run_needs_a_ready_seat_before_any_call`.
- F603 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; F599; Task 5 fix round 1, LOW 3) — The SDK's own parsing raises `AssertionError` (`from_union`, e.g. a `batchSize` sent as text), `TypeError` or `KeyError`, and `CopilotClient.stop()` raises an `ExceptionGroup`. These escaped `_seat`, so `worker.main()` raised with a traceback instead of exiting 2 (probe P4c). Each now refuses with the typed line `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED reason=runtime_unready class=<name>`, with no error text. Tests: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_a_runtime_that_cannot_answer_readiness_refuses_by_class_alone`, `::test_a_worker_whose_runtime_fails_its_parsing_or_its_stop_exits_two_typed`.
- F604 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; F600; Task 5 fix round 1, LOW 4) — The smoke printed the JSON call's `charge` beside the plain call's `nano_aiu` (probe P6: `charge=0.003 nano_aiu=100000000`). For a Copilot model, `charge` is now the plain call's own AI units at the reservation's credit price (`copilot.settled_charge`), so both figures describe one call. A gateway endpoint, which states no AI units, keeps the JSON call's charge. Test: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_the_production_smoke_takes_copilot_only_on_a_real_transport` (each call now has its own bill).
- F605 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; AR-15; Task 5 fix round 1, LOW 5) — `ModelInfo.from_dict` keeps `supportedReasoningEfforts` as sent, so `usable`'s `in` matched a substring: the text `"xhigh"` made `@high` usable (probe P5). Only a list of names now takes an effort, matched exactly, and none listed means none taken. Any other value takes no effort, and its line prints `efforts=-`. Test: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_efforts_that_are_no_list_of_names_take_no_effort`.
- D78 (2026-10-07, owner decision (a) of 2026-10-06 in D77, addendum 2) — The Copilot CLI fallback transport: a model named `copilot-cli:<model>[@<effort>]`, answered by the Copilot CLI instead of the SDK, chosen by name and never automatically (D77). Designed in the plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-01-copilot-sdk-adapter.md`, Copilot 2/3, Task 4: the CLI's flags and deny rules under `child_environment`, the spend-free negative probe at readiness, a pinned `CLI_OUTPUT_TOKENS` cap per model) and deferred, not built: the owner chose the SDK transport only, until the firm's PC shows the SDK cannot be installed. Until then `caos.copilot.parsed` reads a `copilot-cli:` name so it is never taken for an endpoint, `output_cap` and `request_ceiling` refuse it `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`, and readiness refuses any approved list that names one (N188). Alternatives: build both transports now (a second untested transport, against fakes alone, for a case not yet shown to exist); drop the fallback (leaves no path if the SDK is barred on the PC). Evidence: `tests/test_context_bound.py::test_a_cli_copilot_model_has_no_ceiling_until_its_transport_is_built`, `tests/test_copilot.py::test_a_copilot_cli_name_is_refused_at_readiness_in_the_sdk_only_scope`.
- D79 (2026-10-07) — The worker on an analyst's Windows PC, where the Copilot seat is (D77). Four facts stopped it. `install_stop_handler` handled SIGTERM only, which a console never sends, so Ctrl+C raised `KeyboardInterrupt` mid-module: it now handles SIGTERM, SIGINT and (Windows) SIGBREAK, and only sets `stopping`. Closing the console window is no Python signal, so on Windows a console control handler (`SetConsoleCtrlHandler`) maps the close, logoff and shutdown events to the same stop and holds the process open until `main` has drained and released its lease, for at most `CLOSE_GRACE_SECONDS` (4, inside Windows' five); a console that refuses the handler is said on stderr (`CONSOLE_CLOSE_UNHANDLED`) and the worker runs on, and an unreleased lease expires on its own (D6). `caos/evidence/pdf.py` imported the POSIX-only `resource` module unguarded in the extraction child: `_limit_address_space` now returns `False` where there is none, and the parent states once per process, at `WARNING`, that the child is bounded by its deadline (the parent kills it, `SOURCE_EXTRACTION_TIMEOUT`) and by the decoded-bytes budget (`SOURCE_TOO_LARGE`) -- the limits that already bind on macOS -- so the cap is not skipped silently. And `vendor/deploy-v/**` and `icm/**` were `text: unspecified`, so Git for Windows' `core.autocrlf` changed the bytes the bundle manifest and `HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` verify: they, `tests/parity/golden/**` and `tests/fixtures/**` are now `-text` (the corpus documents already were). No Windows CI job: `check_gate_config.py` pins `ubuntu-latest` and widening it is the owner's call (N125). Not done: a Windows Job Object memory cap through `ctypes` (an untestable native binding for a bound the deadline and budget already give). Alternatives: WSL (one more install for IT). Evidence: `tests/test_pc_worker.py` simulates the platform; the worker's first start on a Windows clone is the real proof.
- F606 (2026-10-07, D79) — On Windows the worker had no orderly stop (Ctrl+C killed it mid-module, a closed window left the lease to expire), PDF extraction raised `ImportError`, and a default checkout converted the bundle's bytes. Fixed as D79. Tests: `tests/test_pc_worker.py`.
- D77, addendum 3 (2026-10-07; Task 7) — Status. The SDK transport is built: Copilot 1/3 (names, reply mapping, charge, SDK transport, dispatch, reservation credit price, settled check; F-series through Task 3) and Copilot 3/3 (readiness, the smoke and qualification gates, the worker on Windows, D79; F600-F605 for Task 5). The `copilot-cli:` fallback (D78, Copilot 2/3) is deferred, not dropped, and refused at readiness (N188). The live smoke is pending on the firm's seat (N185-N187): no run has used a real seat, so the nano-AIU unit, the listing's price units, the host form and the over-limit behaviour are assumptions the SDK's types and fakes support. The analyst's runbook is `docs/COPILOT_WORKER.md`. Evidence: `tests/test_copilot.py`; the digest command in the runbook was run against `caos.copilot.runtime_digest` on a sample directory and printed the same value.
- F607 (2026-10-07, D79, Task 6 fix round 1) — `.gitattributes` left `caos/calculators/cash_flow.py` (HOST_INTEGRITY's `scripts/cash_flow.py`, `verified_host_bytes`), `caos/deliverable/render.py` (`RENDERER_SHA256`) and `caos/deliverable/verify_package.py` (archived and compared byte for byte) convertible: with `core.autocrlf=true` the first gained 453 CRs and the host refused `AUTHORITY_BYTES_MISMATCH`. All three are `-text` now; the sweep of every sha256 pin found no other byte-hashed file (the qualification set digest is over parsed JSON). The runbook's stop and checkout notes were corrected. Test: `tests/test_pc_worker.py::test_the_byte_verified_trees_are_never_converted`.
- F608 (2026-10-07, Task 6 fix round 1) — `test_the_chat_model_carries_the_socket_deadline_and_never_retries` hung about 300 s: databricks-sdk 0.140.0 probes `/.well-known/databricks-config` in `Config.__init__` and retries for its default budget against an unserved host. The test patches `Config._resolve_host_metadata` and asserts the same (6 s); it was deselected in the Task 6 run and is not now.
- F609 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R4, D116; whole-branch review MEDIUM-1) — A `copilot:` model's request ceiling was the seat's listed context as read by the worker process that started (`CONTEXT_LISTED`, F586, F598), so a billed answer replayed by another worker (`replay_billed`, `_replayed_answer`) was re-fitted under that worker's listing and could be judged against other lines than its call showed, breaking D116's "same lines on replay" across processes. Every `copilot:` model is now declared statically beside D116's table, `caos.provider.COPILOT_CONTEXT_TOKENS` = 1,000,000 tokens, the long-context tier each session requests (source: owner, 2026-10-06, 1M); a pinned `CONTEXT_TOKENS` entry under a full `copilot:` name still precedes it. Readiness holds the seat's listed long-context `maxPromptTokens` to that figure as a floor and refuses `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` when it is lower or not listed (`context_check=ok|low|unlisted` on the model line); no other listed figure stands in for it. `CONTEXT_LISTED`, `declare_listed` and the per-process `_declare` path are removed, so F598's same-listing rule has nothing left to guard; `preflight.context_warnings` asks `copilot.context_tokens`. No migration. Tests: `tests/test_context_bound.py::test_two_workers_with_different_listings_fit_a_replay_alike`, `::test_a_copilot_model_is_declared_at_the_owner_context`, `::test_a_pinned_copilot_entry_is_read_under_its_full_name`, `tests/test_copilot.py::test_readiness_holds_the_long_context_listing_to_the_declared_floor`, `::test_a_listing_that_moved_changes_no_ceiling`.
- F610 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; R2.6; whole-branch review LOW-1) — The review read `unstored` in `execute_handoff` as a failed bill write and the over-reservation park as landing with no ledger row. It is the body's blob alone (F42): `bill` commits the charge with the address left empty, and a bill no ST-12 retry could write raises `STORE_UNAVAILABLE` inside `bill`, before `_settled_within` is asked, so no run parks on a charge the ledger does not hold. The order was kept and the comment now says this. No code change; two tests pin both paths and passed before the comment moved. Tests: `tests/test_canonical_execution.py::test_an_overrun_whose_body_was_not_stored_is_billed_before_it_parks` (ledger holds the whole charge, no diagnostic, `overspent` true, refused `BUDGET_CHARGE_OVER_RESERVATION`), `::test_an_overrun_whose_bill_was_not_written_is_a_store_fault_never_a_park` (`STORE_UNAVAILABLE`, no over-reservation line, no ledger row).
- F611 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; F600, A31; whole-branch review LOW-3) — `docs/COPILOT_WORKER.md` section 7 said the smoke makes one paid call; `scripts/gateway_smoke.py` makes two (the plain call and the JSON-mode call F27 requires), each a fresh session capped at the SDK's 30-credit floor, under the in-process `reserving` context alone, with no ledger reservation. The runbook now states the two calls, the absence of a ledger reservation and that the seat's GitHub budget, not `CAOS_RUN_CEILING`, bounds the spend. The smoke is unchanged: dropping the JSON call would let an endpoint that ignores JSON mode pass it (AR-17). Docs only; no test applies.
- F612 (2026-10-07, D77, addendum 2; ST-9, D117; whole-branch review Info) — `ChatCopilot._generate` handed `ask` its full `timeout` (720 s) on every try, so after a 429 re-send an abandoned session could run, and spend, up to 720 s past the worker's `call=deadline`. `models._invoked` now sets `caos.provider.call_seconds`, the seconds left of the one call deadline, in the context the call's thread runs in, and `ChatCopilot` asks for no more than the lesser of it and its own timeout; a gateway chat model ignores it. `docs/COPILOT_WORKER.md` section 8 now says the App's in-process worker refuses `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` at readiness whenever the approved list names a `copilot:` model, so `/api/health` `workers` rests on the PC worker (N124). Test: `tests/test_copilot.py::test_a_resent_call_asks_for_no_more_than_the_deadline_left` (failed with `720.0 == 618.0` before the change).

## Depth 1a, 2026-10-08 (owner: "Review latest frontend design and how to apply the changes", then "Apply the new design use global project settings")

- D122 (2026-10-08, owner decision of 8 October 2026; the Claude Design handoff "Adding depth to enterprise form", `CAOS Workspace.dc.html` token table `a`, `handoff/depth-notes.md`, `handoff/tokens-1a.css`; amends D37's ink primary and DESIGN.md's "Signal-only colour" and panel rule; keeps D66 and D74) — The workspace takes depth direction 1a, "Layered", at the theme layer, so every surface inherits it rather than any screen being restyled by hand. **Values:** `frontend/src/styles/tokens.css` takes `tokens-1a.css`'s light values on `:root` and its dark values in the dark variant: cool neutral tiers (canvas `oklch(0.975 0.004 255)` light, `oklch(0.195 0.009 260)` dark), cards raised off them, the sidebar the lowest tier, `--radius` 0.536rem (radius-xl, a card's, 12px), and a cobalt primary, `oklch(0.5 0.19 262)` light and `oklch(0.7 0.14 262)` dark. The primary sits 0.045 (light) and 0.033 (dark) from `--info` in OKLab, so the two read as one blue; the owner accepted that, and DESIGN.md now says running never rests on that hue alone, because every state is its mark and its word. **Names:** the handoff's 58 `--caos-*` tokens are folded into 17 surfaces and 9 shadows named in shadcn's pattern (brief 7.2: `--header`, `--card-border`, `--card-header`, `--card-footer`, `--well`, `--well-border`, `--table-head`, `--table-hover`, `--selected`, `--selected-border`, `--segment`, `--segment-active`, `--button`, `--button-border`, `--sidebar-hover`, `--route-edge`, `--route-dot`; `--main-shadow`, `--header-shadow`, `--card-shadow`, `--raised-shadow`, `--control-shadow`, `--primary-shadow`, `--recessed-shadow`, `--paper-shadow`, `--float-shadow`), each a utility through `@theme inline` (`bg-header`, `shadow-card`), so no component carries an arbitrary value. Equal values were merged rather than renamed: `sum-top` is `--card`, `hdr-fg` `--foreground`, `hdr-muted` `--muted-foreground`, `hdr-primary-*` `--primary`, `card-hd-border` `--border`; `sb-active-shadow`, `sb-case-shadow` and `seg-on-shadow` are `--raised-shadow`; `well-shadow`, `field-shadow` and `seg-shadow` are `--recessed-shadow`; the index takes the card's own. **Not taken:** `demo-*`, which would put back the warning wash D66 removed (the banner stays a line in the header's colour); `alert-*`, which the mock drew in the destructive hue over a notice the workspace gives its warning tone, and which named an undefined token (F613); `brand-text`, which `--info` and `--primary` already carry; `na-bg` and `border-strong`, which nothing in the workspace would hold; `hdr-chip-*`, as the header's badges keep their tones; and `r-card`/`r-ctl`, which are `radius-xl` and `radius-lg`. **Layout kept:** the mock drew key figures and caveats as wells inside one module card; in the D74 workbench they are panels of their own on the canvas, so they stay raised cards, and the route canvas, which does sit inside its panel, is the well. **Consumers:** `caos.css` (panels and their header band, tables, selected rows and set versions, fields, pills, the route well, edges and selected node, the module index as a card, the raised section in the sidebar, the paper), `charts.css` (the chart toggle as an outline button; nothing inside a plot), the shadcn primitives (`sidebar`, `button`, `badge`, `input`, `textarea`, `native-select`, `tabs`) and the shell (`SiteHeader`, `SectionSummary`, `CaseSwitcher`, `SectionTabs`). **Measured:** every text pair 4.5:1 and every edge and chart hue 3:1 in both themes, computed from the token values (125 pairs; tightest text 4.67, light inactive tab text on the tab track; tightest non-text 3.02, dark `--chart-4` on the card); the dark chart ramp passes the dataviz validator on the new dark card `#1b1e23` (worst all-pairs CVD ΔE 9.3); `charts.test.tsx` and the new `tokens.test.ts` hold them; the axe matrix is clean on Chromium in both themes once F615 is in (Firefox and WebKit are not installed in the build container; CI runs all three).
- F613 (2026-10-08, D122; handoff review) — `tokens-1a.css` set `--caos-alert-bg` and `--caos-alert-border` as `color-mix(in oklab, var(--danger) …)`, and the workspace defines no `--danger` (its token is `--destructive`). A `color-mix()` over an undefined `var()` is invalid at computed-value time, so the alert would have drawn with no fill and no edge, and no gate would have said so. The alert tokens were not taken (D122). The class of fault is now a test: no stylesheet may read a custom property that no stylesheet, `@property` or style prop defines. Test: `frontend/tests/unit/tokens.test.ts` "reads no custom property that no stylesheet, @property or style prop defines" (fails naming `--danger` when a rule reads it).
- F614 (2026-10-08, D122; handoff review) — `tokens-1a.css` remapped `--foreground` to a cool ink but left `--card-foreground`, `--popover-foreground`, `--secondary-foreground` and `--accent-foreground` at the old neutral ink, so text in a card or the summary (`text-card-foreground`) would have sat beside page text of another colour; it left `--sidebar-ring` on the old blue while `--ring` went cobalt; and in dark it raised the card to `oklch(0.235 0.01 260)` but left `--popover` at `oklch(0.205 0 0)`, so a menu would have drawn darker than the card it floats over, and `--secondary` and `--accent` neutral against cool surfaces. Each now follows its surface: the four text tokens are `--foreground`, `--sidebar-ring` is `--ring`, dark `--popover` is `oklch(0.255 0.012 260)` (fg 14.5:1, muted 6.4:1), and `--secondary` and `--accent` are `--muted`. The floating shadow, a light-only `rgb()` value, would also have been weaker in dark than the new card shadow; it is now `--float-shadow`, per theme and the largest. Tests: `frontend/tests/unit/tokens.test.ts` "sets card, menu, secondary and accent text in the page's own ink, in both themes" and "lifts a dark menu above the card it floats over, and a card above the canvas".
- F615 (2026-10-08, D122; the axe matrix on Chromium, dark, every Run fixture at every width) — A status badge drew its tint by mixing its hue with `transparent`, so its fill was whatever lay beneath it plus 15%. Depth put lighter surfaces under badges: on the dark panel header band (`--card-header`, `oklch(0.25 0.011 260)`), the route's "blocked" tally and a `crit` tag read `#ff6467` on `#412c31`, 4.46:1, under the 4.5:1 floor (16 entries, 20 nodes).
