The handoff measured its 4.68:1 on the card alone; the same mix also falls short on the dark table hover (4.43), the selected row (4.15) and a menu (4.42), states the matrix does not reach. A badge's fill is now its tint over the card, opaque, wherever it sits: `--success-tint`, `--warning-tint`, `--destructive-tint` and `--info-tint` are `color-mix(in oklab, var(--tone) var(--tint), var(--card))`, read by the shadcn `Badge` tones, the `.tag` tones and the citation chip. On the card they draw as before; no status hue and no depth surface changed. Test: `frontend/tests/unit/tokens.test.ts` "fills every status badge, tag and citation chip with its tint over the card, never over what lies under it" (fails when a `.tag` tone mixes with `transparent` again); the matrix, re-run, is clean.

## Charts for every module, 2026-10-10 (owner: "Where could charts and visualisations be used for modules which do not have any. Does the methodology need to be rewritten?", then "yes" to recording the map and building its first step)

- D123 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190–N193) — The methodology is not rewritten for charts. Every module's output profile already declares its registers' ids and columns (`completeness_check.load_contract`: about 300 registers across 22 profiles), and the bundle's own `find_registers` already locates them (the host calls it in `caos/methodology/handoff.py` and `caos/qualification/matrix.py`), so serving them typed is host work (N94) and what to draw from them is N190. Step 1 is frontend only: CP-1's `cp1.model_account_register`, served typed since D32 and drawn nowhere, now draws four figures (`sections/analysis/figures.tsx`). `accountLines`: EBITDA reported and adjusted, debt against cash, and the revolver drawn against committed, as lines of the register's values as served, nothing derived (no leverage, no net debt: a ratio is a calculation, and the browser makes none). A flow keeps to one type of period, the one with the most periods (the register's last period's on a tie), since a quarter beside a year draws a fall that is only their lengths; a balance keeps one period a date, since a quarter, a half-year and a twelve-month period ending together state one balance three times; both run by the period register's end dates, since its listing order is not time's (the demo's lists FY2025 before its quarters). `cashFlowBridge`: the latest period by end date that states both ends, bridged as the bundle's own model bridges it (`cp_model_v3/calculations.py`: CFO, capex, acquisitions, debt, equity, dividends and other, to the stated net change in cash), drawn by D61's `WaterfallChart`, unused until now. A step the register does not list is left out, one it lists without a value is a gap carrying its status, and where the steps miss the stated change the chart's residual says by how much (CP-MODEL's `ncf_variance`). The selected-figure panel hard-coded every pressed mark as "Model-authored, not host-verified", which a residual is not; `FigurePick` now carries the mark's origin, and a residual reads as computed here, not served. Earnings read after the KPIs and before the add-backs that bridge them, the cash-flow bridge after the add-backs, and the balances before the maturities. The demo fixture gains no account register: its figures would be invented for a named issuer, so the tests build one from made-up values over the fixture's period register, and the demo and the axe matrix draw none of the four until a real run's register is captured (N191). Alternatives: revenue on the earnings figure (it flattens EBITDA, and CP-1's segment schedule already draws revenue); one figure per account (eight cards for one register); net debt and leverage computed in the browser (a calculation, not a figure served). Evidence: `figures.test.tsx` (`accountLines`, `cashFlowBridge`, the reading order, and the residual's origin in the panel).

## Registers served typed, 2026-10-10 (owner: "Step 2: N94 and the first five charts", then "add to plans building all the charts stated")

- D124 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N94, N190) — Each accepted handoff now serves the registers its module's output profile declares, typed, beside D32's tagged tables: `HandoffView.registers` (`RegisterView`: `register_id`, `columns` as written, `declared`, `rows`) with its own `registers_unavailable_reason`. The host reads them with the bundle's own locator, the call `check()` makes at acceptance: `completeness_check.load_contract` over the module's verified `SKILL.md`, then `_locate_registers` (`find_registers`' own body, which also returns each row's cell count) with the declared and retired ids, so a figure reads the table the gate verified. Each cell is `figure_value`'s (Decimal, never the vendor's float), and D32's bounds and codes apply. `declared` names, for each header cell, the profile column the bundle's `_resolve_columns` binds to it, the resolution `check()` applied, so the browser reads a register by the profile's column names however the model spelled the header (`Headroom (x)` is `Headroom`); a template column (`Period 1…N`) binds none, and its cells keep their own header text; a cell two columns claim goes to the one whose key it spells, else to none (T4C.4 written without `Test` keeps `Test Type`). The vendor's private functions are called deliberately: its bytes are pinned (invariant 4), `handoff._pipe_tables` already calls `cp_tables._split_row` and `_row_cells`, the tests pin both shapes, and a re-pin must re-check them. Refused as `TABLES_MALFORMED`, all registers at once since a partial set would read as the whole: a row of another width than its header (the vendor pads and cuts, which served a current basis of `3.50x` as the threshold), a header with an empty or repeated cell (the vendor's row keeps the last), and a vendor `KeyError` or `ValueError` on the answer (a CP-1A title spelled with `İ` or `ı`; acceptance's `check()` refuses that answer and `verify_accepted` re-runs it on every read, so the reader refuses what it should never see). A module with no output profile (CP-CF) serves none, which is not a refusal. Registers ride their own list, so a register past a bound never withholds the tagged tables. How often a real answer's registers are refused whole is not yet measured (N194). Cost: the read locates each handoff's registers a second time (`verify_accepted`'s `check()` is the first), 1.7 ms against 2.1 ms on the CP-1A fixture, and reads and hashes the module's `SKILL.md`; no store or blob read. Alternatives: the registers in `tables` (one bound and one reason for both, so a ragged register would withhold CP-1's tables); a host copy of `_resolve_columns` (a second reading that can drift from the gate's); rows keyed by the declared name alone (the header's own spelling, which can carry a unit, would be lost). The demo fixtures serve no registers (N191). Evidence: `tests/test_handoff_tables.py`, `tests/test_wire_contract.py`, `tests/test_model_section.py`, `tests/test_analysis_section.py`, `frontend/tests/unit/wire-contract.test.ts`.

## The bullet chart, 2026-10-10 (owner: "Step 2: N94 and the first five charts"; N190's bullets)

- D125 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — A new chart kind, `BulletChart` (`frontend/src/charts/BulletChart.tsx`), on D61's band geometry, which gains markers (`BandMarker`: a `rule` across a category's band at a value, or a `dot`), each a focusable mark grown to 24px, after its category's bars in the keyboard order, counted in the value axis's extent, and drawn as an n/a gap with its reason when its value is null. A bullet row is a test: its current basis a bar from zero, its threshold a rule (dashed when model-authored, as a model line is; solid for the host), labelled by direction (`ceiling`, `floor`, or `direction not stated`). Headroom is never drawn and never computed: it prints as served in the marks' names and the table twin, since the bundle's `covenant_headroom.py` exists because a computed headroom with the wrong sign reads as cushion. The rule's accessible name leads with its own word (`ceiling 5.00x against a current basis of 3.70x`), not the bar's, so a row's two buttons never share a name. Alternatives: a grouped bar of threshold against current basis (it reads as two magnitudes, not a measure against a limit); headroom as a third bar (a figure the host would compute). Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/charts.test.tsx` (names, gaps, the twin, keyboard order, hit boxes).

## The range strip, 2026-10-10 (N190's ranges)

- D126 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — A second new chart kind, `RangeStripChart` (`frontend/src/charts/RangeStripChart.tsx`): a row per metric, its interquartile range a bar floating Q1 to Q3, min, median and max as rules, and one dot (the borrower, or an implied value) under a label the caller names. The interquartile bar floats because a range is an interval, not a magnitude, as a bridge's steps float (DESIGN.md's "bars start at zero" is for magnitudes); the axis still holds zero. A statistic a register does not declare is absent (nothing drawn, its twin column left out); one it declares without a value is a gap with its reason. A pressed interquartile bar selects an interval (`ChartSelection.interval`), never a single value it does not have. Two changes to D125's marks, from its review: legend swatches draw a `rule` and a `dot` as the marks are (dashed or solid, hollow or filled), and a bar sharing its band with markers is drawn thinner than the rule (in a 26px row, 14px against 22px), so a rule reads past both sides of it. `said` and an origin-aware `cellOf` are shared in `charts/series.ts`. Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/charts.test.tsx`.

## The figure files' shared module, 2026-10-10 (review of N190's first figures)

- F616 (2026-10-10; review of the first register figures, N190) — `figures.tsx` and `register-figures.tsx` imported each other, safe only while neither built a value from the other at module scope, and `Figure` was one interface whose per-kind rows were optional, so a figure missing its rows drew an empty chart through `?? []`. Fixed with no behaviour change: what both files share moved to a leaf module, `sections/analysis/figure-core.ts`, and `Figure` became a union discriminated by `kind`, each kind's rows required, `Chart` switching exhaustively. Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/figures.test.tsx`, `register-figures.test.tsx` (unchanged in what they assert).

## Register figures; covenant headroom, 2026-10-10 (N190's first five)

- D127 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190, after D124) — The browser draws figures from D124's registers in `sections/analysis/register-figures.tsx`, beside the tagged tables' `figures.tsx` (the owner's brief named `figures.tsx` for the first five; thirty figures would triple it). A register is read only for its own module (`registerRows`), since register ids repeat across modules (`T4.6` is CP-1's, CP-1B's and CP-1C's), its rows keyed by the profile's declared columns (`declared[i] ?? columns[i]`). Rows of one unit share a figure, one value axis, and a figure per unit (`x`, `%`, or none, read from the cell's text past trailing citation markers), since an axis never carries two units. First: CP-4's T4C.4 covenant headroom as bullets, a row per test, threshold and current basis as served, headroom printed and never computed, the direction read only from `Test Type` through the bundle's own aliases (`covenant_headroom.py` `TEST_TYPE_ALIASES`, normalised as its `normalise_test_type` does), never from the test's name: a `Test Type` outside them reads "direction not stated". Every mark is model-authored (N193). A register refusal withholds the register figures only and a tables refusal the table figures only. Alternatives: inferring direction from "leverage" or "coverage" (the failure the bundle's script exists to remove); one figure per test (a card per row). Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/register-figures.test.tsx` (a ceiling, a floor, an unknown direction, a null threshold, two units splitting, another module's id), `figures.test.tsx`.

- D128 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — CP-1C's T4.6 peer distribution as range strips: a row per metric from `Min`, `Q1`, `Median`, `Q3` and `Max`, the borrower's value the dot, grouped by unit as D127's; the summary prints each borrower value and `Borrower Position` as served, and the source line `Peer Avg` and `N`. Nothing is computed: no percentile, no rank. A pressed interquartile bar's selected-figure panel reads "Q1 to Q3" with both ends as served. The figures view names each refusal present (`data-tables-unavailable`, `data-registers-unavailable`), and either source still draws when the other is refused. Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/register-figures.test.tsx`, `figures.test.tsx`.

## The register bridges, 2026-10-10 (N190's bridges)

- D129 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — CP-1D's T1D.4 quality-adjusted EBITDA bridge as D61's waterfall, read by `bridgeFigure` (`register-figures.tsx`), which later bridges reuse by parameter. The first row opens the bridge (its `Amount`, else its cumulative level); a later row with an `Amount` figure is a change as served; one with only a cumulative figure is a stated total; one with neither is a change gap carrying its text; and a closing total follows only when the last row is a change that states its level, so the summary names an end only when it is a total. The schema's empty bridge (one `NONE` row) draws nothing. Each change wears its status's colour (`Supported` positive, `Challenged` series-3, `Rejected` negative, `Insufficient Information` series-4; any other word none), and the word is in its accessible name and its twin row, so colour never carries it alone; the legend lists each status worn and keeps Increase and Decrease while an uncoloured change draws a pole, so Supported and Increase share a hue. The running level a change's name and twin print is the host's exact sum of the steps (`bridge.ts`), not the register's own cumulative column, which the schema says "reconciles step by step": where the two disagree mid-bridge, the next stated total's Unreconciled residual says by how much. Evidence: `register-figures.test.tsx`, `charts.test.tsx`.

- D130 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — CP-2D's T2E.5 liquidity bridge and CP-4C's T4E.5 value allocation as waterfalls. The one figure drawn other than as served: a use the method subtracts (cash interest, cash taxes, mandatory capex, debt amortisation and maturities, other cash uses: REF_CP-2D step 05 and `liquidity_bridge.py`, which take them as magnitudes) and an allocation of the available value are drawn as the served magnitude negated, exact string work, and each step's name and twin row say so ("served 45, a use the method subtracts"), so the sign is never read as the model's. Beginning and ending accessible liquidity are stated totals; rows before the beginning total bridge from zero. The value allocation draws one waterfall per scenario and entity, from the group's available value to its last residual; the fulcrum class is named from T4E.6, matched by scenario, and marked on the claim that starts its text (the longest such claim). Evidence: `register-figures.test.tsx`, `charts.test.tsx`.

- D131 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — CP-1D's T1E.3 adjusted-debt bridge, through D129's reader by parameter (no status column, so no colours), and CP-1C's T4.10 implied enterprise value by method as D126's range strips: low, median and high, the implied EV the dot, no quartiles (absent, not gaps). And, before more stacks land: CP-1's maturity wall's stack builder moved to `figure-core.ts` (`stackFigure`, its output unchanged: a segment the exact sum of its rows, labelled a sum in its source line; a class and year with no row zero; one whose every row is unstated a gap), and D127's grouping takes any key with its title suffix (unit, currency, agency). Evidence: `register-figures.test.tsx`, `figures.test.tsx`.

## Register stacks and grouped bars, 2026-10-10 (N190's stacks)

- D132 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — Stacks from four registers on D131's builder: CP-2D's T2E.2 liquidity by accessibility and T2E.3 cash uses by timing (mandatory against discretionary), CP-2E's T2F.2 fixed against floating debt and CP-3C's T3D.2 maturity wall. Amounts in different currencies never share an axis: T2F.2 and T3D.2 draw a figure per `Currency`; T2E.2 and T2E.3 declare none, so they draw one figure with no unit (N192). A maturity year is read only from a date that starts with four digits, CP-1's rule, so any other spelling reads "Undated" (N195). Evidence: `register-figures.test.tsx`.

- D133 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — Grouped and stacked bars from CP-3C, CP-4 and CP-4C: T3D.8 exposure under the base, stress and LME cases as grouped bars; T4C.5 basket usage stacked on remaining capacity, its `Estimated Capacity` named in the source and never drawn or checked against the two; T4E.7 allowed claim against total recovery by class, per scenario and currency. Where a row's cells state different units, the row appears in each unit's figure, and a cell of another unit is a gap that says where it is drawn: no unit is invented and no axis carries two. CP-4C's T4E.2 draws nothing, and T4E.7's consideration column is not drawn, since each packs several figures in one cell that the bundle's reader cannot split (N192). A grouped bar chart is a `bars` kind of D127's figure union. Evidence: `register-figures.test.tsx`, `figures.test.tsx`.

## Register lines, tornadoes, scores and new chart kinds, 2026-10-10 (N190's remaining map)

- D134 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — CP-2G's T2H.4 forecast (revenue, EBITDA, FCF) and T2H.6 credit path (gross/net leverage, coverage, FCF/debt) as base-against-downside lines, one figure per metric, the cases ordered BASE, DOWNSIDE, then the rest (trimmed and case-insensitive, the order CP-2G's tagged `forecastDrivers` now shares from `figure-core.ts`). A packed `gross/net leverage` cell is a gap carrying its text (N192). Where one period and case is stated twice and the cells differ, the point is a gap naming every text ("stated twice: 4.0x, 5.0x"); identical rows draw the value. That rule, never the first row silently, holds for every register figure keyed by a column the model can repeat. Evidence: `register-figures.test.tsx`, `figures.test.tsx`.

- D135 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — Tornadoes from CP-2E's T2F.5, CP-2A's T2B.6 and CP-3D's T3E.8 (per scenario): D61's diverging bars, categories ordered by the size of their value, largest first, gaps last (a float orders the marks and never prints). CP-2H's T2R.4 rating triggers as D125's bullets, one figure per agency and unit: a trigger is its metric, rating type and stated direction, so an upgrade and a downgrade trigger on one metric are two rows; the threshold and the case or period value as served (a packed cell a gap), the headroom printed and never computed, and the direction read only through `covenant_headroom.py`'s aliases, since CP-2H's own SKILL.md names that script as the reader of `trigger direction`. Evidence: `register-figures.test.tsx`.

- D136 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — Score registers as horizontal bars: CP-3's T3.3 weighted factor scores and T3.7 composite score by rank, CP-4's T4.11 legal area scores and CP-6's T6A.6 debate scores, each as served, the summary naming the highest and the lowest (a float orders them). Evidence: `register-figures.test.tsx`.

- D137 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — A third new chart kind on D125's band markers and D126's spans, the dumbbell (`charts/DumbbellChart.tsx`), and CP-8's T7.4 expected against realised with it: a row per metric, two dots joined by a line, a null end a gap and no line, grouped by unit; the variance prints as served and nothing is computed from the two ends. Evidence: `charts.test.tsx`, `register-figures.test.tsx`.

- D138 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — A fourth new chart kind, the scatter (`charts/ScatterChart.tsx`), the first on a numeric x axis and the first to use Recharts' `ScatterChart` and `Scatter` (read against the installed 3.10.1 types; no Tooltip or Legend, so the marks stay this app's), and CP-3D's T3E.3 spread against maturity with it. The x axis is time: a date is placed by its UTC day only when written `YYYY-MM-DD`, `YYYY-MM` or `YYYY` (a day that does not exist is refused; `03/04/2031` is never guessed); a point without a readable date or a figure is not placed, is listed in the table twin with its reason, and the caption counts it. Points are coloured by seniority. A spread written with its unit ("350bps") is not a figure to the bundle's reader, so it is unplaced (N196). Evidence: `charts.test.tsx`, `register-figures.test.tsx`.

- D139 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — A probability and impact matrix (`charts/RiskMatrix.tsx`) and CP-2A's T5.4 with it: a four-by-four grid of the ordinal labels REF_CP-2B step 05 declares (High, Medium, Low, Unknown), any other text counted as Unknown and kept in the names. It is drawn as SVG through D61's frame, not by a Recharts chart (Recharts has no grid), so the frame's marks, keyboard order, table twin and provenance rules hold; this is DESIGN.md's one exception to "drawn by Recharts". A cell's count of rows is the one figure computed here, and a figure that computes says so where a reader inspects it: `FigurePick.computed` prints "Computed here: a count of the model's rows, not served" in place of the origin line. Evidence: `charts.test.tsx`, `register-figures.test.tsx`, `figures.test.tsx`.

- D140 (2026-10-10, owner decision of 10 October 2026; N190) — Counts in the module's Audit tab, the map's last row: CP-5's T5.9 issue log by module and severity (the log consolidates T5.2–T5.8, so those are not counted again; severities in the reference's order, CRITICAL, MATERIAL, MINOR, matched case-insensitively), and each CP-L10 topic register (TL10.2 to TL40.2) by materiality and disposition. A count of rows is an exact integer, the one figure computed here: every title says "count", every mark is named "a count of the model's rows" (drawn hatched, since the rows are the model's), a value of one takes the singular, and a pressed count shows D139's "Computed here" panel inside the Counts group, where it was pressed. Evidence: `register-figures.test.tsx`, `analysis.test.tsx`.

## Citation tests count work, 2026-10-11 (CI runs 38092324317 and 38092342682)

- F620 (2026-10-11; D105, F501, F493 fix round 1; CI runs 38092324317 and 38092342682) — Three citation tests guarded their cost with a wall clock, so a shared runner or a loaded machine failed them with no code change: `test_excerpts_of_a_long_page_are_found_in_one_search_each` measured 2.09 s and 2.6 s against its 2.0 s bound on CI, failing the `test` job on PRs that change no Python; `test_a_near_miss_never_measures_a_long_line` 0.53 s against 0.5 s locally; and `test_near_places_cost_one_scan_each_on_adversarial_pages` held 64 repetitions to 3.0 s. Each now counts the work its name states, with the real search and the real ratio still answering. The two excerpt tests hand a page's flats their joined keys as a `str` that counts `find` calls. Each of the 512 excerpts of the long page costs exactly two, the hit and the miss that ends the one scan; a search on the first word with a Python step per partial match failed it at 16,438 for one excerpt, and a scan of the keys in Python at 0. On the adversarial pages a quote refused `CITATION_NOT_LOCATED` costs four finds (eight of one repeated word) or three (a first word that is no key), one scan per needle and one interior check per edge pass; the pre-fix `_one_excerpt` (`7ccfc3f2^`) over today's `places`, which found the quote's interior and rejected each near place in Python, failed it at 68,573. The near-miss test records every `difflib.SequenceMatcher` `near_line` builds: none for the 5,000-word look-alikes or a long line sharing both ends, and exactly `NEAR_MEASURED` of `NEAR_MEASURED_WORDS` words a side at the cap; with `_similar`'s word bound removed it failed on 64 ratios of 5,000 words. No production code changed and no gate moved. Tests: `tests/test_excerpt_citations.py::test_excerpts_of_a_long_page_are_found_in_one_search_each`, `::test_near_places_cost_one_scan_each_on_adversarial_pages`, `tests/test_whole_line_citations.py::test_a_near_miss_never_measures_a_long_line`.

## Credit model calculator audit round, 2026-10-11 (Book/Model catch-up series C1-C6; adversary review of slices 03-13 and live run 9b9e2b72)

- F623 (2026-10-11; Book/Model credit-model calculator, `caos/calculators/credit_model/` and `caos/methodology/model_inputs.py`; an independent adversarial review of the calculator as landed from snapshot f823060f found 6 MAJOR and 12 MINOR findings, and a live run, 9b9e2b72, found more; owner decision: land the fixes before the Model read serves any figure) — Each fix is one commit on the Book/Model branch and lands in the catch-up series C1-C6, which stacks between slices 30 and 31. **MAJOR-1 and MAJOR-2, periods by their dates.** The calculator read a YTD's length from `fiscal_quarter`, which CP-1 leaves null on every period that is not a QUARTER (the example register broke the contract the same way), so the prior-year YTD (PYTD) was not found and the LTM's flows were all `COMPONENT_MISSING`; and quarters, PYTDs and the twelve months ending a quarter were paired by label without a span check, so a 9M labelled quarter 2 became the prior H1 and a Q1 gave a 15-month LTM. A YTD now takes its quarter from its whole months (`_ytd_quarter`); the PYTD is the prior year's YTD of the same quarter, else the one YTD of the same whole months a year earlier by its dates, else the prior year's quarters, and counts only with the YTD's whole months twelve months earlier; calculated quarters, quarters to date and a sparse item's twelve months need every quarter three whole months and each starting the month after the last, else `COMPONENT_MISSING`, noted `UNSPANNED`. A span allows `SLACK_DAYS` (7) either way so 52/53-week calendars resolve, adjacency stays exact. A period is built from its parts only when they tile it. A sparse item's periods are worked out shortest first, so 200 nested periods no longer raise `RecursionError` (MINOR-7). **MAJOR-3, opening debt.** A facility with no row by the opening date was taken as a new issue, nil at the opening, so a term loan CP-1 wrote only from Q3 2025 opened the LTM 2,340 low with no note. It is now a new issue only where CP-1's `total_debt` at the opening agrees, within its rounding, with the opening stack less its finance leases; otherwise the opening is CP-1's stated figure plus those leases, noted "facility history incomplete" (as is the residual), and `COMPONENT_MISSING` without a stated figure or with a facility in another currency left out. **MAJOR-4, bases.** `PeriodRow` reads `accounting_basis` and `entity_perimeter`; a period whose basis or perimeter differs from the anchor period's (case and separators aside, not stated matches anything) enters no other period's figure, balance or stack, and the limitations name it. **MAJOR-5, silent nil.** Every sum that takes a part with no row as nothing (a sparse cover, a sparse quarter or LTM equal to its FY, an add-back with no row, lease and interest in financing, a PF facility the LTM has no balance for) is noted "not stated, treated as zero (part)", and the note reaches every figure built from it. A sparse LTM with some of its FY, YTD and PYTD stated and another without a row is `COMPONENT_MISSING`, noted `PARTLY_STATED`, never a sum over 18 or 6 months. **MAJOR-6, PF rows.** The limitations name each period with PF rows but no PF column and the number of adjustments not applied. **Live run 9b9e2b72.** An account row marked Not Available or Not Calculable has no value whatever figure it carries; `calc`, the ratios, statements, `_pf_add`, days ratios and the projection keep the missing parts' notes instead of a bare `COMPONENT_MISSING`; opening cash never carries a negative zero. **MINOR.** MINOR-2 the debt residual and MINOR-11 an excluded add-back keep their notes; MINOR-8 a percent growth driver is rounded half to even to `MAX_DECIMALS`; MINOR-9 a positive cash tax is a refund and keeps its sign; MINOR-12 two loose assertions are exact; MINOR-5 the PF roll-forward doc says where it foots; MINOR-3, 4 and 6 were already resolved. **MINOR-10 and its cost.** A fixed coupon from 0 to 30 percent is taken as stated, so a zero-coupon convertible prices; a coupon written as a decimal fraction (0.065 for 6.5%) now reads as 0.065%, so Model figures that depend on a fixed coupon are wrong until the follow-up in N198 (owner decision: land now, fix later). Mutmut on the round's functions left 45 survivors; tests now kill 19, and the other 26 are not individually recorded. Evidence: `tests/test_credit_model_history.py`, `_statements.py`, `_debt.py`, `_cells.py`, `_projection.py`, `_assemble.py` and `tests/test_model_inputs.py`.
- F624 (2026-10-11; F623, N198; the second calculator catch-up increment, C6, cut from Book/Model commit cf57f3de; the Book/Model session's calc round 3 and the re-verification of 855b4bcf; owner decision: land the committed tip now, later fixes follow as further increments) — Six changes to `caos/calculators/credit_model/` and `caos/methodology/model_inputs.py`. **Coupon as a fraction (closes the cost F623 recorded for MINOR-10; a732f9a3, 855b4bcf).** The reader (`coupon_percent`) takes a fixed coupon strictly between 0 and 0.5 as a decimal fraction written in place of the percent and reads it times 100 (`0.065` is 6.5), flagging the row; the figure then faces the range 0 (a zero-coupon convertible) or 0.5-30 inclusive (`coupon_in_range`, shared with `projection`). The facility's projected cells carry "coupon read as a fraction" and the stack shows the converted percent. The reading applies only to a `FIXED` row's coupon, where it prices; any other row keeps `fixed_rate` as read, and a coupon written with `%` is the percent it states (`0.065%` prices at 0.065%, no note). Cost if wrong: a genuine sub-0.5% fixed coupon reads 100 times over, noted. **Whole-week spans (cb660658).** A 53-week filer's 14-week January-March quarter is 98 days against 90, past `SLACK_DAYS` (7), so it was no quarter and every YTD and PYTD holding it went with it. A span whose length is a whole number of weeks is now n whole months when the day after it ends lies within `WEEK_SLACK_DAYS` (14) of its first day plus n months; every other span keeps 7; two n within the slack (months 28 days apart) are none. **A twelve-month window is one span (42d1b34b).** Four quarters each within the slack of three months could sum 392 days (four 14-week quarters) and FY + YTD - PYTD 379 days (a 27-week YTD less a 25-week PYTD). The trailing window now needs its span, first quarter's start to last quarter's end, to be twelve whole months under the same slack, and an LTM needs its YTD and PYTD within `SLACK_DAYS` of each other's length; otherwise `COMPONENT_MISSING`, noted `UNSPANNED`. A real 53-week year and a 27-week H1 against a 26-week one still resolve. **Footings are separated pairwise (e7870970).** A sum combined periods whose stated bases differed whenever the anchor stated none (AS_REPORTED FY + RESTATED PYTD gave an LTM of 950, no note). Every figure now takes the footing of the periods it reads (a column's own and its terms', a period and its components, a sparse period and its parts with a figure, the four quarters of twelve months): where two state a basis, or a perimeter, differently it is `COMPONENT_MISSING`, noted that the basis or perimeter differs; not stated still matches anything. The limitation names those periods too (`uncombined_periods`). An off-footing period without its own row of a sparse item is no figure, never its cover from parts on the anchor's footing. **Growth across footings (cf57f3de).** FY2025's revenue, adjusted EBITDA and business-unit growth were computed against a restated FY2024 beside a limitation saying FY2024 is not combined. Growth now reads each column's footing (`column_footings`): against a prior whose footing differs from the column's or the anchor's, or a column whose own periods differ, it is `COMPONENT_MISSING`, noted that the basis or perimeter differs. **Tests (4e549bbc, 7f1e486c).** Three test preconditions assert exact values, and a test bounds the sparse walk's nesting at `MAX_NESTING`, killing `_resolve_within`'s two surviving mutants. Findings closed from the stack review (adversary-c) and the re-verification of 855b4bcf: MAJOR-B (a 53rd week in Q1), MINOR-A and MINOR-B (the footing bypasses), MINOR-C (compounding slack), M1, M2, M3, L1, L3. Still open after this increment, in `next.md`: N199-N203. The Book/Model session's round 4 continues; its uncommitted work is not in this increment. Evidence: `tests/test_credit_model_history.py`, `_cells.py`, `_metrics.py`, `_projection.py` and `tests/test_model_inputs.py`.
- F625 (2026-10-11; F624, N199, N200, N201, N202(a); the third and last calculator catch-up increment, C8, cut from Book/Model commit 8fc533bb; the Book/Model session's calc round 4 and its task review (mx-precise, approved); owner decision: the calculator is frozen at 8fc533bb) — Five changes to `caos/calculators/credit_model/`. **An add-back absent from an interim or a quarter is nil, noted (MAJOR-A; 8501730d, 0e9550bf).** CP-1 writes an add-back only for the periods it occurred in, so one begun in the YTD, ended before it, or a one-off in one quarter made the LTM's and the quarters' twelve-month adjusted EBITDA and adjusted leverage `COMPONENT_MISSING` (Q2 2026's adjusted leverage 3.4574 gone). An add-back's interim or quarter without a row is now nothing, named "not stated, treated as zero" on the cell and on every figure built from it (`_named_sum`; the nested interims carry their inner notes). Only add-backs (`addback:` keys) take this; a row that exists but is NOT_STATED, and a missing quarter column, are still refused. Recurring flows classed in financing (`interest_paid_in_financing`, `lease_payments_in_financing`) keep the one-sided and all-four refusals: never an 18-month sum. A test pins MAJOR-B (a 53-week year with the extra week in Q1 resolves its LTM to CP-1's 11654). Cost if wrong: a programme CP-1 omitted from an interim by mistake reads as nil, noted. **A DEBT ADJUSTMENT row classed a finance lease is never a lease (Minor D; da71624b).** The opening-debt check subtracted such a row as a lease while the closing kept it in total debt, so a genuine new issue read as missing history (opening 11620, noted, not 11790). Both now share one finance-lease test (`debt._finance_lease`). **A positive cash tax served as a refund says so (Minor E; ad5d3e9f, 8fc533bb).** CP-1 writes inflows positive, so a positive cash tax is kept as a refund; a model that wrote its outflow positive read that way silently. The `cash_taxes` cell is now noted "positive: read as a tax refund" wherever it is above nil; nil gets no note and the value is unchanged. **The implied rate carries an opening whose facility history is incomplete (L2; e0cd5c79).** Where the LTM's opening debt is CP-1's stated total because a facility's earlier rows are missing, the implied rate built from it, a facility priced at it (`facility_rate`'s note) and a weighted rate with no facility mix now say "facility history incomplete"; `facility_rate` takes the implied rate as a cell so its note travels with its figure. **Mutation round (8fc533bb).** `_component`'s footing check never decided anything (`_flow` refuses the same figure first, the same way) and is dropped; `column_footings` reads the worked-out footings directly; `_apart` takes the prior's footing; a cash tax of nil is no refund while any figure above nil is. **The calculator is frozen at 8fc533bb.** The Book/Model session's task review of a1ead337..8fc533bb approved it with five low items parked as N204-N208; the live proof is N209, and the C6-C8 adversary pass (MERGEABLE-FOR-31, no critical or high) adds N210 (a sub-0.5% fixed coupon priced 100 times over, MEDIUM) and N211, and raises N207 to MEDIUM. Findings closed here: stack adversary MAJOR-A, MINOR-D, MINOR-E and re-verification L2 (MAJOR-B was already fixed by cb660658 and is now pinned by a test). Evidence: `tests/test_credit_model_history.py`, `_debt.py`, `_projection.py`, `_statements.py`, `_assemble.py` and `tests/test_model_inputs.py`.

## Timing tests count work, 2026-10-11 (after F620; a survey of every clock-dependent test)

- F621 (2026-10-11; F620, N197; MAX-06, EV-2, N39, F497, ED-8, N36; CI runs 36362168428, 36361358596, 36242538704, 36224801112 and 36100611499) — After F620 a survey of every test whose result hangs on the clock found the ones seen failing and every remaining cost bound; each now asserts the property its name states without a clock, through wrappers that count and delegate to the real code, and each was shown failing on the regression it was written for. `test_quote_normalisation.py::test_a_long_near_match_costs_no_more_than_the_page` (`< 1.0` s; 1.01 to 1.26 s on CI five times) counts the matcher's own steps (`_extended`, `_key`, `_edge_equal`) against `4 * (page + quote)` at each step and asks that `_match_at` never run, so the pre-MAX-06 search, `_match_at` from every start, fails at its first call; a search that bypasses those steps is not held. The three `test_whole_line_citations.py::test_placing_*_stays_linear_in_the_lines_delivered` (`< 0.5` s) count one candidate test per line delivered and at most `NEAR_MEASURED` measurements; a rescan per candidate (21,600 looks for 10,800 lines) or a raised cap fails them. `test_canonical_handoff.py`'s three refused-at-once tests (`< 1.0` s, seven cases) hold the vendor's validators unread (EV-2's backtracking heading expression, N39's reader size) while the handoff is refused `HANDOFF_MALFORMED`; each host bound removed reaches them once and fails; the host bounds' own cost is not counted. `test_evidence_selection.py::test_a_space_padded_or_overlong_item_names_nothing_and_answers_at_once` (`< 2.0` s) holds a 20,026-character item away from the page matchers, alone and now beside a mapped item, which also covers F497's `_unmapped_fault` bound. `test_forecast_handoff.py::test_unclosed_block_openers_cost_no_more_than_their_length` (`< 1.0` s) counts two opener and two closer searches; a scan past an unclosed opener makes 8,002 and 8,001. `test_identity_platform.py::test_a_trickling_workspace_is_given_up_at_the_deadline` (`< 2.0` s; failed locally under load) drives identity's clock one step per receive of a lockstep trickle: three requests share one exchange that gives up after four receives, its helper thread exits, and the workspace is never read by a path the trickle did not feed; the lookup without ED-8's deadline answers instead of refusing, and a helper that drains the body after the deadline starves the trickle and fails. `::test_a_cached_caller_is_served_while_a_cold_burst_waits` (`< pause / 2`, ordered by a 0.5 s sleep; failed locally under load) waits on events until all 45 burst requests are in and the workspace holds the one lookup, then asks that the cached caller get past identity, with no workspace call, before the workspace answers (whatever the store then says), and that the burst end with `SCIM_WAITING_LIMIT` served and the rest refused `IDENTITY_UNAVAILABLE`; a cache check moved behind the waiting slot or the open flight fails it, and the old test passed with the first. No production code changed and no gate moved; the tests still timed are N197.

## Workbench navigation test waits for focus, 2026-10-11 (PRs #134 and #148)

- F622 (2026-10-11; PRs #134 and #148) — `frontend/tests/workbench/navigation.spec.ts` "demo Model, Report, and Committee routes render parsed v1 content" failed in Firefox at `expect(record).toBeVisible()` (line 133), seen on CI for both PRs and in 2 of 15 local runs. After the second Escape closed the evidence drawer the test went on at once: `expect(record).toBeHidden()` holds trivially, then `summary.focus()` and Enter. The drawer (`evidence/Overlay.tsx`, Base UI Dialog) stays mounted and the page behind it inert through its exit transition and returns focus to the opener chip only at the end, so the focus call was ignored or overwritten and Enter reached the C1 marker, not the summary. A test defect, not an app one: Escape returning focus to the opener is the contract. The test now waits for the opener to hold focus (`await expect(marker).toBeFocused()`) before it moves on; no retry, timeout or sleep was added. Evidence: the unmodified test in Firefox failed 3 of 75 local runs (2 of 15, 0 of 30, 1 of 30); with the wait it passed 150 of 150 in Firefox and 25 of 25 each in Chromium and WebKit.

## F637: The final check names the placeholder rule, 2026-10-11 (CP-4 refusals, run 11f75bfa)

- F637 (2026-10-11; F505, D34, invariant 4; owner: "apply") — Run `11f75bfa-0c87-4490-be3b-178721fd0a99` (FULL_CREDIT_32 / RELATIVE_VALUE + CP-CF, openai/gpt-6-luna) refused CP-4 four times. The offline diagnosis is `docs/rebuild/runs/cp4-refusals-2026-10-11/REPORT.md` (git-ignored): the vendor's own `completeness_check` refused a critical register cell whose whole value is on the SKILL.md blocklist `critical_cell_values_casefold` (a bare `[Insufficient Information]`, or `Not Calculable from Provided Materials`) in 3 of the 4 answers, 4 cells in all, while the model wrote the canon's reason form, `[Insufficient Information] — <reason>`, in the neighbouring cells of the same rows; the reason form passes. The host's final check said nothing about it. The original 2026-10-11 `final_check.md` said, directly after the committee-status sentence, that no cell of a register's critical column may be, in any letter case, exactly one of the values the authority lists as `critical_cell_values_casefold`, and that where the evidence is missing the authority's reason form is written, never the bare placeholder. The original sentence named the vendor's blocklist field but omitted its exemption field (corrected in the 2026-10-12 addendum below); nothing was forked; `vendor/deploy-v` is untouched. `cp0_final_check.md` is unchanged: CP-0 was not at fault. Pins: `icm/HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` and `caos/methodology/host_pin.py` regenerated by `scripts/host_manifest.py` (`final_check.md` 2,720 → 3,022 bytes, `HOST_MANIFEST_SHA256` `89fdcf95...` → `be564d9f...`); no test pinned the old value. Goldens: the `prompt` group only, all 27 cases, +302 bytes each (the four lines and their line ends; a diff of one case's `text` with the sentence cut out equals the old text), regenerated by D34's mirror procedure -- a clone of the legacy snapshot `b5a3932f` with `vendor/deploy-v` taken from this tree, the host's prompt blocks read from `icm/shared/prompt/`, and D107's qualified markers and linked-citation binder, D113's evidence-first order, D38 and D93's withheld notes, the source-preparation text, the register header and the final check's arguments mirrored, and the host's test fixtures copied with the package renamed -- which, with the pre-edit blocks, reproduced all 27 committed `prompt` goldens and all 16 `forecast` goldens (and both manifests) byte for byte before the sentence was added. No other group moves. D81's header repeat is not mirrored: no fixture run reaches nine lines (F477), so it moves no golden. CP-4's own SKILL.md, in CP-4D binding rules 3 and 5 (lines 351-353), prescribes the bare `[Insufficient Information]`, which `completeness_check` refuses in a critical column; the host sentence follows the checker, which is the authority, and the vendor wording is N212. Whether the sentence changes the model's behaviour takes a live run.

## CP-1 fork amendment r14, 2026-10-10 (Book/Model Plan 1 Task 1; landed as stacked PR 01)

- D141 (2026-10-10, owner-authorised on 10 October 2026 in the Book/Model design session, recorded as "CP-1 fork amendment (owner-authorised 2026-10-10)" in the design spec §3 and Plan 1 Task 1 under `docs/superpowers/`, which stacked PR 02 adds; authority to edit the vendor bundle for this amendment only, under D31's and D34's fork rules; branch commits ecab96ec and 96009432) — Vendor fork r14: CP-1 carries what a host credit model needs. **What changed** (REF_CP-1_13 in `skills/cp-1-canonical-data-foundation/references/REF_CP-1_STEPS.md`, the skill's T4.18 columns and a conditional stable table in its `SKILL.md`, `CP-1_SCHEMA_REFERENCE.md`): (1) three controlled account ids, `operating_lease_cost`, `lease_payments_in_financing` and `interest_paid_in_financing`, outflows negative as for the existing ids; (2) six debt register columns after `lease_classification` and before `source_id`: `rate_type` (`FIXED`, `FLOATING`, `NOT_STATED`), `reference_rate` (`SOFR`, `TERM_SOFR`, `EURIBOR`, `SONIA`, `NONE`, `NOT_STATED`), `spread_bps`, `floor_bps`, `fixed_rate` and `annual_amortisation`, each sourced like every other column and never inferred from the instrument name; (3) `total_debt` excludes lease liabilities (rows classified `FINANCE_LEASE` or `OPERATING_LEASE`), checked by a `DEBT_TOTAL_EX_LEASES` reconciliation row; (4) an optional tagged `cp1.pro_forma_adjustment_register` (`adjustment_id | label | period_id | effective_date | classification | metric_id | value | source_id | source_locator`), written only when a source states an adjustment for activity outside the reported LTM; (5) income taxes classified outside operating activities are a limitation in Gaps and Conflicts, with no metric id. **The r14 rulings** (fix round 1, 96009432): `annual_amortisation` is `0` for a bullet or no scheduled amortisation and `null` only when not stated, which is a gap; `reference_rate` `NOT_STATED` is a floating instrument whose reference is unsourced, `NONE` is fixed-rate or no reference; a null `fixed_rate` is a gap unless the row is `FLOATING`. CP-MODEL's `validate_cp_model_inputs.py` `METRIC_IDS` gains the three ids; `STABLE_TABLE_REQUIRED_COLUMNS` and the mandatory account sets are unchanged, so a run accepted before r14 stays valid input. **Re-pin** (D31's procedure): `python3 -B vendor/deploy-v/verify_package.py --refresh` (115 vendor tests, 2 skips, 10 self-checks, PASS; no authority component moved, the authority bundle stays `97106f19`), build `d73f4b91` (was `d8a307a9`; ecab96ec alone built `b1522a11`, manifest `675533e3`), manifest `2f7e9635` (was `812ba297`); `uv run python scripts/host_manifest.py` (the manifest pin in `bundle_pin.py`; no host prompt text moved, `HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` unchanged); the build pins in `test_bundle_pin.py`, `test_canonical_proof.py`, `test_loop_charges.py`, `test_methodology_bundle.py` (build and manifest) and `test_qualification_matrix.py`; `test_delivered_authority.py`'s `MEASURED` sizes do not move (r14 amends CP-1's method only). Parity, by D34's procedure (a clone of the legacy snapshot `b5a3932f` with the host's prompt text, fixtures, D81's run headers, D107's qualified markers and D113's evidence-first order mirrored and `vendor/deploy-v` synced from the host tree, verified first to reproduce `rebuild/databricks`'s 27 `prompt` and 16 `forecast` goldens byte for byte, then synced from this fork): 10 `prompt` cases move, `model_owner__CP-1` and `relative_value__CP-1` by CP-1's amended authority, and `model_owner__CP-2G`, `model_owner__CP-4`, `relative_value__CP-1C`, `-CP-2`, `-CP-2A`, `-CP-2G`, `-CP-3` and `-CP-4` by the CP-1 fixture's six new cells through the upstream digest and each prompt's content-derived tags; the `forecast` group is unchanged. For this landing the whole re-pin was re-run on `rebuild/databricks` at `d07fea55` (2026-10-11): the refresh reproduced the branch's vendor tree, `bundle_pin.py` and the 10 goldens byte for byte. **Host:** the route fixtures carry the six terms (`tests/canonical_route_fixtures.py`'s notes row `FIXED | NONE | null | null | 5.000 | null`, `tests/cp1b_route_fixtures.py`'s bullet notes at `6.500` with `0` amortisation); the demo CP-1 (`frontend/fixtures/analysis.json`) states debt excluding leases ($5,478M, 1.1x net) and lists the finance-lease row's value-bearing nulls in Gaps and Conflicts; `frontend/src/sections/analysis/parts.ts` titles the new stable table "Pro forma adjustments" (a bridge). A record accepted under `d8a307a9` refuses `ORCHESTRATION_BUILD_MOVED` as on every re-pin.
