## Book/Model tooling, glossary and plans, 2026-10-10 (landed as stacked PR 02)

- D142 (2026-10-10; the project rule that no dependency lands without a decision record, though the global rules pre-approve mutmut as a test tool; branch commits 357a809e, c70a943e, 3a40bd34 and the `[tool.mutmut]` hunks of 6ada7348, 8b80caa9, 8e8e57db, d9da40f0, 7925c5d6, 546dc7a5 and f40d2526) — `mutmut==3.8.0` (BSD-3-Clause) joins the `dev` dependency group for mutation tests of the credit model's money arithmetic and the rate deck store, run by hand, never by a gate: `[tool.mutmut]` in `pyproject.toml` mutates only `caos/calculators/credit_model/*` and `caos/store/rate_decks.py`, copies `vendor` and `icm` beside the package so the suite's conftest loads, and selects the credit model and rate deck test files, which later PRs of the series add; its working copy `mutants/` is gitignored. The lock gains mutmut and what it pulls in (`libcst`, `textual`, `linkify-it-py`, `mdit-py-plugins`, `pyyaml-ft`, `setproctitle`). **Glossary** (`CONTEXT.md`, the Book/Model design's grilling session): **issuer** (not company, obligor), **readiness check** (not preflight, dry run), **rate deck** (not rate sheet) and **book entry** (not listing). **Vocabulary gates:** `scripts/check_vocabulary.py` and `frontend/scripts/check-vocabulary.mjs` enforce `obligor`, `dry_run` and `rate_sheet` (no identifier under `caos`, `scripts`, `icm`, `tests`, `frontend/src` or `frontend/tests` used them); the Python gate exempts `company` (`company_description` is the bundle's own field name), `preflight` (the enterprise deploy preflight checks a workspace, not sources) and `listing` (`CaseListing` and `RunListing` are store rows a directory lists), each with its reason. **Plans:** the Book/Model design spec and its worked layout example (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-10-book-model-redesign-design.md`, `…-model-layout-example.html`) and Plans 1-4 (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-10-credit-model-foundation.md`, `-model-section.md`, `-book-entries.md`, `-book-ui.md`), as the branch last wrote them; migrations 0050 and 0051 are theirs, 0049 stays with the readiness-check branch. Evidence: `uv lock --check`, `uv run python scripts/check_vocabulary.py` and `npm --prefix frontend run lint` (the TypeScript vocabulary gate).

## Rate decks, 2026-10-10 (Book/Model Plan 2 Task 1; landed as stacked PRs 26-29)

- D143 (2026-10-10, Plan 2 Task 1 in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-10-model-section.md`, which stacked PR 02 adds; branch commit 2a8e8e63 and its four review fix rounds f40d2526, 7929a5a4, 5f592662 and 94ce6a86; the store and migration land in PR 26, the read half and the API in PR 27, the run pin in PR 28, the remaining tests and races in PR 29) — **A rate deck** is the desk's dated statement of reference rates (`CONTEXT.md`): for `SOFR`, `TERM_SOFR`, `EURIBOR` and `SONIA`, a Base and a Downside rate per fiscal year FY+1 to FY+5, each a decimal strictly inside (-1, 1) (0.0380 is 3.80%) of at most 64 characters in plain notation (what the wire's decimal text carries back), 1 to 20 rows, no (reference rate, year) twice (`check_rates`, `REQUEST_INVALID` before any write). The store reads a deck back as Plan 1's `projection.RateDeck`, Decimal, never float. **Saved by a global ADMIN, immutable.** `POST /api/v1/rate-decks` (`SaveRateDeck` to `RateDeckSaved{deck_id}`) is the second global write beside a qualification verdict, shaped as it is: compared by rank (`at_least`), and any other role is answered `ENDPOINT_NOT_FOUND` before the key, the body or the store is read; the saver is the actor, never a body field; no case audit chain (the deck belongs to no case), so the command receipt is written under `NIL_SCOPE` in the same transaction as the deck, idempotent by key (a different body under it is `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`; twin saves under one key keep one deck); the one log line, `rate_deck_saved deck_id=<id> rows=<n>`, names no rate. `GET /api/v1/rate-decks/latest` answers `RateDeckView` or `null` to any signed-in role in one statement. `V1_COMMANDS` goes from 30 to 32 (`SaveRateDeck`, `RateDeckSaved`); `RateDeckView` joins the schema; `tests/test_api_routes.py` pins both global-write modules to their exact routes and to the receipt helpers (`GLOBAL_WRITES`), so a route added to either later fails there. Migration `0050_rate_decks.sql` adds `rate_decks`, `rate_deck_rates` and `run_rate_decks`, each refusing UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE, and a deck gains no rate outside the transaction that saved it. Who wrote what is decided by write-once transaction ids (`rate_decks.saved_xact`, `runs.created_xact`: `xid8` from `pg_current_xact_id()`, top-level, so a savepoint changes nothing, and 64-bit, so no wraparound), never by a timestamp a later transaction could rewrite. `rates_sha256` (`rates_digest`: canonical JSON of the rows sorted by rate and year, each rate as its value, since Postgres keeps a numeric's scale but not a zero's sign) is written at the save and checked on every read, so rates changed behind the triggers refuse `ARTIFACT_RECORD_MISMATCH` rather than read as a different deck. **Latest by identity.** "Latest" is the greatest `deck_seq`, an identity drawn while the save lock (`SAVE_LOCK`, the transaction-scoped advisory lock class 0x0CA00050 = 211812432, second key 0, which the migration names by number and a test ties to the store's constant) is held exclusively, so its order is commit order. The store refuses a deck inserted without that lock, one with an identity its save did not draw (`OVERRIDING SYSTEM VALUE`), one at or below the latest (a sequence moved back) and one claiming another transaction saved it; `saved_at` is the clock read under the lock, finite, for display only. **Pinned per run at creation.** `start_run` (through `started_run`, which names the pinned deck, `PinnedDeck`, for the `RUN_CREATED` payload's `rate_deck: {deck_id, rates_sha256}`) pins the latest committed deck in the unit that creates the run, and the store refuses a pin from any other transaction: the run and its pin commit together or not at all, and a later save never moves a pin. With no deck, nothing is pinned. The pin takes the save lock shared first, so it waits for a save in flight and pins that deck: a deck acknowledged to its admin before the run commits is never passed over. The wait is bounded by a transaction-local `lock_timeout` (`PIN_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS`, 5000, or the caller's tighter bound, read from `pg_catalog.pg_settings` and put back after the wait); past it the run start is refused `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`, retryable, so a save queued behind a long run unit cannot hold every other run start. `CREATE_RUN_IO` goes from 15 to 19 (the pin's bound, wait, unbound and insert). The one documented fail-open: a store below 0050 (the populated-upgrade suites start runs on a migration prefix) starts an ordinary run, pins nothing and says so once per run on stderr (`rate_deck_pin_skipped run=<id> reason=no_table`), answered by the run insert's own `RETURNING`, at no extra round trip; a store the app serves is always at the full schema, since `apply_schema` refuses drift at boot. **The runs table guard.** A pin is checked against its run's creating transaction, so a run must never be created again under the same id: `runs.created_xact` is written once (existing runs take the migrating transaction's id, so no run created before decks can ever be pinned), a run's id never changes (an UPDATE or an upsert's UPDATE moving it is refused; writing the same id is no change), and `runs` refuses DELETE by a row trigger and TRUNCATE by a statement trigger, which a row trigger does not see. `tests/test_frozen_evidence.py`, `test_route_integrity.py` and `test_run_events.py` adapt to that: a `TRUNCATE ... CASCADE` reaching `runs` is refused by `runs` itself, and the one test that stands in for a run row gone under its lock disables `run_not_deleted` inside its rolled-back unit. **Every guard** runs with `search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp` and names the store's tables through `TG_TABLE_SCHEMA` (the migration still names no schema, DL-1), so a session's temporary `runs`, `rate_decks`, `pg_locks` or `pg_settings` shadow is ignored, tested as a role holding only SELECT and INSERT. **Numbering:** 0049 stays free for the readiness-check branch (D142); whichever lands second takes the next free number in `MIGRATIONS` order, since `scripts/rollback_check.py` compares the tuple in order. Evidence: `tests/test_rate_decks.py`; the rate-deck races in `tests/test_postgres_races.py` (two saves stamped in commit order, twin saves under one key, a run started while a save is in flight pins that deck, a run start queued behind a waiting save gives up typed, the pin's bound only tightens the caller's); `tests/test_run_commands.py` (the `RUN_CREATED` payload binds the pinned deck), `tests/test_model_choice_api.py`, `tests/test_wire_contract.py` and `tests/test_api_routes.py`.
- D143 addendum (2026-10-12; rate-deck save bound, slice 59) — The pin-only name `PIN_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS` above is now the common `LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS` (5000 ms), shared by saves and pins. The transaction-local bound is the smaller of that value and the caller's nonzero `lock_timeout`, read from `pg_catalog.pg_settings`. A save keeps it through the exclusive advisory-lock wait and both INSERTs, then restores the caller's setting on success. A lock timeout refuses `STORE_UNAVAILABLE` and leaves the failed unit for the caller to roll back; no restoration query is issued in an aborted unit. The exclusive lock still lasts until the transaction ends. Pinning keeps its four statements and fresh snapshot after the shared-lock wait; saving now takes five statements, making `SAVE_IO` seven and a losing twin replay eight.
- D144 (2026-10-11, Plan 2 Task 2 in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-10-model-section.md`; Book/Model commits 7588f409, bf1c661e and 4f7cda19; stacked PRs 31 and 32; the review level is `mx-adversary`, because the read serves money figures) — **The Model read serves the host calculator's credit model, 1:1.** `GET /api/v1/cases/{case_id}/model` now answers `ModelBody` v2: the run fields Analysis answers with (`case_id`, `latest_run_id`, `displayed_run_id`, `displayed_run_status`, `subject`), `currency`, `unit`, `exclude_unrealised`, `rate_deck` (the deck's id and as-of date), `columns` (at most `MODEL_COLUMNS_MAX` = 64), `lines` (at most `MODEL_LINES_MAX` = 400, in their bands, each with exactly one cell per column: a `model_validator` refuses a body otherwise), `limitations` (at most `MODEL_LIMITATIONS_MAX` = 2048 notes of at most `MODEL_LIMITATION_CHARS`, the bound a note listing every column needs) and `forecast`, the accepted CP-CF record kept beside the model for comparison. `blocked_by` and `unavailable_reason` leave the body. Every figure is `caos.calculators.credit_model`'s, an exact decimal string in plain notation (invariant 7), or the typed reason it has none (`COMPONENT_MISSING` among them) with the calculator's own note, its basis and the stated value; the read derives, maps and bounds, and does no arithmetic. The figures shown are the calculator as it stands; the open items N199 to N203 apply to them. A CP-1 cell's `[C<n>]` marker resolves to CP-1's own citation (D107); a cell lists at most `MODEL_SOURCES_MAX` = 8 sources, and a longer list is a stated limitation. **Limitations are stated, never dropped.** `limitations` carries the calculator's own, then the read's: the PIK note where a facility's coupon names PIK, the cut sources, a reconciliation bridge the calculator's own check finds open (`reconciliation_faults`; it does not refuse an assist model, it is shown, and logged as `model_reconciliation_fault case= run= count=`), and each case below. **Unreadable model tables are stated, not refused.** CP-1 model tables that do not parse (an older run's, written before the CP-1 fork, or withheld past a bound) serve a partial document with the accepted CP-CF forecast, no columns or lines, and the limitation `CP-1 model tables unreadable`; unreadable or withheld CP-2G drivers build the model without growth, the business units held flat, with `CP-2G growth drivers unavailable: business units held flat`. Both log `model_tables_unreadable case= run= module= code=` and no text; any other refusal still refuses. **Oversized figures are withheld.** A cell whose plain decimal (the value or the stated one) runs past the wire's 64-character `DecimalText` is served `NOT_APPLICABLE` with the note `figure too large to serve`, its sources kept, with the limitation `figures too large to serve were withheld` and the log line `model_figure_unservable case= run= count=` (no figure); never a 500. **Bounded grid mapping.** The `CreditModel`'s column and line counts are checked before it is mapped; past 64 or 400 the document is partial, the grid empty, the forecast kept, with `model exceeds the grid bounds` and `model_grid_oversize case= run= columns= lines=`. `observed_empty` is false whenever the forecast is served. **The deck is pinned per run.** A projection reads the rate deck the run pinned at its creation (D143), never a newer one, in one statement (`DECK_IO` = 1, paid where CP-1 is accepted); `IO_BUDGET` = Analysis' + `PIN_IO` + `DECK_IO` (252, recorded in `tests/io_measurements.json`), and the Book's per-row budget subtracts `DECK_IO`, since it reads no deck. **Authority.** No accepted CP-1 is an empty body that says the handoffs are pending (spec M1). A source withdrawn from any accepted handoff's citations refuses the whole read `ARTIFACT_RECORD_MISMATCH` (spec B8), whether the forecast or the model asks. `exclude_unrealised` is `1`/`true` or `0`/`false`/absent, parsed before the store opens (anything else `REQUEST_INVALID`). Frontend: the shapes are mirrored in `documents.ts`, `schema.json` is regenerated, and `ModelSection` shows the served lines as a plain table until the grid (PR 34). Evidence: `tests/test_model_section.py` (PR 31 pins the empty and unreadable-tables bodies, the query parser and the budget; PR 32 adds the served model, the pinned deck, withheld figures, the grid bound and the limitations), `tests/test_wire_contract.py`, `tests/test_api_routes.py`, `frontend/tests/unit/model.test.tsx`, `frontend/tests/unit/wire-contract.test.ts`.

## LineChart end label, 2026-10-11 (Book/Model slice 35)

- F626 (2026-10-11; the Model chart strip, `frontend/src/charts/LineChart.tsx`; fixed in the Book/Model branch's commit 50776041 ("chart-strip points in view") and tested in 1a4c9895) — A series' end label (the last value written beside the plot) was drawn whenever the labels did not collide, whatever their width, and the plot gave the labels the width they asked for. A served value can run to dozens of digits (the test uses one of 37 decimals), so one long label took most of the chart's width and squeezed the plot until its points left the view. An end label wider than `END_ROOM` (64 px, with its 10 px gap) is now left out; the legend and the readout, which name the value in full, carry it, and the plot keeps its width. A label that fits is drawn as before and the collision rule is unchanged. Cost if wrong: a value whose rounded text is wider than 64 px has no label beside the line, only in the legend and readout. Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/charts.test.tsx::draws an end label that fits and leaves a long served value to the legend and readout` (a short end value is labelled; a 37-digit one is not, all four points have a non-negative x, and the readout still names it).

- F633 (2026-10-12; owner-approved CI speedup) — The monolithic test and frontend jobs serialized independent work. Four deterministic test-file shards now produce four distinct coverage datasets; the aggregate test job combines all four before enforcing the unchanged 80% floor, coverage scanner and race tests. Frontend static/unit/build, accessibility and browser jobs run independently with required aggregate frontend status preserved. Whole-job fingerprints, shard inventory, artifact cardinality, failure propagation and immutable thresholds are pinned by tests. The fast-gates helper includes the repository guard tests and requires Postgres. Focused gate tests and positive/negative coverage-union probes pass; hosted integration remains a required CI check.
- F634 (2026-10-12; accessibility WebKit navigation failure) — The full scan sometimes stops on page.goto's exact WebKit internal-error message, with no accessibility or layout violation. Navigation retries that engine failure once with the same page, URL and options. All other exceptions propagate immediately, the second failure propagates unchanged, and readiness/axe/layout/full-matrix gates are unchanged. Fourteen focused Node cases pass, including timeout, repeated failure, unrelated messages, argument/receiver/response identity and HTTP responses. Distinct controller tournament roles selected the verified predicate; no retry was added to a scan verdict.
- F629 (2026-10-12; SCIM identity worker startup) — scim_me reserved a bounded exchange slot before starting its helper thread. A RuntimeError during thread construction or startup escaped untyped and left the slot reserved forever. Startup now returns the slot and raises IDENTITY_UNAVAILABLE without exposing the error text. The running worker still returns its own slot in its existing finally block. Regression tests force construction and start failures against a one-slot semaphore and verify the typed refusal, suppressed error context, and exactly one returned slot; identity and health caller tests pass.

- F630 (2026-10-12; frontend transitive dependency advisory) — The lock contained source-map-js 1.2.1, affected by GHSA-68fv-2mgg-jv7q. The owner-approved npm audit fix --package-lock-only --ignore-scripts updates it to 1.2.2 and refreshes compatible transitive brace-expansion versions and bundled optional dependency metadata. package.json and all direct pins are unchanged. npm audit reports zero vulnerabilities; locked npm ci, frontend lint/typecheck, all 826 unit tests, production build and demo build pass. No dependency or npm-audit CI gate was added.

- F631 (2026-10-12; guided register repair, adopted from reviewed peer cb5460e) — A repair could fix its named fault while dropping other required registers from the carried answer. The host now names the required registers that answer contains, using the pinned vendor locator in contract order. This bounded keep line rides only beside faults and the carried answer, outside the fault-count cap; request-size and budget fallbacks drop it with the answer. Real-Postgres retry/register tests pass. The controller's distinct-role rewrite tournament selected the verified implementation without an unnecessary copy of the trusted register mapping.
- F632 (2026-10-12; duplicate citation repair, adopted from reviewed peer 9ddc6cb) — Repeated citations were refused correctly but reported as a malformed JSON transport, and the refused answer was lost. Strict acceptance still refuses repeats; repair diagnostics parse the otherwise valid transport, name duplicate places accurately and carry the answer. Feedback bounds, typed errors and exception-group behavior are preserved. Real-Postgres retry/register tests, caller types and ICM gates pass; the controller personally verified the selected rewrite and mixed exception-group behavior.
- D145 (2026-10-12; Plan 3 Task 2, Book entries store/add/withdraw slices 37-40; frozen source cf57f3de with entries fix rounds f823060f and b86dbad7 only) — A Book entry belongs to one completed run, for ever; withdrawal never deletes it and its four withdrawal columns are stamped once. The store owns the clock and identity; issuer_key is upper(trim(issuer_id)). Metrics are decimal strings or null on each basis. The reviewed snapshot digest binds sorted accepted artifact lines and every stored figure: pinned deck, currency, unit, metrics and limitations. The Model's default view serves that digest; alternate views and absent grids serve none. Later Book read grants and interface work remain outside this stack.

- F635 (2026-10-12; frozen entries scope, confidence review against cf57f3de) — `AddToBook` used the eight-character rating bound for agency outlooks, rejecting valid `DEVELOPING` and `WATCH_POS`/`WATCH_NEG`/`WATCH_DEV` before the command's vocabulary check. The outlook has its own bound, the maximum length in `classification.OUTLOOKS` (10), mirrored in TypeScript. Ratings keep their eight-character bound. Evidence: the previous request raises `string_too_long` on `moodys_outlook="DEVELOPING"`; `test_every_agency_outlook_is_accepted_and_no_other` checks every agency and outlook, refuses unknown words, and stores the longest valid outlooks. No later Book read/grant/interface types are included.
- F636 (2026-10-12; Book entries SQL guard, slice 37) — An OTHER withdrawal with a NULL note passed the table's CHECK because its regular expression returned SQL UNKNOWN, which a CHECK accepts. The constraint now explicitly requires a non-NULL note containing non-whitespace for OTHER. Direct SQL regression updates reject NULL, empty and whitespace-only notes and leave the entry live; a meaningful OTHER note and ADDED_IN_ERROR without a note still withdraw once. The unshipped 0051 migration is corrected in place; immutable fields and withdrawal stamps are unchanged. Evidence: `tests/test_book_entries.py::test_the_store_inserts_an_entry_live_and_withdraws_it_once`, observed failing before the constraint fix on real PostgreSQL.
- F627 (2026-10-12; Book/Model slices 34a–35) — The sliced Model grid shipped its band buttons before slice 35's minimum target sizing, so mobile accessibility checks found an 18 px control. The grid's band and group buttons now have a 24 px minimum height in slice 34a. A mistakenly tracked root Vitest cache also failed the end-of-file hook; it is removed and root node_modules is ignored. No gate or suppression budget changed. Verification: 804 frontend unit tests, frontend lint/typecheck, all pre-commit hooks, and the Model route at 320×640 in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, light and dark, with zero violations, scan errors or layout failures.

- F628 (2026-10-12; Book/Model read slices 31–32) — When CP-1 tables were unreadable or the grid exceeded its wire bounds, the read served an empty grid, a limitation and any accepted forecast. CreditModelTable returned early on an empty grid, hiding that limitation. The limitations panel now remains visible when the grid is empty; the table and currency tag remain conditional on lines. Four regression cases cover both refusal reasons with and without a forecast. Each fails on the original slice and passes with the fix; the focused Model suite, typecheck and frontend lint pass.

- D146 (2026-10-12; Book read cutover) — The Book serves desk-wide published issuers: any signed-in viewer sees the newest live entry per issuer, ordered within that issuer by analysis date, added time and entry sequence, with the other entries as history. An issuer whose entries are all withdrawn is absent. Figures, snapshot digest and limitations remain the stored values; projected fiscal years are separate from metric leaves, and the metric catalogue uses the Model's declared lines. The source warning covers every pinned source, including uncited sources, whose live identity no longer matches. Reads bound issuers to 1,000 and history to 100 per issuer, name truncation, and use two store queries without blob reads. Served action roles are advisory; commands recheck authority. This cutover records no live-provider or production-scale proof.

- D147 (2026-10-12; desk-wide Book read grant) — A signed-in non-member may read Analysis and Model for the explicitly named run of a live Book entry and pages of that exact run’s pinned, live sources. This adopts the reviewed pinned-source bound: no citation-only index exists. Every other section, event stream and command remains member-only. Granted documents serve the entry’s issuer name, no standing or actions, no other-run latest ID and no Model snapshot digest; disappearance of the live entry before the case-row read refuses privately. Withdrawal revokes the next read. Granted reads log only case, run and user IDs, without audit-chain writes. The Model member path retains its existing document behavior; Add to Book offers follow in their own slice.

- D148 (2026-10-12; Add to Book and Book controls adoption) — The Model header offers Add to Book only with the served action, snapshot digest, subject and displayed run. The dialog starts from the issuer’s currently displayed Book entry when one can be read; a failed Book read is reported and starts a blank form. Sector, domicile, agency scales and rating-bound outlooks use the governed vocabularies. Confirmation names the actual Model snapshot digest, and the governed command client posts that digest with the stated classification. Success stays visible in the dialog. Closing after a successful add rereads the same case, run and unrealised-add-back view once through the shared request-order hook; if the add completes after the dialog has closed, the Model is reread when it completes. A failed reread is reported; after reopening, closing the dialog may request another read without repeating the completed write. The Book shows each issuer’s newest live entry and its stored history, including withdrawn entries; an issuer with no live entry is absent. Served column, basis, sort and rating/set/range filters update the current view while preserving unrelated URL context. Identity columns stay selected, set choices use served values, decimal bounds remain exact, and a money range waits for an explicit native currency. Each permitted withdrawal confirms that entry’s own stored digest, requires a note for Other, and rereads the Book after success. This records source adoption, not live-provider or production proof.

- F639 (2026-10-12; original bounded rate-deck save, slice 59; reviewed source 586041aa and 5a0f196c) — A save waiting for the exclusive save lock, or blocked at either INSERT while holding that lock, could queue later run pins indefinitely. The shared `_lock_bound` now limits all three waits to `LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS` (5000 ms) or the caller's tighter nonzero bound. Only `LockNotAvailable` becomes the typed `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`; the caller rolls back the failed unit, including any deck written before a rates INSERT times out, so no orphan deck or receipt commits. Successful saves restore the caller's timeout, and failure performs no restoration query that could mask the refusal. Validation and parameter preparation precede the bound; transaction lock lifetime, pin ordering and fresh snapshot, immutable rates and exactly-once receipts are preserved. Evidence: `tests/test_rate_decks.py` (restoration and actual statement counts) and the rate-deck races in `tests/test_postgres_races.py` (advisory and both table-lock refusals, rollback without partial writes, and queued pins released when a save gives up).

- F638 (2026-10-12; reviewed calculator follow-up, slice 61; exact integration f869237c, owner tip a07125ad and its six recorded predecessors) — A fixed coupon strictly between zero and half a percent could be priced one hundred times over. The fixed row's `margin_or_coupon` text now decides whether its figure is a genuine percent or a decimal fraction: only the matching written percent or times-100 percent decides; both or neither leave the reading undecided and pricing uses the noted implied-rate fallback. Fraction conversion notes follow drawn facilities into weighted rates and interest, including balances below one unit, while existing exclusion, zero and no-mix notes survive. A latest fiscal year that is short, long, undated or cannot be spanned does not build a host LTM; an independently stated LTM remains stated. The latest labelled FY is always the projection base, including one without an end date: an incomplete or nonannual base withholds nonstructural projected figures across both cases and all chained years with a typed `COMPONENT_MISSING` explanation. Structural NOT_PROJECTED/NOT_APPLICABLE, historical figures and malformed-driver refusals remain. Consecutive FY growth selects the actual preceding label and refuses unequal or unknown fiscal durations with an explanation; equal nonannual spans may compare. Existing whole-month, calendar, leap and 52/53-week semantics, corporate and other-footing precedence, and quarter/YTD/LTM rules remain. Source is the frozen reviewed implementation; slice 61 carries the parser, coupon, fiscal-duration, LTM and projection-base regressions, with remaining exact reviewed tests in slices 62–63. This records adoption, not live provider or deployment proof.
- F640 (2026-10-12; supported Book/Model integration repair, slice 61; exact source 25080aab) — Book projection offsets used the last historically displayed FY, so an undated or short latest labelled FY displayed before an older dated FY could shift the six stored fiscal-year labels away from the calculator's projection base. `_base_year` now selects the greatest parsed historical FY label, independent of display order, retaining the existing no-history projection fallback. Eight real parser-to-calculator-to-served-Model-to-entry-snapshot cases cover both period orders, plain and split fiscal-year labels, and undated or short latest years: Base and Downside FY1–FY3 retain 2026–2028, withheld numbers and their explanation remain, and the snapshot digest agrees. Current Book authority, withdrawal-note guards, historical display ordering and all other Model mapping remain unchanged.

- D149 (2026-10-13; explicit forecast cash movements and owned debt service, source 9651343a) — Model and Book may project from an explicitly selected `OWNED_CASH_DEBT_SERVICE` basis that binds CP-1 actuals/calendar/opening, CP-2G annual operating and interest drivers plus selector, and CP-4 future aggregate principal. Historical net-debt movement enters once in the first projected year; future principal remains separate from historical gross repayment. The extension also supports explicit segment revenue levels and optional equity, other investing/financing, restricted-cash and carrying movements; omitting these inputs preserves legacy outputs. Negative cash is an unfunded position, with no automatic refinancing or revolver draw. Instrument, rank, secured debt, weighted rate, RCF and liquidity remain `NOT_PROJECTED`. Source passports preserve quote, marker, owner, pointer and locator, and ambiguous attribution refuses. Model displays aggregate cash/debt, unallocated debt and sixteen independent numeric checks within the existing 40-value period and eight-visible-source bounds. Evidence: `tests/test_cash_flow_forecast.py`, `tests/test_forecast_debt_service.py`, `tests/test_credit_model_owned_debt_service.py`, `tests/test_model_section.py`, `tests/test_canonical_execution.py` and `tests/test_book_section.py`; all 52 required source gates and two freshness checks passed on the exact committed tree. This adopts source behavior only: the v3 analyst assumptions were human-approved for a new native case on 2026-10-13, while the forecast remains without full governed acceptance, the prior live case remains retired, and no completed new paid ten-stage Model/Book proof or real deployment is claimed.
